With every monitor unplugged, Shingle and its clients keep running with their windows in place and nothing is drawn; a returning monitor is lit again. Hotplugs wait for a pending page flip.
- The output is described with the EDID's make, model and serial and every mode of the connector. A new connector renames the core output, so the tiling tree and workspaces stay and sessions are saved per connector, and replaces the `wl_output` global, finishing the old output's xdg-outputs, power and gamma controls, output-management heads and workspace groups; the configured position, scale and transform of that connector apply.
- wlr-output-management lists every mode; native mode changes pass a `TEST_ONLY` modeset before they are set, after any pending flip, and tests change nothing. A config reload applies changed output modes.
- Nested and headless: an explicit configured mode sizes the headless output and the nested window and sets the refresh rate clients see; a named `HEADLESS-1` entry replaces the default; custom modes from wlr-output-management resize the output; scale and transform changes keep the physical size.
- A session restored with other output names waits for those outputs: under the default `output_layout.unplug: return`, its windows join the one output and go back when an output of that name or EDID identity appears, which a native rename counts as (MO-2).

Only hardware can show: real udev hotplug events (their timing, `HOTPLUG=1` on the card, events during a VT switch), connectors' connection state and mode lists after unplugging and replugging (including monitors that keep the connector connected in standby), real EDIDs and hwdata names, drivers accepting modesets to other modes and other connectors, whether a pending flip on an unplugged connector completes or times out, monitors following `wlr-randr` mode changes, and clients (bars, `wlr-randr`, Xwayland) handling the replaced `wl_output` global.

### Native backend: several monitors

MO-7 in [design/multi-output.md](design/multi-output.md#mo-7-as-built), in `shingle-native`, `shingle` and `shingle-bin`. Built, clippy-checked and unit-tested through the fake card (now with CRTCs, primary planes and pending flips per pipe), the fake seat and the fake hotplug monitor only; it has never opened a DRM device. Its hardware checks are steps 25 to 35 of [native-testing.md](native-testing.md).

- Every connected connector that is not `off` is lit, in priority order (lit ones, those named in `outputs`, internal panels, card order), as far as CRTCs and primary planes allow: connectors are matched to CRTCs as wlroots' `match_connectors_with_crtcs` matches them, lit outputs keeping their CRTCs (crates/shingle-native/src/crtcs.rs, `Card::allocate`). Each new monitor takes the first of its modes that passes a `TEST_ONLY` modeset together with the monitors lit before it; then one blocking modeset sets them all and turns off only what none of them uses.
- Each monitor flips on its own CRTC and is paced by its own flips; flip events are routed by CRTC to `frame_presented` of their own output, so frame callbacks and presentation feedback come from the monitor a window is on, at its refresh rate. A lost flip is recovered with a modeset of that monitor alone.
- Hotplug, resume and reloaded modes keep lit monitors on their CRTCs, drop unplugged ones (turning their CRTCs off) and light new ones. The compositor's outputs follow: the first monitor renames the default output, the others are added and removed under `output_layout.unplug`, so under `return` a window goes back to its monitor when it is replugged, also on another connector by EDID identity. Connected monitors that are not lit are listed as disabled heads; the dark last output is lit again when its monitor returns. Full modesets wait for every pending flip, and no new frame starts meanwhile.
- wlr-output-management configurations are tested and carried out as a whole: mode changes of several heads and monitors turned off and on are one `TEST_ONLY` modeset and one real one, and a failed modeset restores the old configuration (`OutputRequest` now names the state of every changed head). What a configuration set holds across hotplugs and config reloads until Shingle restarts.
- wlr-output-power and idle DPMS turn each monitor's CRTC off and on by itself.
- Absolute devices map to `input_devices.NAME.output` (Wayfire's `[input-device:NAME] output`), else to the only monitor, else to the whole layout, and relative motion crosses between monitors.
- `--outputs N` lights at most N monitors.

Only hardware can show: real drivers accepting the combined modeset of two and three CRTCs and its teardown, the CRTC routing of real encoders and `possible_crtcs`, shared limits (bandwidth, clocks) failing the combined `TEST_ONLY` and the smaller mode chosen then, flip events and timestamps of several CRTCs at different refresh rates, the copy path keeping up with several monitors on one GPU, udev events when a second monitor, a dock or an MST hub comes and goes, EDID identities of real monitors moved between connectors, `wlr-randr` and `wlopm` against several real heads, a VT switch with several monitors lit, and real touchscreens and tablets mapped to an output.

### Native backend: zero-copy scanout

NB-8 in [design/native-backend.md](design/native-backend.md#nb-8-as-built) and [design/dmabuf.md](design/dmabuf.md#nb-8-as-built), in `shingle-native`. Built, clippy-checked and unit-tested through the fake card, its fake GBM and a fake renderer only; it has never allocated a GBM buffer, made a framebuffer of one or imported one into a renderer. Its hardware checks are steps 36 to 42 of [native-testing.md](native-testing.md).

- Every lit monitor starts on copy scanout. Before its next frame, once it is on, the seat is enabled and no flip of it is pending, the display tries zero-copy: the primary plane's `IN_FORMATS` for XRGB8888 ∩ the renderer's render modifiers that hold the mode (DB-1's `DmabufCaps`) ∩ single plane by GBM, three GBM buffers (`SCANOUT | RENDERING`) with one of them, a framebuffer of each with its modifier, and the renderer importing them (`DmabufUse::Render`) and clearing them to black; then a `TEST_ONLY` flip of the plane's `FB_ID` to one of them and the same flip blocking, after which the dumb buffers are freed.
- Frames of a zero-copy monitor are drawn into the buffer off screen between the foreign acquire and release barriers, the renderer waits at most 100 ms for the GPU, and the flip is presented with `vsync`, `hw_clock`, `hw_completion` and `zero_copy`.
- Whatever fails keeps copy scanout and logs `output NAME: copy scanout, because REASON` once per monitor and mode: `SHINGLE_SCANOUT=copy`, no GBM device, a renderer that cannot import DMA-BUFs or is not on the card's PCI slot, a plane without `IN_FORMATS`, no shared single-plane modifier (naming both lists), a GBM allocation, framebuffer or import that fails, or a refused test flip. A refusal for lack of DRM master is tried again later.
- A zero-copy monitor falls back to copy buffers, by the same tested blocking flip, when a renderer cannot import its buffers (as after a GPU loss onto another device) or the card refuses a flip to one of them. A GPU loss alone keeps zero-copy, since the GBM buffers belong to the card; the new renderer imports them again. A new mode (hotplug, output management, reloaded modes) is tried again with buffers of its size; a VT switch and DPMS keep the buffers.
- tools/native_test/present_check.py takes `--scanout zero-copy|copy` and counts frames presented as zero-copy.

Only hardware can show: which modifiers real planes list in `IN_FORMATS` and whether one of them is also a Vulkan render modifier there (on NVIDIA, DB-1 found only block-linear render modifiers, so it needs nvidia-drm to list one), GBM on the card allocating with that modifier and its GEM handle on a duplicated descriptor, drivers accepting framebuffers with the modifier and a flip from linear dumb buffers to a tiled GBM buffer without a modeset, the import of GBM's buffers into the renderer and a correct picture (no wrong colours, tiling garbage or tearing) with the foreign barriers, the GPU wait keeping frames within one refresh, `ZERO_COPY` in real presentation feedback, the CPU time saved against copy scanout, buffers kept across VT switches and DPMS, new buffers after mode changes and hotplug on several monitors, and recovery from a GPU reset.

### Native backend: planes, VRR, tearing and fences

NB-10 in [design/native-backend.md](design/native-backend.md#nb-10-as-built), in `shingle-native`, `shingle-frontend`, `shingle-render` and `shingle`. Built, clippy-checked and unit-tested through the fake card and a fake renderer only; it has never set a cursor plane, `VRR_ENABLED`, an async flip or a fence on a card. Its hardware checks are steps 44 to 51 of [native-testing.md](native-testing.md). Everything is per lit monitor, on copy and zero-copy scanout alike.

- Cursor plane: each CRTC's cursor plane (type cursor, ARGB8888; one that only that CRTC can use first, and a plane shared by CRTCs goes to the first output) shows the cursor from two ARGB8888 dumb buffers of the driver's cursor size, the image written into the buffer off screen when it changes. When only the cursor moved, a flip of the cursor plane alone moves it and presents nothing to clients; otherwise the change rides on the frame's flip. The first commit that shows the cursor is tested, and every modeset (start, DPMS on, resume, hotplug) shows it again; an output turned off or unplugged turns its cursor plane off.
- The frontend hands the cursor to the plane only where the plane shows the same pixels: not on a zoomed, rotated or flipped output, with inverted colours or a gamma ramp, for a cursor buffer with a viewport, a transform, another buffer scale than the output's or a DMA-BUF, or one larger than the plane. Then it draws the cursor into the frame, switching in one flip, and the log names the reason (`output CONN: the cursor is drawn, because ...`). A plane the card refuses leaves the cursor to the frame for good. Captures do not show a cursor on the plane, as in Wayfire with a hardware cursor. Cursor surfaces keep a CPU copy of their shm buffers for it.
- The drawn cursor now moves: the frontend asks for a frame of the outputs it left and entered whenever it moves or changes (before, native's default arrow stood still until something else redrew the output).
- VRR: `VRR_ENABLED` follows the output's `vrr` where the connector's `vrr_capable` is 1 and the CRTC has the property, set by every modeset; a reload or a replugged monitor that changes it makes a modeset. A VRR output's frames are presented with a refresh of 0, and its wlr-output-management heads (version 4) report adaptive sync enabled, which they used to report disabled always. The log says why a configured `vrr` stays off.
- Tearing: frames of a monitor where a visible surface asks for asynchronous presentation (`wp_tearing_control_v1`) flip with `PAGE_FLIP_ASYNC` where `DRM_CAP_ATOMIC_ASYNC_PAGE_FLIP` says the driver takes it and the flip changes only the primary plane; their feedback has no `vsync` flag. A refused async flip is made again in sync, and that monitor's flips stay in sync.
- Fences: `shingle_render::FenceExporter` exports a sync_file of a frame's submission from a Vulkan semaphore (`VK_KHR_external_semaphore_fd`, `SYNC_FD`), and zero-copy frames hand it to the primary plane as `IN_FENCE_FD` instead of waiting up to 100 ms for the GPU. Without an exporter, after the card refused a fence (waited for on the CPU and the flip made again) or when the export fails, the renderer waits as before.
- `SHINGLE_CURSOR_PLANE=0` or `WLR_NO_HARDWARE_CURSORS=1`, `SHINGLE_ASYNC_FLIPS=0` and `SHINGLE_IN_FENCE=0` force each fallback; `SHINGLE_TRACE_FLIPS=1` ends the line of an async flip with ` async` and logs `CONN: cursor plane flip` for flips of the cursor plane alone. `present_probe --tearing` asks for tearing, and tools/native_test/present_check.py takes `--tearing` and `--vrr`.

Only hardware can show: which CRTCs have cursor planes and of what size, drivers accepting the cursor plane's property set (signed positions off the edges, the full buffer size, ARGB8888 dumb buffers, the plane on and off in flips without a modeset) and cursor-only flips with an event while frames flip on other CRTCs, the pointer's look (premultiplied alpha, hotspot, no trail or flicker) and smoothness, `vrr_capable` of real monitors, a monitor reporting adaptive sync and following the frame rate, `DRM_CAP_ATOMIC_ASYNC_PAGE_FLIP` and drivers accepting async atomic flips (with `IN_FENCE_FD`, and of tiled GBM buffers), tearing a client asked for, drivers accepting `IN_FENCE_FD` from a Vulkan sync_file and waiting for it (no half-drawn frames), the timing and CPU time with fences against the wait, and a GPU hang seen through a flip that does not complete instead of the 100 ms wait.

### Native test machine bring-up

tools/native_test/bringup.py and tools/native_test/start_tty1.py, described in [laptop-bringup.md](laptop-bringup.md#the-bring-up-driver). Unit-tested through a fake SSH machine only; the laptop had not arrived when they were written.

- `report HOST`: lspci, the DRM cards with their drivers and `boot_vga`, connectors and their status, `/dev/dri`, `libinput list-devices` and `/proc/bus/input/devices`, `drm_info -j` per card, kernel, Mesa, libdrm, libinput, libseat and NVIDIA versions, Vulkan devices, logind and seatd, into target/bringup/HOST/report.md; per card the expected zero-copy path and cursor plane, from drm_info's primary-plane XR24 modifiers, cursor planes and size, async flips and `vrr_capable`, or the GPU family's usual paths (i915, xe, amdgpu, radeon, nvidia-drm, nouveau, simpledrm) without drm_info.
- `check HOST`: step 0's packages, marker, deployed build on a login `PATH`, test config, groups (seatd's, `video`, `input`), a password-less sudo rule for `chvt`, `fgconsole` and uinput.py, a getty on tty1 and, optionally, a toolchain for building there, each with the exact command that fixes it; sudo is only asked `-n -l`.
- `deploy HOST`: a release build here with `--jobs 2` below a load of 12, copied with the tools and the start script to `~/shingle-bringup` when the binaries' highest `GLIBC_` version, `NEEDED` libraries and architecture fit the target, else the commit pushed over the same SSH connection to `~/shingle` and built there; the commit goes into `DEPLOYED.json`.
- `step N [HOST]`: the step's text from native-testing.md and the `shingle-tty1` command for it; with HOST, steps 1 to 7, 10 to 14, 37, 38, 45, 48, 49 and 51 are driven over SSH (IPC, uinput and `chvt` through `sudo -n`, wlopm, grim, present_check.py, SIGTERM, SIGINT and SIGKILL) and recorded in the [Results](native-testing.md#results) format in target/bringup/HOST/results.md.
- `collect HOST`: the run's files and native.log copied to target/bringup/HOST/runs/STARTED, with present_check.py on every probe output and the log checks of tools/native_test/bringup_logs.py in summary.md.
- `shingle-tty1 --step N`: `shingle --backend native` with the step's config, client and trace variables, logging to `~/.local/state/shingle/native.log`, the run's files in `~/.local/state/shingle/bringup/current` and the previous run kept under `bringup/previous`.
- Safety: every subcommand refuses localhost, this host's names and addresses (also after `ssh -G`, before connecting), and a target with this host's `/etc/machine-id` or hostname; `deploy` and driven steps need `/etc/shingle-native-test-machine` on the target. Signals go only to the PID the start script recorded, checked for comm `shingle` and its start time in the same remote command. `shingle-tty1` refuses without the marker, over SSH, in a graphical session, off a VT and while the last run's Shingle runs.

Only the laptop can show whether drm_info's JSON has the fields the report reads (the parser follows drm_info's source and takes what it finds), whether wev's lines match the parser of step 3's timestamps, whether Debian's `sh -l` puts `~/.local/bin` on `PATH` for `check`, and whether the deployed release binary runs there.

### Application launcher

crates/shingle-launcher, docs/design/launcher.md ([features.md](features.md#application-launcher)). Its Decisions were taken on the operator's behalf and wait for review.

- Discovery: desktop entries of `$XDG_DATA_HOME` and `$XDG_DATA_DIRS` by desktop file ID with the first directory winning, filtered by `Type`, `NoDisplay`, `Hidden`, `OnlyShowIn`, `NotShowIn` and `TryExec`, localised by `LC_ALL`, `LC_MESSAGES` or `LANG`; `Exec` unquoted with its field codes; commands on `$PATH` not already named by an entry. The index is cached in `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/shingle/launcher-index.json` and rescanned in a thread after every first frame.
- Matching: fzy's scoring over the shown name, generic name, keywords and program, a bonus for entries over plain commands, and frecency from `$XDG_STATE_HOME/shingle/launcher-history.json`; a Run row and Ctrl+Enter run the typed text.
- The layout: `$XDG_DATA_HOME/shingle/launcher.yaml` with favourites, categories and their placed entries, renames, hidden entries and shortcuts, written atomically after every change, empty lists left out, and set aside as `launcher.yaml.bad` when it does not parse.
- The panel: Gloomy's widgets placed absolutely, its `TextInput` for the field, the menu and drag on its overlay layers, drawn on lavapipe (`launcher.renderer: auto`; `hardware` or `software` force one) and copied into `wl_shm` buffers over a premultiplied backdrop; a full-output overlay layer surface with exclusive keyboard, key repeat from the compositor's rate, and the cursor-shape protocol when offered.
- Editing: Ctrl+D, F2, Ctrl+N, Ctrl+E, Ctrl+M, Ctrl+H, Ctrl+Up and Down, Delete, Shift+F2, Ctrl+Shift+Up and Down, the right-click menus of entries and categories, and drags onto rows, categories, Favourites and Hidden.
- Launching: shingle-session's `launch_command` with a `ClientEnvironment` from the launcher's own `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, `DISPLAY` and `WAYFIRE_SOCKET`, without `WAYLAND_SOCKET`; terminal entries in `launcher.terminal`, `$TERMINAL`, foot, kitty, alacritty or xterm. A second invocation closes the first through `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/shingle-launcher-DISPLAY.sock`.
- Config: the `launcher:` section (crates/shingle-config/src/launcher.rs), validated, with the defaults for Wayfire and i3 imports, whose command bindings (such as one running rofi) stay as they are; data/shingle.yaml binds `super+space`.
- Icons: looked up by the Icon Theme Specification in `launcher.icon_theme`, else KDE's `kdeglobals` or GTK's `settings.ini`, its parents, hicolor and pixmaps; PNG and SVG decoded at the drawn size in device pixels and cached per theme and size in `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/shingle/launcher-icons`; read at the first draw, the rest decoded in a thread after the first frame and drawn as it arrives; a letter tile without one.
- Desktop actions: `[Desktop Action]` groups as search results under their entry, and first in the entry's menu, opened by a right click, the Menu key or Shift+F10.
- Fractional scale: `wp_fractional_scale_v1` with `wp_viewporter`, a buffer of the output's device pixels; the integer buffer scale without them.
- WCM: a Launcher page for the section (crates/shingle-wcm/src/ui.rs, `launcher_page`).
- Not done: XPM icons, text in SVG icons and GNOME's dconf icon theme ([design/launcher.md](design/launcher.md#open-items)). Never run under the host's Wayfire, only under headless Wayfire in the GPU harness.
- Shingle itself, found by the launcher's real runs: layer surfaces are sized by their viewport and buffer scale, not their buffer's pixels (crates/shingle/src/layer_shell.rs), and shm screencopy of an untransformed scaled output carries its device pixels rather than logical pixels repeated (crates/shingle/src/capture.rs, `crop_device`; crates/shingle-render/src/target.rs, `capture_frame_scaled`); STIPC takes `KEY_COMPOSE`.

## Verification

### Automated

`cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` runs 1606 tests and ignores 3 (2026-11-27, after the launcher's icons, desktop actions, fractional scale and WCM page, on the host with its render node, with none failing). Their 27 new tests: 20 in crates/shingle-launcher (desktop action groups, actions in the index with their icons and `Path`, action matching by their own name or both names and their frecency, actions in the catalog's search, the model launching an action from search and opening the menu with the Menu key, Shift+F10 and a right click, icon slots and letter tiles and the key-opened menu's place in the view; icon paths from the environment, directory size matching and distances, exact and closest directories, parents, hicolor, pixmaps, `~/.icons`, absolute paths and an inheritance loop, the theme from the config, KDE and GTK; SVG and PNG decoding, the cache file's round trip and rejection, decoding only changed files; icons drawn on lavapipe one texel per device pixel at scales 1, 1.5 and 2; PPM screenshots), 1 in crates/shingle-config/src/launcher.rs (renderer names), 2 in crates/shingle-wcm (the Launcher page's fields and their edits and refusals), 1 in crates/shingle/src/ipc.rs (STIPC key names), 1 in crates/shingle/src/output/protocol_tests.rs (a layer surface's size at buffer scale 2 and behind a viewport), 1 in crates/shingle/src/capture.rs (a device frame cut to the buffer) and 1 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests/captures.rs (a scale-2 output captured once in its device pixels). tools/gpu_test's test_launcher_checks.py has 7 Python tests (4 new).

Before them it ran 1579 tests and ignored 3 (2026-11-27, after the offscreen pacing fix, on the host with its render node, with none failing). Its 2 new tests in crates/shingle/src/lib.rs, through the in-process test client: `frame_presented` leaves the frame callback readable on the client's socket at once, and events sent between turns (a changed output's) reach the client's socket within 25 ms of a `poll` that starts right after a housekeeping tick. Without the fix the first finds nothing on the socket and the second sees the events after 49.6 to 50.0 ms, when the next tick ended the wait.

Before them it ran 1577 tests and ignored 3 (2026-11-27, after the application launcher, on the host with its render node, with none failing). The IPC test of `input/list-devices` checks the new fields too, and tools/output_test's Python tests have one new test and tools/gpu_test's two. After XW-6 it ran 1376 tests and ignored 3; XW-6's 21 new tests are described with the other XWayland tests below. After NB-10 it ran 1355 tests and ignored 3. NB-10's 45 new tests: 17 in crates/shingle-native/src/display/planes_tests.rs through the fake card (the cursor plane found and turned off by the start, no plane and the override, flips of the cursor plane alone with the first one tested and events that present nothing, a frame carrying the cursor and DPMS and resume modesets keeping it, a refused plane in a test and in a frame's flip, CRTCs sharing a cursor plane, an output turned off turning its cursor plane off, VRR on where configured and capable with a refresh of 0 and off otherwise, a reloaded `vrr`, async flips without `vsync`, none without the capability or with the override, a refused async flip made again in sync, a cursor change keeping a frame in sync, a fenced frame's `IN_FENCE_FD`, a refused fence and the fence override); 7 in crates/shingle-native/src/host/tests.rs with a real frontend, compositor and client (the drawn cursor redrawn on pointer motion, a cursor on the plane moving without frames, the override, inverted colours taking the cursor back into the frame in the flip that turns the plane off, a tearing client's async flips, VRR from the config, fenced frames until the card refuses a fence); 4 in kms/atomic.rs (the cursor plane in modesets, the teardown sparing it, a flip with a moved cursor and a fence, properties missing); 2 in cursor.rs, 4 in features.rs and 1 in outputs.rs; 6 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests/cursor.rs (frames where the drawn cursor moves, none for one on a plane, the plane's image and place, an empty plane, what it cannot show, frames leaving it out on the GPU); 3 in crates/shingle (the cursor on the pointer output only, cursor pixels kept with shm uploads, heads reporting adaptive sync); and 1 in crates/shingle-render/src/fence.rs, a device test that exported pending fences that signalled. One of four full runs had one failing test that the next three did not repeat; it was not identified. The colour and focus fixes, just before, added 6: 3 in crates/shingle-render/src/color.rs (sRGB decoding and clamping, every byte surviving decoding and the GPU's encoding as a fill and a clear colour, fills premultiplied and clears not); 2 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests/colours.rs on the GPU (`background_color` and Expo's background drawn as `#336699`'s bytes, around and inside the cells; a third, the drop preview premultiplied and its border drawn as its bytes, runs its tile check without a GPU) and 1 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests/switcher.rs (minimizing the focused view leaves the focus order alone). WG-6's sRGB test in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frame_raster.rs moved to color.rs. The three colour tests failed before the fix (124, 170, 203 for 51, 102, 153, and an unpremultiplied preview); the focus test passed before, as there was nothing to fix. `tile_flags_force_opaque_and_decode_srgb` in crates/shingle-render/src/gpu.rs now requires a texel's exact bytes, which the truncating gamma LUT missed by up to 4. Updated for the bytes: the screencopy tests in crates/shingle/src/screencopy.rs and output/protocol_tests.rs (the default background is 26, not 89), the frontend's screencopy test (26, and 254 inverted), vswipe's clear colour, and the pinned pixels `plain`, `scaled_transformed_zoomed`, `frame` and `gamma_hue_inverted`, re-blessed only for the gamma LUT. tools/gpu_test/test_colour_checks.py has 4 and test_focus_checks.py 3 Python tests of the harness helpers.

After WG-7 it ran 1304 tests and ignored 3. WG-7's 29 new tests: 16 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/switcher_view.rs (selection order forward, backward and mixed, two views and one, every slot's offset, depth, scale, turn and opacity and the dimmed background, a thumbnail expiring past a side while its copy fills the other, the render order by depth and stacking, letting go and finishing, pressing again during the exit, removed and minimized views, the options, views without bounds, and the projection against Wayfire's numbers); 8 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests/switcher.rs (input scripts through `FakeHost`: selection order both ways, the first press's modifiers and other keys held back, a view closing while switching, the last one closing, one view and none, a minimized view selected and restored, Scale ending the view, and on the GPU the thumbnails over black with the background layer dimmed and the panel above, then the usual scene after letting go); 3 in crates/shingle-render/src/draw.rs (an identity projection, the division by w and a corner behind the eye); 1 in crates/shingle/src/output/protocol_tests.rs (layer surfaces below and above, and stacking per output) and 1 in crates/shingle-config/src/lib.rs (the import, defaults, plugin list, bad values and YAML). tools/gpu_test/test_switcher_checks.py has 4 Python tests of the harness helpers.

After WG-6 it ran 1275 tests and ignored 3. WG-6's 42 new tests: 13 in crates/shingle/src/frame.rs (Wayfire's sizes and margins, shrinking and growing by margins, where the buttons, title and borders go, button order, scale and padding, dialogs and a title bar of 0, alignment leaving the layout alone, the hit order and corner edges, inert borders of tiles, and a release on the pressed button, a press shown until its release, the five-pixel drag, the double click and a border press); 10 in crates/shingle/src/decorator/tests.rs through the in-process test client (a server-side toplevel configured inside its framed tile with IPC's `geometry`, `bbox` and `base-geometry`, client-side toplevels and the plugin unloaded, the preferred mode, `unset_mode` and forcing, forced and ignored views, the three buttons, the title drag, a corner resize with its cursor and the double click, a floating window keeping its client as its frame comes and goes, fullscreen, a reload changing and removing the frame, placement and size limits with the frame) and 2 of its helpers; 3 in crates/shingle/src/xwayland/windows.rs (X windows framed or not by their hints and a hint change, a request clamped with its frame, a dialog's frame centred on its parent's); 7 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frame_raster.rs (sRGB decoding, a frame's pieces, a title's size, alignment, ellipsis and font scale, button pixels in each look); 1 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/frames.rs (a frame carried along with its surface); 4 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests/frames.rs (the tiles and the focus colours of a frame on the GPU, a title-bar drag and the maximize button through the frontend's input, Scale placing the frame in its slot); 1 in crates/shingle-render/tests/offscreen_pixels.rs (a decorated window's pinned pixels, tests/golden/frame.rgba) and 1 in crates/shingle-config/src/lib.rs (every option imported, clamping, the plugin list, bad values, YAML). crates/shingle-xwayland/src/xwm/props.rs's Motif test checks wlroots' reading now, and the examples' option tests the new `wl_probe --decoration` and `xw_probe --no-decorations`. tools/gpu_test/test_decoration_checks.py has 3 Python tests of the harness helpers, and tools/xwayland_test/test_run.py one more.

After WG-5 it ran 1233 tests and ignored 3. WG-5's 26 new tests: 11 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/vswipe.rs (direction locking, disabled axes and single rows, diagonal and promoted swipes, speed factor and cap, the rubber band, every case of `finish_target`, long and short swipes, the slide over the duration, smooth and immediate updates, a zero duration, diagonal swipes across several workspaces); 1 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/workspace_transition.rs (the wall at a fractional viewport); 12 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests/vswipe.rs, input scripts through `FakeHost` (long and short swipes without vswitch's slide, direction locking, disabled axes, the grid's edges in every wraparound mode, both thresholds, speed factor and cap, free movement, smooth and immediate commits and a swipe during the slide, swipes of other finger counts and undecided ones reaching a client and a started one cancelled for it, the plugin unloaded and other finger counts, Scale and a disabled pointer, and the wall's gap and background on the GPU); 1 in crates/shingle-config/src/lib.rs (every option imported, Wayfire's defaults, the plugin list, bad values, YAML) and 1 in crates/shingle/src/ipc.rs (`stipc/feed_gesture` to clients and to a frontend, and its errors). tools/gpu_test/test_vswipe_checks.py has 2 Python tests of the harness helpers.

After WG-4 it ran 1207 tests and ignored 3. WG-4's 31 new tests: 17 in crates/shingle/src/wayfire_shell/tests.rs (edge and corner triggers, hotspot bounds and proximity, the hotspot timer and its leave, and through the in-process test client the global's version, hotspots entering after their timeout, proximity and leave, a hotspot's timer waking the event loop by itself, version 2 without proximity, an output going ending its hotspots and fullscreen, fullscreen on its own output only with focus on the other output, a client's own fullscreen request, `toggle_menu` per output and from version 2, the IPC method with `output_id`, `output-id` and its errors, a STIPC super tap without a frontend pressed alone, with a key, a button or Shift in between, held too long and on the right logo key, the binding off, inhibits counted and released with their client, `interactive_move`, and an inert object for an output that is gone); 5 in crates/shingle/src/modifier_binding.rs (pressed alone, another key in between, the timeout and 0, interruptions, several modifiers); 1 in crates/shingle/src/output/protocol_tests.rs (a fullscreen window hides only its own output's panels); 1 in crates/shingle-config/src/lib.rs (modifier-alone bindings in text, YAML and matching) and 2 in wayfire.rs (`toggle_menu` and `modifier_binding_timeout` imported, commands taking a modifier alone, the plugin list turning the binding off); 5 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests.rs (a super tap toggling the menu and not with a key, button or Shift in between, STIPC keys through the frontend, holding too long, a command bound to `<alt>`, an inhibited output drawn black, and a client-started move leaving no button held). tools/output_test/test_wayland_wire.py has 5 Python tests of the raw Wayland client and the harness helpers.

After WG-3 it ran 1176 tests and ignored 3. WG-3's 17 new tests: 7 in crates/shingle-session/src/environment.rs (Wayfire's variables in a bare environment, an inherited `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP` kept even when empty, a configured cursor theme and size replacing inherited ones, the `default` theme leaving the inherited one, the default size keeping only a numeric inherited size, `WAYFIRE_CONFIG_FILE` replacing or removing a host one, a spawned shell seeing the variables); 3 in crates/shingle-config/src/lib.rs (only a Wayfire file is exported, a relative path made absolute, `load` recording the format it detected); 3 in crates/shingle/src/lib.rs (the cursor variables after `apply_config` and after `set-config-options`, the Wayfire file kept across `apply_config` and an inherited `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP`); 2 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests.rs (a reload of the file changing the cursor variables and `WAYFIRE_CONFIG_FILE` of later children, `super+shift+2` sending the window without following it); 2 in crates/shingle-bin/src/main.rs (the session file's keys, and its `Exec` planning a native start in a display manager's environment). tools/output_test/test_run.py has 3 more Python tests, of the `env` parser and the environment comparison.

After WG-2 it ran 1159 tests and ignored 3. WG-2's 27 new tests: 4 in crates/shingle-config/src/wayfire_options.rs (the table holds the pinned metadata's 332 options, sorted, with their types, defaults and entries; values report the file's text, or the default when unset or rejected; dynamic lists pair their entries by name; object instances such as `output:DP-1`); 6 in plugins.rs (plugin files naming their plugin, no list turning everything on, `ipc` and unknown names always on, an empty list turning everything off, shared bindings, `xkb:` commands); 8 in wayfire.rs (an empty file has Wayfire's defaults and none of Shingle's extra bindings, every default of the table applies but `squeezimize`, Shingle bindings set by their own names, unlisted plugins off and their text kept, the shared maximize binding, the numbered vswitch lists, autostart's wf-shell rule in its three cases and without the plugin, the values kept for IPC); 3 in crates/shingle/src/shell.rs (views float and cannot tile without simple-tile and open at the workarea's top left without place, a restored tile floats, a reload without simple-tile floats the tiles); 1 in lib.rs (the protocol plugins' globals); 2 in ipc.rs (every option answered with its live value or default, and what a Wayfire file set); 1 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/bindings.rs and 2 in frontend/tests.rs (the numbered vswitch bindings switching, carrying and sending through STIPC keys; unloaded scale and simple-tile). Tests that imported a snippet of an unlisted plugin now give a `[core] plugins` line, and the defaults they expect are Wayfire's. tools/wayfire_options/test_generate.py has 9 Python tests of the generator on a sample of the metadata and of the checked-in table, and tools/gpu_test/test_wayfire_checks.py 3 of the harness helpers.

After WG-1 it ran 1132 tests and ignored 3. WG-1's 35 new tests: 7 in crates/shingle-config/src/ini.rs, one per wf-config line rule (unescaped `#` anywhere, `\#`, a trailing `\` joining lines, `\\` not joining, empty and comment lines ending a continuation, trimming as wf-config's, sections and malformed lines); 7 in activator.rs for each binding kind (keys and buttons with their modifiers, alternatives and empty ones, `none` and `disabled`, a modifier alone, gestures, hotspots, unknown modifiers); 5 in import.rs (a failed or out-of-range option keeping the previous value, notes becoming warnings, Expo bindings and rules outside the final grid dropped, the animation fallbacks, single bindings); 8 in wayfire.rs (Wayfire's stock wayfire.ini at the surveyed commit warning only about `[cube]` and `[wayfire-shell]`, the synthetic WCM-style fixture's 15 warnings and values, the old `pinch in 4` key gone, lines outside sections, an unescaped `#` after `=`, a bad value among good ones, command bindings and the sections Shingle reads); 1 in i3.rs; 3 in view_matcher.rs (Wayfire's default `enabled_for`, `equals` predicates, the view types); 2 in lib.rs (the survey's B1 and B2 findings, first pinned as refused, now each imported; colour notations); 1 in crates/shingle/src/ipc.rs for the inner and outer gap options; and 1 in crates/shingle-wcm for the warning report. Tests that expected an error from a bad Wayfire or i3 value now expect a warning and the default.

NB-8 brought the count to 1097. Its 21 new tests, all in crates/shingle-native and all through fakes: 4 in scanout/gbm.rs check the negotiation (the plane's order kept; metadata planes, modifiers unknown to GBM and the implicit modifier left out), its reasons naming both lists, three buffers allocated with the negotiated modifiers, made framebuffers of, imported, flipped least recently shown first and freed, and each failure (no common modifier, no GPU, GBM allocation, two planes, a refused framebuffer, a failed import, no `IN_FORMATS`) freeing what it made; 2 in scanout.rs `SHINGLE_SCANOUT` and importers without a GPU; 11 in display/zero_copy_tests.rs the display through the fake card (the `TEST_ONLY` and blocking flip with only `FB_ID` and the dumb buffers freed, frames presented with `ZERO_COPY`, `SHINGLE_SCANOUT=copy` committing nothing, no GBM, a renderer without a GPU, no shared modifier, no `IN_FORMATS`, a refused test freeing the GBM buffers, a test refused for lack of master tried again, waiting for a pending flip, a pause and DPMS off, modesets of a resume showing a GBM buffer, the fallback when the renderer cannot draw and when a flip is refused, a new mode tried again at its size and a refused mode change keeping zero-copy); 4 in host/tests.rs the backend with a real compositor and client (frames drawn into the GBM buffers after the tested blocking flip and the client's feedback carrying `ZERO_COPY`, `SHINGLE_SCANOUT=copy` reading frames back without it, the fallback after a renderer cannot draw into the buffers, and a GPU loss drawing on into the same buffers). tools/native_test's Python tests, now 17, add present_check.py's zero-copy count and `--scanout`, and its argument parsing. No test constructs `GbmAllocator`, `DrmDevice` or `GpuRenderer`.

MO-7 brought the count to 1076. Its 39 new tests: 8 in crates/shingle-native/src/crtcs.rs check the CRTC matching (a CRTC per connector, a constrained connector not starved by an earlier one, more connectors than CRTCs leaving the last dark, connectors kept on their current CRTC, lit outputs never moved, connectors without a possible CRTC, a CRTC claimed twice and four monitors on three CRTCs); 1 in outputs.rs the connector priority (lit, named, internal, card order, without disconnected and `off` connectors); 2 in kms/output.rs the allocation of CRTCs and primary planes with a lit output keeping its pair and the teardown of several outputs; 1 in kms/atomic.rs the combined request of two outputs and a teardown; 13 in display/tests.rs the display on a fake card with a CRTC per connector: two monitors in one tested modeset, the teardown sparing every lit output, a failing combined test leaving the second monitor dark and listed as unlit, a shared width limit giving the second a smaller mode, flips paced and routed per CRTC with each output's own sequence, a lost flip recovered on its output alone, a second and a third monitor plugged in and one unplugged with its CRTC turned off, a monitor lit once a CRTC is free, outputs turned off and on one at a time while another's flip is pending, a configuration across heads tested and applied at once and kept across a hotplug and a reload, a refused configuration restoring every output with its buffers and blobs, the `--outputs` cap and a named output lit first; 7 in host/tests.rs the backend with a real compositor and client: two outputs paced by their own flips with frame callbacks only from the window's output, monitors plugged in and out adding and removing outputs and the window coming back under `return`, a hotplug waiting for every flip without starting frames, a monitor without a free CRTC listed as a disabled head and lit when one is free, output management turning one monitor off and on, wlr-output-power turning one monitor off while the other draws, and absolute devices mapped to their output or the layout with relative motion crossing; 1 in input.rs the translator's events on a mapped output; 6 in crates/shingle/src/output/backend_tests.rs the compositor's side (mode changes of two heads as one request, a monitor turned off and on through the backend with its head kept, every monitor off refused without asking the backend, connected monitors listed as disabled heads, the dark last output lit again with its windows). The output request test in crates/shingle/src/output/tests.rs now expects heads, and the `--outputs` plan test in shingle-bin now accepts native. No NB-7 or NB-9 display or host test changed; the host fixture's flip helper reads the fake card's pending flips per CRTC. The Wayland pin transcript is unchanged. tools/native_test has 15 Python tests (1 new, for `present_check.py --output`). Before MO-7 it ran 1037 (MO-6). After MO-6 it ran 1037: Its 12 new tests: 5 in crates/shingle/src/output/host.rs check that without host windows `stipc/create_wayland_output` and `destroy_wayland_output` refuse with Wayfire's errors; that creating adds `HEADLESS-2` at once at 1280,0 and asks the host for its window after `Event::OutputAdded`; that destroying refuses a missing or mistyped `output`, an unknown output, an output created over IPC and the last host window; that a window closed under `return` sends its window to the other output and gives it back when the next window takes the freed name; and that points and motion on a window map to its own output. 1 in crates/shingle/src/output/registry.rs checks the lowest free `HEADLESS-N`, kept by turned-off outputs. 5 in crates/shingle-nested/src/windows.rs check the window-to-output map both ways, closing a window, one window per output, nested's focus while any window has it, start names and titles, and window sizes per output (which replaced the one-window size test in lib.rs). 1 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests/outputs.rs checks through `FakeHost` that STIPC opens and closes host windows and that `output_resized_on` sizes the window's output, and 1 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/test_gpu.rs the identity gamma ramp. The method inventory test now counts 85, and the `--outputs` plan test accepts nested and refuses native. The Wayland pin transcript is unchanged. The frontend test binary crashed with SIGSEGV inside Vulkan's validation layer in 7 of 40 runs before its tests shared one GPU context and in none of 40 runs of the same binary, and then of 20 runs of `cargo test -p shingle-frontend --lib`, afterwards. The Python tests: tools/gpu_test 56 (3 new, for nested_output_checks.py), tools/xwayland_test 31 (2 new, for passing the host uid and refusing a non-numeric one), tools/output_test 9 and tools/session_test 14. Before MO-6 it ran 1025 (MO-5). After MO-5 it ran 1025: its 19 new tests: 10 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests/outputs.rs drive the frontend with `FakeHost`, the in-process client and a recording target on two outputs and check that each output draws only its own windows, moved into its own coordinates; that a commit asks for frames of its window's output only; that presenting one output sends only the frame callbacks of its windows; that Scale lays out and draws only the active output's windows on that output, leaves the other drawn as it is and ends when the other output becomes active; that the "Workspace set N" label is drawn at 10,10 on the output that selected a set, not on the other, until `label_duration` passes, with `next_deadline` waking for its end; that a floating window being dragged across the boundary is drawn on both outputs and, dropped on its own, only there; that the wheel zooms the output under the pointer, around a point relative to it; that a capture is answered by its own output's frame at that output's size; that an output created over IPC gives its window frame callbacks without any host frame and is drawn for a capture, while the host is asked only for its own outputs; and that STIPC motion moves the frontend's pointer. 4 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/outputs.rs check rectangle overlap, the label's place, the 60 Hz grid of IPC outputs and saturating translation. In crates/shingle, 1 in crates/shingle/src/ipc.rs checks that STIPC motion, buttons and the `S-` Super become events for a frontend and are dropped while the session is inactive; 2 in crates/shingle/src/output/focus_tests.rs that selecting a set asks for the label on both outputs whose set changed, and on the same output again, and that a window straddling two outputs is drawn only on its own by default, with `window_surfaces` listing it; 1 in crates/shingle/src/output/protocol_tests.rs that with `remove_output_limits` it is drawn on and enters both. 1 in shingle-bin paces two offscreen outputs at two rates on their own, and 3 existing ones were adapted (`--outputs` accepted for offscreen, the per-output host and target); the frontend's scene tests now name the output they draw. Each new scene test draws both outputs with one target, as a host draws them on one device. NVIDIA's driver still crashes the frontend test binary now and then (SIGSEGV in its Vulkan loader while tests create devices in parallel): 1 run in 12 with and 1 in 12 without the new tests, as before MO-5. The Wayland pin transcript is unchanged. tools/gpu_test gained 7 Python unit tests (output_checks.py's frame counts, rates, event parsing and layout) and one adapted, now 53. Before MO-5 it ran 1006 (MO-4). After MO-4 it ran 1006: Its 22 new tests: 15 in crates/shingle/src/output/focus_tests.rs check, with the in-process client and two outputs, that pointer motion alone keeps the active output and a click on the other output makes it active and focuses its window, with `Event::OutputFocused` and one `output-gain-focus`; that focusing a window, also over IPC, focuses its output and focusing the active output changes nothing; that the pointer is pulled onto the closest output; that `oswitch` next wraps, warps the pointer to the target's centre with `Event::PointerWarp` naming it, takes the focused window along with `view-set-output` and `view-wset-changed`, and that left, right and below follow the layout; that `configure-view` with `output_id` appends a tiled window to the target tree, scales a floating window into the target output (100,50 on 1280x720 to 1342,41 on 800x600) and fills it with a fullscreen one, refuses an unknown output, and with a geometry places the window there; that `vswitch/set-workspace` switches the second output's workspace with its view and without focusing it, refuses a view of another output, `send-view` works on any output's view and activators with `output-id` or `output_id` make that output active, with `output id not found!` for an unknown one; that `simple-tile` layouts reach any set and move views of other sets in; that `wsets/set-output-wset` takes a set from another output, which gets a new one, and swaps the sets in two calls while empty sets go, and `send-view-to-wset` parks a window in a set nobody shows; that a `start_on_output` rule moves a new window; that `set_fullscreen` with the second output's `wl_output` moves the window there at its size; that a dialog maps on its parent's output and moves with it; that a floating window of an unplugged output is scaled onto the active one with its events; that the active output that goes is focused again when it returns within the timeout and not after; and that STIPC `super+o` and a click run without a frontend. 2 in crates/shingle/src/output/moving.rs check scaling and clamping rectangles, 1 in crates/shingle/src/output/bindings.rs matches oswitch and wsets bindings, 1 in crates/shingle-core/src/wset.rs creates named sets, 1 in crates/shingle-config/src/window_rule.rs parses `start_on_output`, and 2 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests.rs check through `FakeHost` that `super+o` focuses the next output and warps the pointer and `super+shift+o` takes the focused window, and that a move drag makes the output it enters active and drops a tile into that output's tree. The method inventory test now counts 83. The Wayland pin transcript is unchanged. tools/output_test's 9 Python unit tests pass unchanged. Before MO-4 it ran 984 (MO-3). After MO-3 it ran 984: Its 23 new tests: 17 in crates/shingle/src/output/protocol_tests.rs check, with the in-process client bound to both outputs, that a window enters only the output it is shown on, once however often it commits, and leaves it when it moves to the other output's set or another workspace hides it, and leaves an output that goes; that its fractional and buffer scales are its output's; that a layer panel on `HEADLESS-2` is configured to that output's width, takes its exclusive zone from that output's workarea only, is drawn only there and is closed when the output goes, and that a panel without an output goes on the active one; that `locked` waits until both outputs have a lock surface of their own size, each output draws its own, removing one keeps the session locked and a second surface on one output is `duplicate_output`; that presentation feedback and FIFO barriers wait for the frames of the surface's primary output; that `grim`-style captures of a scale-2 output are in its physical pixels and output-local coordinates, regions are clipped to their output and a region beside it fails; that output management turns an output off (its head listed as disabled, also to a manager bound later, its window joining the other output) and on again (at a new position, its window back), refuses to turn every output off, keeps the first output first and keeps configured placements across a config reload; that power controls turn one output off, the last one off is DPMS off and waking lights both, with a removed output's control failed; that each output has a gamma ramp and one control of its own; that each output has its own ext-workspace group with output-named ids and activation switching only that output; that a foreign-toplevel handle follows its window's output; and the warning for negative positions. 3 in crates/shingle/src/surface_outputs.rs check the primary-output rule (largest overlap above a tenth, the previous output kept below it, a surface on no output keeping its own) and rectangle intersection; 3 in crates/shingle/src/screencopy.rs check capture geometry: a region scaled into the buffer and logical pixels spread over buffer pixels, a turned output's buffer turned back, and regions that miss the output. The Wayland pin transcript is unchanged. tools/output_test gained 2 Python unit tests (grim's scale and the colour tests), now 9, and tools/xwayland_test 1 (the `xrandr --listmonitors` parser), now 29. Before MO-3 it ran 961 (MO-2). After MO-2 it ran 961: Its 25 new tests: 17 in crates/shingle/src/output/multi_tests.rs check, with the in-process client, that two outputs have their own globals with their names, `wl_output` positions and xdg-output geometry side by side; that an output added later reaches bound clients with its global and output-management head, and its removal disables the global and finishes the head; fixed positions before automatic ones, which move when an earlier output grows or goes; unique names; `move`, `park` and `return` with a window on the removed output, a replugged output's new IPC id, and no empty set kept behind; the active output passing on in add order; the last output going dark and a new monitor taking its windows; a monitor found by its EDID identity on another connector; a restored session waiting for its outputs, with a set whose output never comes merged under `return` into the saved active output; frame callbacks only from the frames of the window's own output; IPC replies with real ids, a parked set and a view on it; creating and destroying headless outputs with Wayfire's errors, events and unreused names and ids; and Wayfire's payloads for `output-layout-changed` (once per batch) and `wset-workspace-changed`, and `output-wset-changed` for a returning output. 3 in crates/shingle/src/output/registry.rs check unreused IPC ids, generations and `HEADLESS-N` names and which outputs have an EDID identity; 5 in shingle-bin check `--outputs` parsing, its refusal for other backends, `WLR_HEADLESS_OUTPUTS`, per-output frame pacing at two rates and the extra headless outputs side by side. 4 existing tests were adapted: the method inventory (79 to 81), the reload of an invalid output (a fixed position now comes from `output_position`), the session saved under a connector name (it now waits for `restore_outputs` and goes back to `DP-1` when the output is described as it) and the placements in crates/shingle/src/output/tests.rs (`position` instead of `x` and `y`). The Wayland pin transcript is unchanged. tools/output_test gained 3 Python unit tests (xdg-output parsing and swapping outputs in a session), now 7. Before MO-2 it ran 936 (MO-1). Its 39 new tests: 14 in crates/shingle-core/src/wset.rs check lowest-free set indices, attaching and parking, parked sets keeping their trees and workarea, the set-keyed calls on parked sets, moving a window with its dialogs, transfers with and without away records, returns to the old places with the copy's leaf states, closed, moved and late-mapped windows during a return, a window moved twice returning to its first set, a window arriving in the set it is away from, a return without a copy, destroying sets, finding parked sets by identity and name (two identical monitors without serials, another monitor on the same connector) and away records through `rename_output`; 4 in crates/shingle-core/src/session.rs load a version 1 file as `master` wrote it, round-trip a parked set with its copy, away record and active output and return its window after the restore, check every new snapshot invariant and restore parked sets; 17 in shingle-config cover `position` in YAML, Wayfire and i3, the wlroots layout, logical sizes, pointer clamping, oswitch's next, previous and directional outputs and its 20 % rule, layout warnings, and the new sections with their defaults, YAML round trip, validation and Wayfire and i3 imports; 2 in crates/shingle/src/ipc.rs apply a cross-direction layout twice and change only weights; and 2 in crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests.rs check that weights alone and a floating window's new rectangle reach the session file. The Wayland pin transcript was rewritten for the session's version 2 form; the trees, windows and every other line in it are unchanged. Before MO-1 it ran 897 (moving session writes off the compositor thread). Its 13 new tests: 12 in crates/shingle-session/src/writer.rs drive `SessionWriter` with a fake slow writer that the test releases write by write, and check that submitting returns while a write is held, that a burst during one write is written once as the newest snapshot, that writes follow the order of submission (300 submissions against a writer that sleeps), that an unchanged snapshot is skipped and a failed one written again, that `finish` waits for the write in progress, drops the one waiting and writes the final snapshot last (even an unchanged one), returning its error rather than reporting it, that dropping the writer still writes what waits, that errors are reported once per kind until a write succeeds, how error kinds are told apart, and that the store writer saves atomically with no temporary file left; one frontend test checks that layout changes reach the file through the writer (crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests.rs). Before it ran 884 (the SIGTERM exit fix). That fix's 5 tests check that headless shutdown has removed the X lock, the X socket and the Wayland socket by the time the session is saved and still saves the outputs (crates/shingle-bin/src/headless.rs), that `Compositor::stop_xwayland` stops a running Xwayland and frees the display before the drop and can be called twice (crates/shingle/src/xwayland/mod.rs), that `Frontend::shutdown` frees the display, saves once and ignores later saves, and saves mapped windows (crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests.rs, 2), and that `SessionStore::save_snapshot` writes the model as it was when the snapshot was taken (crates/shingle-session); the headless and frontend display tests fail with the old order. Before the fix it ran 879 (DB-6). On the DB-6 branch the suite was run inside `bwrap --dev /dev` with lavapipe: 876 passed and the three reference-image tests failed as described below (85 pixels beyond the tolerance), and the new shm pixel tests passed there too. crates/shingle-render/tests/offscreen_pixels.rs passed 20 runs in a row on NVIDIA after its tests began sharing one GPU context. Before DB-6 it ran 856 (DB-5). On the DB-5 branch the suite was run inside `bwrap --dev /dev` with lavapipe: 853 passed and the three reference-image tests failed as described below; the syncobj device tests found no render node there and skipped. Before DB-5 it ran 829 (DB-4 with the FIFO and commit-timing queue). Before DB-4 it ran 797 (DB-3). On the DB-4 branch, before the queue change, the suite was run inside `bwrap --dev /dev` with lavapipe: 824 passed and the three reference-image tests failed as described below; the frontend's DMA-BUF tests ran there on lavapipe with their fake importer. On the DB-3 branch the suite was run inside `bwrap --dev /dev` with lavapipe: 794 passed and the three reference-image tests failed as described below. Before DB-3 it ran 780 (DB-2). On the DB-2 branch the suite was run inside `bwrap --dev /dev` with lavapipe: 777 passed and the three reference-image tests failed as described below. Before DB-2 it ran 758 (DB-1). On the DB-1 branch the suite was run inside `bwrap --dev /dev` with lavapipe: 755 passed and the three reference-image tests failed as described below; the udmabuf import tests skipped (no `/dev/udmabuf`) and the exported-buffer import tests ran on lavapipe. Before DB-1 it ran 740 (NB-9). On the NB-9 branch the suite was run inside `bwrap --dev /dev` with lavapipe: 737 passed and the three reference-image tests failed as described below. Before NB-9 it ran 692. Coverage of the other crates is uneven. On the NB-7 branch the suite was run inside `bwrap --dev /dev` with `VK_ICD_FILENAMES` set to lavapipe, so that no test could open a GPU, input or VT device of this host: 689 passed, and the three reference-image tests in crates/shingle-render/tests/offscreen_pixels.rs failed with 85 pixels beyond the tolerance, exactly as they do on lavapipe without the branch's changes, since the references were rendered on this host's GPU.

The Wayland pin test (`wayland_toplevels_look_unchanged`, crates/shingle/src/wayland_pins.rs) plays one scripted session of xdg toplevels through the in-process test client: two windows (one with size hints), fullscreen, minimize, always-on-top, sticky, alpha, a float through `configure-view`, a retitle, a child window, focus, `close-view` and destruction, then a restore of the saved session into a new compositor. It records every IPC reply and event (`list-views`, `get-focused-view`, `view-info` and the `view-*` events), the renderer events, the visible and Scale surface snapshots, each `xdg_toplevel.configure` with its states, the close requests and the session snapshot (with the test process's command line, directory and PID replaced), 296 lines, and requires them to match crates/shingle/testdata/wayland_pins.txt byte for byte. The file was recorded on `master` at `daad6af`, before the XW-1 refactor, and the refactor left it unchanged. The client-environment tests (crates/shingle-session/src/environment.rs and lib.rs, crates/shingle/src/lib.rs, crates/shingle-bin/src/headless.rs) check the variables with and without an X display and IPC, that values a host set on a command are overridden, and that real children started by `launch_command`, `launch_program`, `launch_autostart`, `launch_saved`, `stipc/run` and headless `--spawn` see no `DISPLAY`, Shingle's `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` and the IPC socket. `launch::capture_pid` is tested on a fake `/proc`.

The XWayland server tests (crates/shingle-xwayland, 32 tests; crates/shingle/src/xwayland.rs, 10; crates/shingle-config, 4; crates/shingle-wcm, 1) cover display selection, stale and malformed locks, a leftover socket file, the abstract socket and cleanup on drop in a temporary directory; Xwayland's argv in both modes; the restart rule; readiness parsing; and, with bash scripts standing in for Xwayland, inherited descriptors and environment, readiness, a failed start, SIGTERM then SIGKILL, lazy start on a real connection, eager start with the compositor, no restart after a quick exit, eager restart at once and lazy restart on the next connection after a run longer than 5 s, a failed window manager stopping the server, a second compositor taking the next display and a changed mode keeping the running display. The X window manager's bring-up is checked request by request against a recording `XConnection`, including missing Composite or XFixes, an old XFixes and a refused redirect. The config tests cover the lazy default, YAML booleans and strings, round trips, the Wayfire import and its `true` default, and WCM's fields. `Config::default` disables XWayland, and the frontend test fixture does too, so no test binds anything in `/tmp`.

The managed-window tests (XW-3; crates/shingle-xwayland, 38 more tests, now 70 with the probe's 2; crates/shingle/src/xwayland/windows.rs, 22) cover the property parsers on byte buffers (`WM_CLASS`, Latin-1 and UTF-8 text, `WM_SIZE_HINTS` with minimum, maximum and base sizes, `WM_HINTS` with input assumed when the flag is missing, `_MOTIF_WM_HINTS`), `_NET_WM_STATE` and `WM_PROTOCOLS` atom lists, the window types, Wayfire's classification (managed, dialog, unmanaged parented popups, override-redirect, DND), `Pairing` with either side first, different serials, destroys on either side, a remap and a replaced serial, and the `ConfigureRequest` policy for unmapped, tiled and floating windows, including clamping to the workarea. Against the recording `XConnection`, the window manager's requests are checked for a `MapRequest` (watch, one pipelined property batch, XRes, `MapWindow`, `WM_STATE` Normal, both client lists), XRes winning over `_NET_WM_PID`, `_NET_WM_NAME` winning over `WM_NAME` and falling back, `WM_TRANSIENT_FOR` naming only known windows, one serial per surface, unmap and destroy, map-order client lists, the value mask of a `ConfigureRequest`, configure with its synthetic `ConfigureNotify` and dedup, `_NET_WM_STATE` written once, close by `WM_DELETE_WINDOW` or `KillClient`, override-redirect windows and quiet `BadWindow` errors for destroyed windows. In `shingle`, the in-process test client connects as Xwayland and window manager events are applied directly: the global is hidden from other clients; zero and repeated serials and a second role are protocol errors; destroying the role object before the surface is allowed; all six orders of buffer, map and serial give one mapped window; X windows tile, get configured and report focus; `list-views` has class, title and PID; the identity comes from `capture_pid`; a placeholder is claimed by class; `view-pre-map` holds an X window; `close-view` and `configure-view` reach the window manager; fullscreen and minimize reach `_NET_WM_STATE`; `ConfigureRequest` follows the placement; title, class and size hints follow property changes; a kind change unmanages and remanages; override-redirect windows stay out of `list-views` and the scene; unmap removes the window and a remap brings it back; a window destroyed before its surface commits and a surface destroyed before the serial message leave no window; and losing the window manager forgets every X window. The harness helpers (`signal_checked`, the xwininfo and xprop parsers, `tiles_fill`) have 13 Python unit tests in tools/xwayland_test/test_run.py.

The X focus, unmanaged-window and client-request tests (XW-4; crates/shingle-xwayland, 27 more tests, now 97 with the probe's 2; crates/shingle, 19: crates/shingle/src/xwayland/behaviour_tests.rs 16, requests.rs 1, xdg_output.rs 2; crates/shingle-bin, 2) cover the ICCCM input-model table and which windows are focused or offered the focus on map, wlroots' override-redirect focus heuristic, the parent a `NORMAL` window drops, `_NET_WM_STATE` add, remove and toggle with maximized needing both directions, `WM_CHANGE_STATE`, the `_NET_WM_MOVERESIZE` edge mapping and the root cursor's pixel format. Against the recording `XConnection` they check the focus requests for each input model with `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` and `_NET_WM_STATE_FOCUSED`, that override-redirect and unknown windows are never focused, `WM_TAKE_FOCUS` offered only to windows that list it, the `FocusIn` guard against another client, around grabs and for the focused window itself, the same-client exception only after the last `SetInputFocus` (with sequence wrap-around), the active window cleared on unmap, client messages turned into requests (and into initial state before the map), modal kept by the window manager, `WM_STATE` Iconic and Normal, restacking with siblings and `_NET_CLIENT_LIST_STACKING`, override-redirect moves and the root cursor request. In `shingle`: unmanaged windows drawn at their X position and above a fullscreen window, out of `list-views`, following moves and gone on unmap; a menu taking the pointer but not the keyboard; an untyped override-redirect window taking the keyboard on its first buffer (also when its serial came first), with the pointer, the X focus and activation left alone, and giving it back; X focus following the keyboard between X and Wayland windows; input models on map; client state requests and the initial state; activation, move, resize and close requests; transient dialogs centred on their parent; fixed-size windows floating; `NORMAL` windows with `WM_TRANSIENT_FOR` tiling without a parent; stacking following focus; an `app_id is "XTerm"` window rule; and X windows in the Scale and Expo surfaces. xdg-output version 3 ends its details with `wl_output.done` (and earlier versions with `zxdg_output_v1.done`), and headless registers its STIPC devices and ticks at the refresh rate. The harness helpers have 19 Python unit tests in tools/xwayland_test/test_run.py (6 new: the `xwininfo -children` parser, Xcursor files, PPM captures and STIPC typing).

The selection bridge tests (XW-5; crates/shingle-xwayland, 20 more tests, now 115 and the probe's 2; crates/shingle, 7 in crates/shingle/src/xwayland/selection/tests.rs; the Wayland probe's 1) cover the MIME and target mapping both ways (UTF-8 and plain text, names with `/`, meta targets left out, each target once, the first target for a type winning, requests served from the other text type) and, against the recording `XConnection` with real pipes for the Wayland side: the selection windows and XFixes selections; taking the X selection for a Wayland selection, `TARGETS` and `TIMESTAMP` (into the target for an obsolete client); refusals without a Wayland selection, for an unoffered type and for `WM_S0`; a small transfer to X as one property after the source ends; 196 KiB to X through `INCR` chunks of 64 KiB, with the requestor's event mask set and reset; a second transfer completing while a source writes nothing; an X owner becoming an offer, its atom names fetched only when unknown, and its end clearing it; a stale X owner's `TARGETS` reply ignored after a newer Wayland selection; a later X owner replacing the Wayland one without the bridge disowning it; a transfer from X copied whole, refused, or through 20 `INCR` chunks that wait for a reader that does not read; and a vanished reader or requestor ending its transfer. In `shingle`, with the test client as a wlr-data-control client: a new device hears both selections at once; an X clipboard and primary selection are offered with their types, and their reads wait for the window manager with the client's descriptor; a Wayland selection is reported once, an X request reaches its source, and an X owner cancels it; a replaced X11 source is forgotten and a late clear leaves a Wayland selection alone; losing the window manager forgets only X selections; and `disable_primary_selection` bridges only the clipboard. The probe's test parses its `--copy` and `--delay-ms` options. The flaky `eager_mode_does_not_wait_for_connections` (it once failed under load) is fixed: fake-Xwayland scripts are written by a `sh` child (`server::write_script`, with its own test), so no fork in a parallel test thread can make their `exec` fail with `ETXTBSY`. The harness helpers have 25 Python unit tests (6 new: payloads, labels, selection options, byte comparison, and helper processes fed through stdin and stopped through `signal_checked`).

The drag and drop tests (XW-6; crates/shingle-xwayland, 12: 10 in crates/shingle-xwayland/src/xwm/tests/dnd.rs against the recording `XConnection` and 2 in xwm/dnd.rs; crates/shingle, 9: 8 in crates/shingle/src/xwayland/dnd/tests.rs with an X window from the fixture and the in-process test client's `wl_data_device`, which it gained, and 1 in behaviour_tests.rs) cover the Xdnd state machine: bring-up of an `XdndAware` selection window and proxy, kept out of the managed windows; a Wayland drag taking `XdndSelection`, entering an aware window with its types, one `XdndPosition` at a time with the latest one sent after the status, a verdict reported once, the drop, the target's conversion going to the source as `Selection::Dnd`, `XdndFinished` only from the window dropped on, and giving up `XdndSelection` at the end; leaving an accepting window reporting that nothing accepts, a refusing window getting `XdndLeave` on the drop and statuses from other windows ignored; nothing sent to windows that are not aware or speak Xdnd 2, and version 4 spoken; an `XdndProxy` used only when it names itself; more than three types in `XdndTypeList`; an X drag only with an X owner, the proxy mapped above everything and unmapped over X windows and after the drop, every `XdndPosition` answered and a changed verdict sent at once, a move proposal reported, the conversion of `XdndSelection` after the drop, and `XdndFinished` performed or refused; messages from another source ignored, the source leaving, the owner going away ending the drag, and types from `XdndTypeList` with meta targets dropped and versions below 3 refused; and the action and position encodings. In `shingle`: a Wayland drag over xterm is planned as start, motion over no X window and then over xterm, and drop, the X verdict reaches the source as `target` and `action`, an X target's request reaches its `send`, and the drop is performed and finished; a verdict taken back cancels the drop; an X drag keeps the pointer on xterm while it is over the Wayland window, offers itself there only once its types are known, maps the proxy with the Wayland target's verdict, gives the pointer back on the release, drops on `XdndDrop`, hands the target's read to the window manager as `XdndSelection` and finishes with the target's action; an X drag released over an X window ends at once; a refused drop, a drop that does not come within the timeout and an `XdndLeave` after the release end it unperformed; an owner with no button held, or with one held on a Wayland window, starts nothing; the owner going ends the drag; a Wayland drag wins over an X owner until it is dropped, and a dropped one whose source lives on is replaced by the next X drag; and a drag icon is drawn on top as `SurfaceRole::DragIcon`, is not hit by the pointer, takes no keyboard and asks for a redraw when it moves. The harness helpers have 2 more Python unit tests (the pointer path of a drag), now 34.

The `shingle-bin` unit tests (crates/shingle-bin/src/backend.rs, cli.rs, main.rs, headless.rs; 42 tests) run backend selection and the start-up decision with an injected environment: `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, `WAYLAND_SOCKET` and `DISPLAY` each select nested, none (or only empty ones) selects native, `--backend`/`--headless` win over `SHINGLE_BACKEND`, which wins over the display variables, and an unknown `SHINGLE_BACKEND` is an error even inside a session. They cover every option, values after `=`, `--spawn` taking the rest of the line (including a `--help` meant for the program), non-UTF-8 paths, missing values, repeated paths, conflicting backends, unknown backends, options and arguments, their messages and exit statuses, a VT login planning native (with `--allow-builtin-seat`), the "no seat" refusal of SSH logins without `XDG_SEAT` with its reason, SSH inside a host session still nesting, `--help` fitting 80 columns and `--version`, and that a headless `--spawn` child gets the socket and its arguments. None of them starts a backend. DB-2 added 12 (now 55): `offscreen` as a backend that is never selected automatically, its `--help` text and conflicts, the planning of an offscreen start inside a host session, SIGINT and SIGTERM blocked for threads started later, and in crates/shingle-bin/src/offscreen.rs the frame pacer (spacing, a commit after an idle spell drawn at once on the grid, waits ending at the next frame, tick or deadline, no spinning at a zero interval), the reported refresh, the host, errors logged once, and a GPU test that draws at the output size, reports the frame as presented and reads a capture back in logical pixels at scale 2 (skipped without an adapter).

The `shingle-native` unit tests (crates/shingle-native, 204 tests and the presentation probe's 1; 39 of them from NB-6, 33 from MO-7 and 21 from NB-8) never construct `LibSeatSession`, `LibinputSource`, `DrmDevice`, `GbmAllocator` or `GpuRenderer`. The startup decisions run on an injected environment and a scripted `FakeSession`: SSH variables with and without `XDG_SEAT`, the wait for the first enable (an early disable acknowledged, a timeout after 5 s), the backends that need `--allow-builtin-seat` and parsing libseat's "Seat opened" log line. The `Translator` tests cover relative motion clamped to the output and frozen while constrained, absolute and touch positions through the output mapping, touch up at the last position, whole wheel clicks from v120 with fractions kept and dropped on reversal, finger scroll stops and pass-through of keys, buttons and gestures; the settings tests check Wayfire's mouse and touchpad option sets and order, `none` profiles, unconfigured keyboards and touchscreens and the touchpad send-events mode. The registry tests, on a real compositor, check one seat device per kind with libinput's name, vendor and product, hotplug, ignored switches, ids kept across a pause, vanished devices removed after it, per-device held keys and the send-events rule for combined devices. The backend tests (crates/shingle-native/src/host/tests.rs) drive `Backend::step` with a `FakeSession` and a `FakeInput` around a real `Frontend`, compositor, IPC socket and an in-process Wayland client (crates/shingle-native/src/test_client.rs) that records seat events with their times: key, motion, button, axis and touch events arrive with the libinput time in milliseconds and the wheel value unchanged by the compositor; `input/list-devices` follows additions and removals; added devices are configured; configure-device releases a held Shift for the client and the xkb state and sets `Disabled`, drops libinput's late release and restores the mode on enable; a combined device stops only with both kinds disabled; Ctrl+Alt+F2 switches without the client seeing F2, and a refused switch passes it on; a VT switch releases a held key and button, reads nothing while paused, keeps ids and settings on resume and drops the release of a key held across it; vanished and IPC-disabled devices stay so across a switch; Caps Lock lights the LEDs; input of unknown devices and kinds is dropped; and SIGTERM, raised in the test thread, ends the loop, saves the session and drops the backend's hold on the seat. The frontend's `set_session_active` and `keyboard_leds`, and `Compositor::set_device_natural_scroll`, have tests in their crates. The NB-7 tests run the KMS layer on `kms::fake::FakeKms`, an in-memory card that applies commits to its properties, records them, rejects them as scripted and delivers page flips when told; they are listed in the design's [NB-7 as built](design/native-backend.md#nb-7-as-built). In the backend tests, a fake renderer stands in for the GPU, so frames, flips, frame callbacks and `wp_presentation` feedback reach the in-process client without a device. `Compositor::dpms_state` and the `Fatal` exit status have tests in their crates. tools/native_test has 14 Python unit tests for its pure helpers, 7 of them for present_check.py; they create no device and open no socket. NB-9 added 26 tests in `shingle-native` (now 150 and the probe's 1), with a fake hotplug monitor (`hotplug::tests::FakeMonitor`) and EDID blobs and more connectors on the fake card; they never construct `UdevMonitor`. With 9 in `shingle-config`, 2 in `shingle-core`, 10 in `shingle`, 1 in `shingle-nested` and 4 Python tests for tools/output_test, they are listed in the design's [NB-9 as built](design/native-backend.md#nb-9-as-built).

The `shingle-frontend` input-script tests (`crates/shingle-frontend/src/frontend/tests.rs`) run a `Frontend` around a compositor on a socket in the temporary directory, with an in-process Wayland client mapping `wl_shm` toplevels and a `FakeHost` that records redraw requests, warps, VT switches, device changes and exit. They feed input through `handle_input` and `process` as a host does and cover Scale (keys, pointer hover and click, touch, cancel restoring focus), Expo (keys and a pointer click), both switchers (dimming while held, focus and opacity on release), a move drag that drops one tile onto another, a workspace switch with its transition, key repeat at the configured rate (and no repeat for modifiers or after release), `Reset`, the Ctrl+Alt+F2 VT switch, the zoom wheel, disabling the pointer over IPC and the quit binding. STIPC tests send `stipc/feed_key` over the real IPC socket and check that a YAML key binding opens Expo, keys then reach Expo, no modifier stays held, a `super+xkb:z` command runs from physical Y with a `de` layout, an IPC binding fires exactly once, and keys are dropped while the session is inactive; all but the IPC binding failed on `master` at `9a2663c`. A scene test records the tiles drawn into an `OffscreenRenderer` and checks the tiled windows, then the Scale thumbnails with a title inside each.
