Two more draw through a target that takes 400 ms per frame, longer than the 300 ms animation, and require the last frame drawn to show the settled state: the window at full size and alpha after the zoom open animation, and the old workspace fully off screen after a workspace slide. Both failed on `master` at `9a2663c`, which ended each animation on its first, intermediate frame. DB-2 added a screencopy test with an in-process screencopy client: no answer before a frame, one capture draw at the output size, the background in sRGB, and the inverted background after inversion; and two tests of the crop of a capture region, inside and outside the frame. The scene tests are skipped without a GPU adapter. `KeyRepeat` has its own unit tests.

`shingle-render` has 29 unit tests, 8 offscreen pixel tests (`crates/shingle-render/tests/offscreen_pixels.rs`) and 4 DMA-BUF import tests with 3 ignored experiments (`crates/shingle-render/tests/dmabuf_import.rs`). DB-1 added 14 unit tests: the fourcc table and its byte order, `makedev`, modifier names, the caps filter on recorded modifier lists (single plane, filterable sampling, `INVALID` left out, refused imports, the UNORM fallback, render needing colour attachment and blending, no modifiers at all), the import checks (format, modifier per use, size, extents, the opaque and sRGB-decode flags), `ForeignAccess`'s acquire and release barriers, layouts, queue families, access masks and stages for sampled and render images, and a GPU test of the shader's opaque and sRGB-decode flags. The import tests and their results on this host are under [With real clients](#with-real-clients); they skip without an adapter that can import. The pixel tests render fixed scenes (solid, translucent, textured, UV-cropped, transformed and rotated tiles; scale, output transform and zoom; gamma ramp and both inversion modes; nearest filtering) and compare the readback with reference images in `crates/shingle-render/tests/golden`, which were rendered by the renderer before the NB-1 split. They need a wgpu adapter and are skipped without one; set `SHINGLE_RENDER_BLESS=1` to regenerate the references. DB-2 added a unit test of the capture state (scale 1, no output transform, zoom and filter kept), now 30.

The frame-capture tests (DB-2; crates/shingle/src/screencopy.rs, 4, with a screencopy client in the in-process test client) cover the CPU composite answering at once without frame capture; with it, no answer before `finish_capture`, the region in `Event::CaptureRequested`, RGBA written as ARGB8888 and a second answer ignored; `failed` for an error or pixels of the wrong size; and nothing for a destroyed frame, `failed` for a destroyed buffer. One more in crates/shingle/src/lib.rs covers `output_physical_size` with scale 2 and `monotonic_time`. The Wayland probe's pattern has 1 test, and the GPU harness 13 Python unit tests in tools/gpu_test/test_checks.py (safe backends, colour names, the quadrant points, matching, flipped and shifted patterns, the background, the configs and the summaries), beside test_run.py's 11.

The buffer-lifetime tests (DB-3; crates/shingle/src/buffer_lock.rs, 10, and crates/shingle/src/commit_queue.rs, 6, with `wl_buffer.release` recorded by the in-process test client) cover a buffer replaced behind a FIFO barrier, behind a commit-timing deadline and in a synchronized subsurface's cache, each released (all three, and a waiting buffer of a destroyed surface, were first written as failing tests against the code before DB-3); shm and single-pixel buffers released when their commit applies; a buffer replaced by `attach` before its commit released at once, and one attached twice locked once; one buffer committed twice behind a barrier released once; no release for a buffer the client destroyed while it waited; and the client's newer pending state kept across a FIFO frame and a parent's commit. The commit queue's tests use a fake blocker source: an unblocked commit applies at once; blocked commits wait, a later one ready first still waits, and all apply in order unmerged, each released; processing stops at the first blocked entry; frame callbacks wait with their commit; state set after a queued commit stays pending; and a destroyed surface drops its queue and releases its buffers. One more in crates/shingle/src/lib.rs covers `BufferSnapshot::rgba` and its unchanged `Debug` output, on which wayland_pins.txt depends; the pin test passes without re-blessing. The GPU harness gained 2 Python unit tests (the release lines, and every check group having a check), now 15.

The linux-dmabuf tests (DB-4; crates/shingle/src/linux_dmabuf/tests.rs, 21, with a fake `DmabufImporter`, memfds as DMA-BUFs and a pipe as a pending fence, and linux-dmabuf in the in-process test client) cover no global without an importer, version 5 once enabled, an empty feedback refused and a second `enable_dmabuf` keeping one global; the format table's layout, seals and read-only descriptor and the one-tranche default and surface feedback; version 3's `format` and `modifier` events; every params error (`plane_idx`, `plane_set`, mixed modifiers, no planes, a gap, two planes for XRGB8888, unknown flags, non-positive sizes, an unadvertised modifier or format, out of bounds, used params); `created` with the import's size, format, modifier, offset and stride; `failed` for an import error, y-invert and a size beyond the modifier's extent, and for an unadvertised pair below version 4; `invalid_wl_buffer` for a failed `create_immed`; a DMA-BUF shown without copying, kept while a snapshot locks it and released when replaced; committed events carrying it; a commit waiting for its fence with a later shm commit behind it, both applied in order once the fence signals; no wait for a signalled fence; a destroyed surface dropping its wait and releasing the buffer; and a destroyed `wl_buffer` keeping the shown DMA-BUF. The frontend's tests (crates/shingle-frontend/src/dmabuf/tests.rs, 6, and 3 in frontend/tests.rs, with a fake importer whose texture lives on the recording target's device) cover the feedback decision (`SHINGLE_DMABUF=0`, no import, no render node, no modifiers, every sampling pair); one import per buffer and later use from the cache, pruning once the buffer is gone, a filter change importing again with the new filter, a failed import tried once per buffer until a GPU loss; a frame that samples a DMA-BUF keeping it from its client until the GPU has finished; import again after `drop_gpu_resources`; and a surface going from a DMA-BUF to shm getting an uploaded texture. The GPU harness gained 9 Python unit tests in tools/gpu_test/test_dmabuf_checks.py (wayland-info parsing, the trace and its summary, drawn, changed and coloured shares, the Firefox profile and its private-HOME guard), and test_checks.py checks the groups of both modules.

The shm upload tests (DB-6, 23) cover, in crates/shingle/src/buffer.rs (6), that only `F_SEAL_SHRINK` counts as sealed, a sealed pool mapped on its first read and mapped again at its new size after a resize, an unsealed pool mapped per read and refused after the client truncated it, a read covering the buffer from its first pixel with its stride, the conversion of all four formats and the DRM fourccs with the opaque X formats; in buffer_lock.rs (3) an shm commit with upload on keeping its buffer until `ShmContent::release`, converting on demand only while locked, a commit without a new buffer keeping the released content, a replaced buffer waiting for its upload, and copies without upload and for icons; in shell.rs (1) the session-lock check on locked, released and copied content. crates/shingle-render/src/upload.rs (3) counts the bytes of rectangles with every stride and refuses bad images, rectangles and formats; the pixel tests (2) draw shm uploads of all four formats with a padded stride and alpha 0 in the X formats, as an RGBA upload draws, and a damaged rectangle, a rebind and a GPU copy. crates/shingle-frontend/src/shm.rs (4) checks the upload rectangles: full for a new texture, clipped damage, damage covering the area as one write and many rectangles as their bounding box; the frontend tests (4) upload a new buffer in full and then a 5×5 damage as 100 bytes, release each before any frame, upload nothing for a commit without a buffer, keep the texture through a filter change and while the surface is on another workspace, drop it once unmapped, and upload a missed commit in full at the next frame. `wl_probe` parses `--damage` and `--animate`; the GPU harness has 6 more Python unit tests (upload sizes from the trace, the damage upload, CPU seconds from `/proc/PID/stat`, view points, probe events and identical captures), now 46 with test_run.py's.

The syncobj tests (DB-5; crates/shingle/src/linux_drm_syncobj/tests.rs, 17, with a fake `SyncobjDevice` whose timelines are signalled by the test and wake real eventfds, so waits go through the event loop, and the syncobj protocol in the in-process test client) cover no global without linux-dmabuf, version 1 and one global after a second enable; `surface_exists`, a new syncobj object after the old one's destroy, and `invalid_timeline`; the commit rules in wlroots' order as a table and every error through the protocol (`no_buffer` for an acquire or release point without a buffer and with a null attach, `no_acquire_point`, `no_release_point`, `conflicting_points` for equal points on one timeline, `unsupported_buffer` for shm, `no_surface` for either point on a destroyed surface); a destroyed syncobj object discarding its points; a signalled acquire point applying at once; an unsignalled one waiting with a ready commit queued behind it, points above 32 bits, both applied in order once the eventfd fires, the replaced buffer released with its release point signalled; one buffer committed twice signalling both release points when it is released; a stalled surface not holding up another surface and releasing both buffers and signalling both points when destroyed; an acquire point replacing the implicit wait on a pipe that never polls readable; and `no_memory` when the eventfd cannot be set up (as a protocol error with wayland-client's Rust backend, as `ENOMEM` with libwayland, which `cargo test --workspace` links through winit). crates/shingle/src/linux_drm_syncobj/timeline.rs has 5: `dev_t` numbers, only `dri/renderD*` accepted from a uevent, a missing device, and on the device a timeline imported from a client's syncobj fd that reads unsignalled until signalled, wakes an eventfd registered before its point had a fence, and is signalled by the compositor for the client to see, plus a memfd refused as a timeline; both device tests ran on this host's `renderD128` and skip without it. The frontend has 1 for `SHINGLE_SYNCOBJ`, and the stall probe 4 for its options, refusing a card node, its fills and its JSON. The GPU harness gained 5 Python unit tests (the syncobj global, trace lines and summary, judging syncobj use in both modes, the modes, prefixed results and probe events), now 40 with test_run.py's.

The FIFO and commit-timing queue (found during DB-4; crates/shingle/src/buffer_lock.rs and commit_timing.rs) is covered by: updates behind a FIFO barrier queued unmerged and holding their buffers, then applied in order and each released at the next frame; updates that set the barrier again shown one per frame; an update behind one waiting for its timestamp waiting behind it, both released when the surface goes; the deadline one refresh and 500 µs before the timestamp; and a burst of updates each timed 40 ms ahead of the clock, committed every 10 ms with a frame after each, which must show its first update and then every later one in order. Before the change that test failed with nothing shown at all; DB-3's two tests that expected a merge now expect the queue. The DMA-BUF cache tests share one GPU device: with one each, the frontend test binary crashed with SIGSEGV in 3 of 15 runs on NVIDIA, and with the shared one it passed 20 of 20.

The flaky `a_hotplug_during_a_pending_flip_waits_for_the_flip` (crates/shingle-native/src/host/tests.rs) is fixed: the native host tests stepped the backend at the real time, and a pending page flip times out after 1 s, so under load the recovery modeset forgot the flip before the test's hotplug step and the new output appeared early ("not yet"). A 1.1 s sleep between the steps reproduced it. `Backend::step_at` takes the time, and the fixture steps at one that stands still.

`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` passes (2026-11-26).

The input-device unit tests (`crates/shingle/src/input_device.rs`, `crates/shingle/src/ipc.rs`, `crates/shingle-nested/src/lib.rs`) cover IPC dispatch, the method inventory (83 since MO-4), field validation and error text, gating of keys, modifiers, motion, buttons, gestures and touch, release of held input on disable, STIPC binding dispatch and host-event classification. Registry tests cover ID allocation without reuse, vendor and product IDs in `input/list-devices`, two keyboards with independent holds (including a key both hold and modifiers kept while the other keyboard holds keys), removing devices while keys and buttons are held, configure-device on unknown and removed IDs, input from unknown or wrongly kinded devices, gestures owned by one of two pointers, and STIPC's choice of device. These run without a Wayland client, so they check compositor state, not delivered protocol events. Two nested-backend fixture tests run a compositor whose sockets are in the temporary directory (`Compositor::with_socket_path`), register nested's devices as `App::new` does and send `input/list-devices` and `input/configure-device` over the real IPC socket; the `list-devices` reply must match, byte for byte, the reply recorded from `master` at `7189b50`.

The `shingle-core` unit tests (`crates/shingle-core/src/tree.rs`, `core.rs`, `session.rs`, `geometry.rs`) pin exact tile order and slot geometry through insertion, drops, swaps and removal; split weights through resizing, minimum extents and floating or minimized windows; focus fallback, directional focus and stacking; floating, snapping, maximized and gap geometry; and placeholder reconciliation by launch identity. Session tests round-trip a two-output model with nested, unevenly weighted splits and every persisted flag through JSON and atomic file saves, and require tile order, sizes, state, focus and stacking to come back exactly, after repeated cycles and after reconciliation. They also cover version, unknown-field and malformed-snapshot errors. They exercise the model only; full frontend/session cycles with real clients are still needed.

The backend host API tests (`crates/shingle/src/lib.rs`, `input.rs`, `input_device.rs`, `presentation.rs`, `cursor_shape.rs`, `clock.rs`, `ipc.rs`) cover `shutdown_requested` after `request_quit` and after raising SIGTERM and SIGINT in the test thread; the event-loop descriptor becoming readable for a connecting client and for the tick, and not staying readable after dispatch; backend input timestamps and their precedence; release of all held input on session pause, dropped input and STIPC bindings while paused, and unchanged IPC device state; and the default cursor's visibility rules. Several use an in-process `wayland-client` test client (crates/shingle/src/test_client.rs) on a socket in the temporary directory, so they check what a client actually receives: `HEADLESS-1`, 310x175 mm and 60 Hz without output info; a described output's name, description, geometry, subpixel and refresh in `wl_output`, xdg-output and output-management heads and modes; updates to bound outputs without a second `name` event; `frame_presented` timestamps, refresh, sequence and flags in `wp_presentation_feedback.presented` and the frame callback time; and frames withheld while the session is inactive.

The map-blocking and keymap tests (`crates/shingle/src/shell.rs`, `input.rs`, `crates/shingle-core/src/tree.rs`, `core.rs`, `session.rs`) drive the in-process test client with xdg toplevels, shm buffers and a `view-pre-map` subscriber. A blocked new view is absent from `simple-tile/get-layout` from creation until `unblock-map`, also when the subscriber arrives after its app ID; its neighbour keeps its configured size; it still answers `view-info`; and on release it joins the layout last. A second client with the blocked view's app ID gets its own window. A blocked view outside `tile_by_default` floats at its buffer size. A blocked client claiming a restored placeholder keeps the placeholder's slot throughout. In the model, a held leaf takes no space or focus, `release_held` appends it, `reconcile_mapped` skips it and sessions do not persist the flag. The keymaps a client receives on `wl_keyboard`, after a virtual-keyboard upload, on an input-method v2 grab and after `set_keymap` with `us,de` contain no `include` and compile with libxkbcommon without include paths. The five protocol tests failed on `master` at `efc0846`.

Writing these tests found three layout bugs, now fixed: splitting a tile across the axis reset its size to an even share; resizing treated floating and minimized siblings as occupying space, so the moved edge and amount were wrong; and directional focus ranked by centre distance, so focus-down from a top-right tile could jump to a full-height left tile. Directional focus now picks the tile across the crossed edge's midpoint, as Wayfire's tile plugin does.

### Soak tests

tools/soak_test/run.py ([development.md](development.md#build-test-and-lint)) runs on this host (20 cores, NVIDIA driver 590.48.01) inside tools/gpu_test/run.py's namespace, at niceness 19, with the debug build and the Vulkan validation layer disabled. A cycle (seed 1) maps three `wl_probe` windows and floats, resizes, maximizes, makes fullscreen, minimizes, raises and moves them between workspaces over IPC and closes one with `close-view`; maps, retitles, resizes and closes two `xw_probe` windows through lazy Xwayland; copies 64 bytes to 300 kB between `wl-copy`, `wl-paste` and `xclip` both ways; drags text into a `wl_probe --drop` from a `wl_probe --drag` or, every other cycle, an `xw_probe --xdnd-source`; adds an output, moves the long-lived anchor window onto it and removes it; and, where there is a frontend, runs `vkcube` for 240 frames, 120 frames of `wl_probe --animate --presentation` for frame latency, a `grim` capture and Scale and Expo. A cycle starts at most every 15 s. Samples are taken once a minute when only the anchor is mapped. Measured on 2026-11-26 and 2026-11-27:

| run | cycles | RSS of Shingle at 0, 5 min and the end | fds | Shingle CPU | whole namespace | frame latency, commit to presentation | result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| offscreen, 120 min (before the frame-callback fix) | 478 | 217.3, 292.1, 294.5 MB (291.4 to 294.5 after minute 5) | 56 to 57 | 11.1 s a minute | 0.37 cores | median of per-minute medians 4.5 ms, highest per-minute p99 19.9 ms, highest 22.3 ms; 57218 frames, none discarded | pass |
| headless, 30 min | 120 | 31.3, 38.3, 38.0 MB (at most 41.0) | 19 | 4.1 s a minute | 0.19 cores | (no frontend) | pass |
| nested in offscreen, 30 min (before the fix) | 113 | nested 287.7, 363.3, 371.6 MB; outer 221.2, 222.6, 225.6 MB | 73 to 74; 57 to 58 | nested 8.4, outer 12.4 s a minute | 0.52 cores | 17.7 ms, 44.4 ms, 53.7 ms; 13530 frames | fail: frame callbacks 5 to 26, GPU memory 147 to 211 MiB |
| nested in offscreen, 30 min (after the fix) | 52 | nested 221.3, 301.1, 303.9 MB; outer 220.7, 223.2, 225.8 MB | 73; 57 | nested 42.2, outer 4.5 s a minute | 0.86 cores | 144.6 ms, 400.1 ms, 815.7 ms; 6188 frames | pass |

In every run the threads stayed constant (7 for offscreen and nested, 1 for headless), and so did every `stipc/object_counts` count after the five-minute warm-up, apart from the frame callbacks noted. GPU memory, from `nvidia-smi` by host PID, stayed at 197 to 198 MiB for offscreen Shingle and the outer Shingle and 205 to 213 MiB for nested after the fix. The offscreen run saved its session 121 times, each file valid JSON, and paused 30 s for load (at most 10.45); for its first hour the headless and then the first nested run shared the host with it. The 1-minute load stayed below 12 except for a moment in the first nested run (12.11, 27 s paused).

Found and fixed (each with a regression test that fails without the fix):

- Decoration objects of clients that exit without destroying them stayed in the compositor's map for as long as Shingle ran, two a cycle (`a_decoration_its_client_never_destroyed_goes_with_the_client`, crates/shingle/src/decorator/tests.rs).
- Frame callbacks of a surface that was hidden when it was destroyed stayed in the compositor's list, and every frame walked it again: 3 more over the two-hour offscreen run, 21 more over the first 30-minute nested run, none in the run after the fix (`frame_callbacks_of_hidden_surfaces_go_with_the_surface`, crates/shingle/src/object_counts.rs).

Found and outside Shingle:

- `wl_probe` never destroyed the buffer it drew for each configure, so a long-lived probe left about 80 buffers and as many pool fds in Shingle each cycle (fds 57 to 1130 in three minutes); the probe now destroys them once released. That is a client leak that Shingle has to keep until the client disconnects.
- With the validation layer that debug builds load through wgpu, offscreen Shingle's RSS grew by about 1.1 MiB a minute (317.8 MB at 5 minutes to 340.2 MB at 25), all of it in the main heap (21.4 to 30.7 MB in ten minutes by smaps), and it used 12 to 13 s of CPU a minute; a 12-minute run of the same build with the layer disabled went from 361.1 MB at 6 minutes to 361.8 MB at 12, at 7.3 s a minute. The harness now disables the layer unless `--validation` is given.

After the fix and a rebase onto master the other harnesses passed again: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 239/239, tools/output_test/run.py 118/118, tools/session_test/run.py --checks slow round-trip 27/27 and tools/gpu_test/run.py with every group 283/283.

Open: the second nested run cycled at less than half the rate of the first and its nested Shingle used five times the CPU, with a latency near 145 ms, mostly in shm uploads into wgpu's staging buffers (by gdb backtraces); a five-minute run of the build without the fix measured the same (42.2 s a minute, 144.7 ms), so the fix did not cause it, and the GPU was at PCIe generation 1 and 210 MHz during that run. Found on 2026-11-27: the uploads went into wgpu's staging memory in the PCIe BAR, which was slow to write while the BAR was crowded, whatever the clocks; they are now staged in system memory ([shm upload speed](#shm-upload-speed)). The single 64 MiB step of nested's GPU memory in the first nested run, at minute 18, did not come back in the second. The native soak is step 53 of [native-testing.md](native-testing.md) and has not been run.

### shm upload speed

Measured on 2026-11-27 on this host (RTX 3060, NVIDIA driver 590.48.01, 246 MiB PCIe BAR heap, no resizable BAR) with debug builds, the Vulkan validation layer disabled, at niceness 19, inside tools/gpu_test/run.py's namespace. tools/shm_bench/run.py ([development.md](development.md#build-test-and-lint)) times `wl_probe --animate 120 --presentation --size WxH [--damage-square 64]` from `wl_surface.commit` to `wp_presentation`, with the upload's own time from the buffer trace. `write_texture` is the upload as DB-6 built it, `Queue::write_texture`, selected in the same build by `SHINGLE_SHM_STAGING=0`; `staging` is the fix, staging buffers in system memory (crates/shingle-render/src/staging.rs); `staging (BAR)` is the fix's code with its buffers in the BAR (`SHINGLE_SHM_STAGING=device-local`). Nested is nested Shingle inside offscreen Shingle, both on the GPU; its output is 1272x682, so larger buffers are cut off but uploaded whole.

| mode | case | latency p50, write_texture | staging | staging (BAR) | upload p50, write_texture | staging | Shingle CPU per frame, write_texture | staging |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| nested | 256x256 full | 36.8 ms | 19.6 ms | 19.9 ms | 20.3 ms | 0.81 ms | 21.7 ms | 5.1 ms |
| nested | 1280x720 full | 290.1 ms | 18.6 ms | 19.2 ms | 273.9 ms | 1.61 ms | 282.0 ms | 5.5 ms |
| nested | 1280x720, 64x64 damage | 18.5 ms | 19.3 ms | 19.5 ms | 0.44 ms | 0.78 ms | 6.8 ms | 5.0 ms |
| nested | 1920x1080 full | 661.9 ms | 17.5 ms | 17.8 ms | 646.6 ms | 2.61 ms | 647.3 ms | 6.3 ms |
| nested | 1920x1080, 64x64 damage | 17.4 ms | 18.6 ms | 16.4 ms | 0.44 ms | 0.82 ms | 9.8 ms | 5.4 ms |
| offscreen | 256x256 full | 2.9 ms | 3.4 ms | 3.3 ms | 0.28 ms | 0.66 ms | 3.6 ms | 4.5 ms |
| offscreen | 1280x720 full | 4.0 ms | 4.5 ms | 4.7 ms | 0.85 ms | 1.46 ms | 4.4 ms | 5.4 ms |
| offscreen | 1280x720, 64x64 damage | 3.8 ms | 4.3 ms | 3.9 ms | 0.27 ms | 0.64 ms | 3.7 ms | 4.4 ms |
| offscreen | 1920x1080 full | 8.1 ms | 6.4 ms | 6.8 ms | 1.79 ms | 2.49 ms | 6.7 ms | 6.5 ms |
| offscreen | 1920x1080, 64x64 damage | 3.9 ms | 4.1 ms | 3.9 ms | 0.29 ms | 0.65 ms | 4.2 ms | 4.2 ms |

Where the time went: in nested Shingle with `write_texture`, the full-buffer cases' time is the upload, and the upload is the `memcpy` in wgpu-core's `StagingBuffer::write` (16 of 16 gdb samples of nested Shingle's main thread during a 1272x682 animation), at about 13 MB/s, linear in the bytes (262 144 bytes in 20 ms, 3.7 MB in 274 ms, 8.3 MB in 647 ms). wgpu allocates that staging buffer for each call from gpu-allocator's `CpuToGpu` location, which prefers device-local, host-visible memory, the BAR, in blocks of 64 MiB and more ([dmabuf.md](design/dmabuf.md#shm-staging-as-built)). `nvidia-smi` showed 42 MiB of the BAR in use by the desktop, 107 MiB with offscreen Shingle running and 172 MiB with the outer and the nested Shingle. Small damage (64x64, 16 KiB) stays cheap, and so do the full writes of offscreen Shingle alone. With temporary builds that are not on the branch, the fix's buffers forced to 64 MiB in the BAR were as slow (1280x720 full: 270 ms in nested, 288 ms in offscreen, with 236 and 171 MiB of the BAR in use), the same buffers in system memory were not (1.6 and 1.4 ms), and a wgpu `MAP_WRITE` buffer made for each upload, as `write_texture` makes one, was slow too (554 ms). So the slow memory is BAR memory that gpu-allocator's large blocks get once the BAR is crowded; why the driver makes those writes slow is not known, and a cause outside Shingle may be part of it, but Shingle chose that memory.

Not the GPU's clocks: the slow rows ran with the GPU anywhere from P8 at 210 MHz and PCIe generation 1 to P0 at 1837 MHz and generation 4, and the fast rows likewise (the per-row state is in the benchmark's results.md). `staging_probe` (crates/shingle-render/examples), run before and after the cases, wrote a fresh 4 MiB allocation at 36 GB/s in system memory (type 3), 27 to 31 GB/s in cached system memory (type 4) and 10.4 GB/s in the BAR (type 5), so the BAR is not slow as such.

What the fix costs: in offscreen Shingle, where `write_texture` was fast, each upload takes about 0.35 ms more (a command buffer and a submission of its own instead of wgpu's pending writes), and latency is the same within the run's spread. Commit-to-presentation latency in nested Shingle is about 18 ms whatever the upload, one frame of the outer Shingle more than offscreen.

The soak's slow second nested run (42 s of CPU a minute, 145 ms latency p50, [Soak tests](#soak-tests)) matches the `write_texture` rows: its latency probe drew full frames of its tile. Why the first nested run was fast is not known; it ran beside the offscreen soak, and the BAR in use then was not recorded.

Resize pacing (`--checks resize resize-nested`, tools/gpu_test/resize_checks.py): kitty, on Mesa's EGL over `wl_shm` in the namespace, tiled beside a `wl_probe`, its boundary dragged 300 pixels over 40 frames with one and then eight STIPC motions per frame. In offscreen Shingle kitty got 39 or 40 configures, one per frame, and drew 25 to 28 sizes during each drag and its final size 2 to 52 ms after the release, with either upload. In nested Shingle with `write_texture` each 40-frame drag took 9.6 and 10.0 s instead of 1.2 s, since every new size was a full upload that held up nested's loop and the IPC replies, and kitty drew its final size 491 and 496 ms after the release: the operator's slow redraw after a resize. With the staging buffers: 1.2 and 1.3 s, 40 configures, 34 and 38 sizes drawn during the drags, the final size 28 and 2 ms after the release. Configures and frame callbacks keep up with the drag, one configure per frame, as under Wayfire; what was slow was the uploads.

Soak (tools/soak_test/run.py `--mode nested --minutes 12`, the full cycle, seed 1), one run after the other on the same build: with `SHINGLE_SHM_STAGING=0`, 21 cycles, nested Shingle at 42.3 s of CPU a minute (median), the outer one at 4.5 s, frame latency 144.5 ms (median of the per-minute medians, highest per-minute p99 155.2 ms), 2499 frames: the soak's second nested run again. With the staging buffers: 46 cycles, nested at 13.5 s a minute, the outer one at 12.3 s (it draws nested's frames, now more of them), latency 19.2 ms (p99 at most 35.7 ms), 5505 frames, none discarded. Both passed the trend test, with fds, threads and GPU memory flat (nested 74 fds, 7 threads, 211 MiB).

The other harnesses with this branch's build: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 239/239, tools/output_test/run.py 118/118, tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip` 27/27 (worst reply 16.5 ms), and tools/gpu_test/run.py with every group, `resize-nested` included, 295/295, no device opened outside the allowed set; its `shm` group recorded 600 frames of 1280x720 in 12.72 s with offscreen Shingle at 5.02 ms of CPU a frame.

### Applications

The application compatibility sweep (`nice -n 19 tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks apps`, tools/gpu_test/app_checks.py, [development.md](development.md#build-test-and-lint)) ran every installed application against offscreen Shingle on this host's NVIDIA GPU on 2026-11-27, debug build, each with a private HOME and fresh profile, a private D-Bus session bus, no network and two outputs: 231 of 233 checks passed in the full run, and GIMP's run after its fix passed all 15. The two failures are recorded below; everything else in the table passed. `ok` is a pass, `FAIL` a failure, `n/a` a step the application has no way to do (a terminal copies only a mouse selection; mpv and kitty have no menu on a click), and an empty cell a step the scenario does not have for it.

| App | Toolkit, as run | maps | tiles | draws | focus | typing | popup | dialog | copy | paste | resize (tiler) | resize (drag) | fullscreen | workspace | output | close | protocol | crash |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| kitty | Wayland, GLFW | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | n/a | n/a | n/a | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| konsole | Wayland, Qt 6 | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | n/a | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| kate | Wayland, Qt 6 | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok (asks to save) | ok | ok |
| dolphin | Wayland, Qt 6 | ok | ok | ok | ok | n/a | ok | ok | n/a | n/a | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| okular | Wayland, Qt 6 | ok | ok | ok | ok | n/a | ok | ok | n/a | n/a | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| evince | Wayland, GTK 3 | ok | ok | ok | ok | n/a | ok | ok | n/a | n/a | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| mpv | Wayland | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok (`f`) | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| vlc | X11, Qt 5 | ok | ok | ok | ok | n/a | ok | FAIL (app) | n/a | n/a | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| gimp | Wayland, GTK 3 | ok | ok | ok | ok | n/a | ok | ok | n/a | n/a | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| inkscape | Wayland, GTK 3 | ok | ok | ok | ok | n/a | ok | ok | n/a | n/a | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| libreoffice | X11, VCL | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| firefox | Wayland, GTK 3 | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| chromium | Wayland | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| google-chrome | Wayland | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| rofi | X11, override-redirect | ok | ok (not managed) | ok | ok | ok (runs the command) |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  | ok |  | ok |
| dunst | Wayland, layer shell | ok (owns the bus name) |  | ok (notification shown) |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  | ok (gone after 3 s) | ok | ok |

What each column checks: `maps`, a view within 90 s (180 s for the heavy ones); `tiles`, the view fills the first output's workarea inside the gaps; `draws`, the capture of the view is not one colour, Shingle's background does not show along its edges and nothing is drawn in the gaps; `focus`, the new window has the keyboard without being asked; `typing`, typed text has its effect (a shell command a terminal runs, text an editor copies back to `wl-paste`, a `data:` URL that paints a browser's page red, mpv's `f`); `popup`, a right click opens a menu (an xdg popup, a GTK 3 popover subsurface, or for X clients an override-redirect window seen in captures) and Escape closes it; `dialog`, the application's open or settings shortcut opens a child view that floats centred on the main window, is drawn, takes the focus, closes with Escape and gives the focus back; `copy` and `paste`, with `wl-paste` and `wl-copy`; `resize`, the application redraws at its new size when the tiler halves it beside a `wl_probe` and when a Super+right drag moves the boundary, and the probe stays visible; `fullscreen`, over the whole output and back to its tile; `workspace`, gone from the first workspace and drawn on the second, then back; `output`, drawn on the second output at its size, then back; `close`, `close-view` ends it or it asks first; `protocol`, no `wl_display.error` in its `WAYLAND_DEBUG` log; `crash`, no crash and no Shingle panic.

Shingle bugs the sweep found, each fixed with a regression test:

- xdg dialogs tiled: Konsole's settings window took a tile and resized its parent, because Wayfire's `can_tile_view` rule (a view with a parent or a fixed size floats) was applied to X toplevels only. xdg dialogs now float centred on their parent at their own size, and their configures leave the size to them until they draw (crates/shingle/src/shell.rs, `can_tile_xdg`, `chooses_own_size`; crates/shingle/src/placement.rs).
- `xdg_surface.set_window_geometry` was ignored: GTK's buffers, which carry 45-pixel shadows around the configured size, were squeezed into the tile, so Evince's window came out smaller and offset, and pointer coordinates were off. The window geometry is now double-buffered surface state and places the surface (crates/shingle/src/shell.rs, `surface_rect`).
- Input regions were read at the commit, not when set: GTK destroys the `wl_region` before committing, so its windows had an empty input region and took no pointer input at all (crates/shingle/src/region.rs).
- A tiled window's client maximize request maximized it over the workarea: Inkscape asks for it at start, and a window tiled beside it was then drawn over it. Tiled windows now ignore the request, as simple-tile does.
- `wl_data_device.set_selection` with a source used before raised `used_source`: GTK sets its source again after offering `SAVE_TARGETS`, so every copy in Firefox ended Firefox with a protocol error. Shingle now accepts it, as wlroots does.

Protocols Wayfire offers that the applications use and Shingle lacked, now implemented with tests: `zxdg_exporter_v1`/`zxdg_importer_v1` (GTK 3's xdg-foreign, used with portal file choosers; crates/shingle/src/foreign.rs) and `org_kde_kwin_server_decoration_manager` (crates/shingle/src/server_decoration.rs).

What applications do themselves, recorded rather than worked around:

- VLC 3 runs its Qt interface through Xwayland, and its file dialog's `WM_TRANSIENT_FOR` names Qt's hidden client-leader window (0x400008, also its `WM_CLIENT_LEADER`), not the main window, so the dialog has no parent and tiles. Wayfire takes the parent from the same property through wlroots and tiles it too.
- LibreOffice runs through Xwayland here (its Qt 6 and KF6 plugins are installed); its dialogs are transient for the main window and float centred on it. Its autocorrect capitalizes the typed sentence.
- Inkscape 1.4 sets a minimum size of 1154x752, taller than a 720-pixel output, so it is drawn scaled into a 672-pixel tile; its canvas menu is a GTK 3 popover, a subsurface.
- GTK 3's file chooser asks for 1203x955 and is clamped into the workarea, as Wayfire's `wf::clamp` does. GTK 3 windows draw their translucent shadows into the gaps.
- Chrome opens a tab of its own (an update notice) a few seconds after it starts; kitty without `confirm_os_window_close=0` asks before closing a window with a running shell; GIMP 3 sometimes shows a welcome dialog before its image window; Kate asks to save the modified document on close.
- Steam was not run: it needs the network to install its runtime and an account to log in, which the sweep never has.

After the fixes the other harnesses still pass (2026-11-27, debug build): tools/xwayland_test/run.py 239 of 239 checks, tools/output_test/run.py 121 of 121, tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip` 27 of 27, tools/gpu_test/run.py with every group but the sweep's 332 of 332 (`screencast` run again on its own after `fetch_screencast_deps.py`, which this workspace had not run), and a 6-minute nested soak (tools/soak_test/run.py `--mode nested`) with no growth flagged, no panic and no protocol error.

Views on a workspace other than the current one reported their geometry in their own workspace's coordinates, where Wayfire's `geometry` is relative to the current workspace (found by the workspace step). Since `shingle-ipc-workspace-coords` IPC follows Wayfire: still in layout coordinates, a view's rectangle is offset by one output width or height per workspace from its set's current one, a switch sends `view-geometry-changed` for every view it moves, and `configure-view` with a rectangle on another workspace moves the view there ([ipc.md](ipc.md#views-on-other-workspaces)).

Real-client results of that branch (2026-11-27, debug build): the sweep's workspace step now also requires the main view's IPC geometry one output width right while it is on the second workspace and the same rectangle less that width once that workspace is current; `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks apps` passed 240 of 240 checks, every application's workspace step with it. tools/xwayland_test/run.py 239 of 239, tools/output_test/run.py 121 of 121, tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip` 27 of 27, `--checks round-trip --master-binary` with a build of `99916cc` (the last `master` writing version 1 sessions, which reports the old coordinates; the round trip now compares its views and layouts with the branch's in each view's own workspace) 27 of 27, and tools/gpu_test/run.py with every group but the sweep's 332 of 332, no device opened outside the allowed set. With a build of the current `master` (`d902349`), which writes version 2, the version 1 run fails only its check that `master` saved version 1.

Real-client results of `shingle-workspace-followups` (2026-11-27, debug build): tools/output_test/run.py 121 of 121 (MO-4's `configure-view` checks now expect the fullscreen and floating windows on HEADLESS-2 at 0,0 and 80,53 of that output), tools/xwayland_test/run.py 239 of 239, tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip --master-binary` with a build of the current `master` (`9b1fb8b`, which writes version 2) 37 of 37, the master group now accepting a session of either version, and tools/gpu_test/run.py with every group but the sweep's 332 of 332 after `fetch_screencast_deps.py` (the `outputs` group configures and compares its window on HEADLESS-2 in that output's plane and the drag's result there, `nested-outputs` matches each nested view's pattern from its output window's corner), no device opened outside the allowed set. `--checks apps` passed 228 of 229: the sweep now places the main view's rectangle in the layout by its output, so the output step judges the second output's capture where the view is; GIMP failed `maps` when it showed its welcome dialog instead of opening the image and quit when the dialog was closed. That is GIMP's own: it showed the dialog in 6 of 10 runs of `--checks app-gimp` with this branch and 1 of 7 with the `master` build, and each run without it passed all 15 of GIMP's checks with this branch (with `master` the output step fails under the new sweep, which expects output-relative rectangles).

### With real clients

Before the rename, real XDG clients and Unix-socket IPC fixtures exercised mapping, geometry, workspaces, XKB layout switching (`us,de`), runtime bindings and event delivery. Plugin activation events were checked over IPC, but no interactive visual test was done in a host session.

After the rename, 2026-11-25: the smoke test and IPC fixtures were repeated under the Shingle names. Python fixtures drove `shingle-nested` (debug build of `ec71234`) in one window on the host session, each run with its own `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, Wayland socket, IPC socket (`--ipc`, also exported as `WAYFIRE_SOCKET`), `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `HOME`; only the nested window itself connected to the host socket. Clients were a raw-protocol Python Wayland client (one `xdg_toplevel`, `wl_shm` buffers redrawn at every configured size, optional `wl_keyboard`, logging every configure, keymap, modifier, key and close event) and `wayland-info`; IPC used Wayfire's length-prefixed JSON. Instances were stopped with SIGKILL. The host output size, not the configured one, set the nested output (853×1440 in these runs). Results, all from observed protocol and IPC state:

| Area | Method | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Nested smoke test | Start, `stipc/get_display`, `list-methods`, `wayland-info`, one client | Pass, 16/16. Private sockets in place, 77 sorted methods, 48 globals including `xdg_wm_base`, `wl_seat`, `wl_shm` and `zwlr_layer_shell_v1`; the client mapped, was focused and was configured to its tile size. |
| Mapping | Three clients, catch-all `events/watch`, `list-views`, `get-layout` | Pass, 8/8. `view-mapped` per client, titles and app IDs from the protocol, tile order equals mapping order, tiles cover the workarea exactly, and each client's last configure equals its view size. |
| Geometry | `configure-view`, `simple-tile/set-layout`, `set-fullscreen`, `set-minimized` | Pass, 19/19. Exact floating rectangle in `view-info` and in the client configure, `view-geometry-changed` with `old-geometry`, the floating view leaves the tree and the rest retile; 2:1 and 1:3 weights reported as 0.67 and 0.25 and configured; fullscreen fills the output with the fullscreen state and restores the tile; minimize and restore keep the order. |
| Workspaces | `vswitch/send-view`, `set-workspace` with and without `view-id`, `binding_right`, `binding_last`, `binding_left` | Pass, 15/15. `view-workspace-changed` with `from`/`to`, the sent view tiled alone on its workspace, `wset-workspace-changed`, carrying a view back, no wraparound at the edge, out-of-grid target rejected. |
| Wayfire IPC | View properties, alpha, `focus-view`, `view-pre-map` blocking, runtime bindings, `close-view` | Pass, 32/32. A blocked view is unmapped, unfocused and gets no `view-mapped` until `unblock-map`; with two subscribers one release keeps it blocked and disconnecting the other maps it. Notification, `command` and `call-method` bindings fire from STIPC `feed_key` with `<super>`; not without the modifier or after `unregister-binding`. `close-view` sends `xdg_toplevel.close` and emits `view-unmapped`. |
| XKB `us,de` | `get-keyboard-state`, `set-keyboard-state`, client keymap and modifiers | Pass, 11/11. Two layouts reported; the keymap the client received compiles with libxkbcommon to `English (US)` and `German`; switching to index 1 and back sends `wl_keyboard.modifiers` with group 1 and 0 and `keyboard-modifier-state-changed`; index 2 rejected. |
| Session round trip | Five clients in a nested, unevenly weighted split, one floating at an exact rectangle on workspace 2 and one floating on workspace 1; SIGKILL and restart twice | Pass, 17/17, after the fix below. Before the fix it failed: every restored client became a new tile and the five placeholders stayed, doubling the windows on each restart. After it, both restarts relaunched all five clients from their saved command lines and `get-layout` for all three workspaces and the `list-views` ID, geometry, tiled edges and state were identical to before, with each client configured to its saved size. |
| Tilefire migration | Session built with Shingle paths, then moved to `tilefire/tilefire.yaml` and `tilefire/session.json`; started without `--config` and with only `TILEFIRE_LOCK`; two windows turned into same-app-ID placeholders without a command, tagged with launch IDs | Pass, 23/23. The Tilefire config applied (3×1 grid, `us,de`); the session restored identically and was saved back to `tilefire/session.json`, with no `shingle/` config or session created; clients started by hand with `TILEFIRE_LAUNCH_ID` claimed their own placeholders in reverse order; a second restart was identical; the idle lock ran the `TILEFIRE_LOCK` program with the private `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`; with both set, `SHINGLE_LOCK` won. |

Bug found and fixed: a toplevel was created as soon as it sent `xdg_toplevel.set_title`, with the launch template's app ID (the program name), so a client that sends its title before its app ID never claimed its restored placeholder. The window is now created at the app ID or first buffer, and a claimed placeholder takes the new title. Regression test: `title_before_app_id_claims_the_restored_placeholder` in `crates/shingle/src/shell.rs`, which drives the protocol over a socket pair.

Observed in that run and fixed since (see the Wayfire compatibility run below): a view blocked in `view-pre-map` already held its tile, so its neighbours shrank around an empty, unrendered slot until it was released; and clients received the keymap as `include` statements rather than a compiled keymap. `shingle-nested` still kept running 3 s after SIGTERM in that run.

Not covered: the PAM service fallback (needs `/etc/pam.d/tilefire`), and a clean quit and restart, because no IPC method quits and the quit binding needs the host keyboard; every restart above followed SIGKILL, so it tested the incremental saves. Host input and the clean quit are listed under [Needs operator: host input](#needs-operator-host-input).

Renderer split (NB-1), 2026-11-25: the same isolated setup (private `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, config, state, `HOME`, IPC and Wayland sockets, `WAYFIRE_SOCKET` overridden) ran `shingle-nested` from `ca03d41` and from the NB-1 branch, one after the other, each with one raw-protocol `wl_shm` client drawing a fixed pattern at its configured size. `grim` took two wlr-screencopy captures of each instance through its private socket. With `data/shingle.yaml` (853×1440 output) and with a copy using output scale 1.5 and transform 90 (1440×853 captures, background visible), the captures before and after were byte-identical (0 differing bytes of 3,684,960 and 4,913,280). Instances were stopped with SIGKILL. The host tiling changed the nested window width once between runs (854 instead of 853), so only captures of the same size were compared.

Wayfire compatibility, 2026-11-25: a Python fixture started `shingle-nested` (debug build of the `shingle-wayfire-compat` branch) in a window on the host session with a private `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, Wayland socket, IPC socket (`--ipc`, also `WAYFIRE_SOCKET`), `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `HOME`, and a copy of `data/shingle.yaml` with `layout: us,de`. Raw-protocol Wayland clients (one `xdg_toplevel` each, `wl_shm` buffers redrawn at every configured size, one with `wl_keyboard`) and two IPC connections drove it; no host input was sent. All 22 checks passed (output 640×1440 in the recorded run):

- One view tiled alone at 640 px. A second IPC connection watched `view-pre-map`, and a second client's view raised it with its metadata. For the following second, `get-layout` listed only the first view at 640 px and every configure the first client received stayed 640 px wide; the blocked view answered `view-info` and was unmapped in `list-views`.
- After `unblock-map`, `get-layout` listed both views at 320 px, the released view last; both clients were configured to 315 px (the tile inset by its gaps), and the view reported `mapped`.
- The keymap the client received (70,372 bytes) contained no `include` and compiled with libxkbcommon without include paths or environment names to `English (US)` and `German`; as a control, an `include`-form keymap failed to compile that way.
- No protocol errors; SIGTERM ended the compositor with status 0.

Behaviour that changed with the fix: a blocked view is configured to the output size until it is released, and then to its tile.

Input devices, 2026-11-25 (before the run above): a Python IPC fixture and a minimal raw-protocol Wayland client (one `xdg_toplevel` with `wl_pointer`, `wl_keyboard` and `wl_touch`) ran against `shingle-nested` in a window on the host session, with a private `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, Wayland socket, IPC socket and XDG config/state directories. All 38 checks passed:

- `list-methods` reported 77 methods including both input methods; `input/list-devices` returned the keyboard, pointer and touch devices (IDs 1–3) with all six fields, all enabled.
- With a button, a key and a touch point held, disabling the pointer delivered a button release, disabling the keyboard delivered a key release, and disabling touch delivered `wl_touch.cancel`.
- With the pointer disabled, `stipc/move_cursor` produced no client event and left `get-cursor-position` unchanged; STIPC button clicks were also dropped.
- A binding registered with `command/register-binding` fired a `command-binding` event from STIPC `feed_key` while enabled, did not fire with the keyboard disabled, and keys produced no client events.
- Touch input was dropped while touch was disabled.
- Re-enabling all three restored client motion and cursor position, key delivery and binding firing.
- Unknown ID, missing `id` or `enabled`, non-boolean `enabled` and string `id` returned Wayfire's errors and changed nothing.

Not covered by that run: gating of real host keyboard, pointer and touch events and the nested frontend's switcher and drag clean-up, which need physical host input (see [Needs operator: host input](#needs-operator-host-input)); and modifier delivery on disable, which was only unit-tested. The fixture's `shingle-nested` did not exit on SIGTERM or SIGINT and had to be killed; that is fixed, see below.

Signals, 2026-11-25: a Python fixture started `shingle-nested --ipc` in a window on the host session with a private `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, Wayland socket (`wayland-0`), IPC socket and XDG config and state directories, waited until `list-methods` answered over IPC (77 methods), then sent one signal. With the NB-2 build, SIGTERM and SIGINT each made it exit with status 0 about 0.11 s later; it wrote `shingle/session.json` to the private state directory and removed its Wayland socket. The same fixture against a build of `master` at `cf7aca5` reproduced the bug: neither signal ended the process within 10 s, and it had to be killed.

Input-device registry, 2026-11-25: two Python fixtures ran against `shingle-nested` built from `master` at `7189b50` and then from the NB-5 branch, each in a window on the host session with a private `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, Wayland socket, IPC socket and XDG config and state directories, ended with SIGTERM (exit 0 every time). No host input was sent.

- IPC transcript, 38 requests: `input/list-devices` before and after toggling devices, every `input/configure-device` error case, `stipc/move_cursor` and `get-cursor-position` with the pointer enabled and disabled, STIPC buttons and touch while disabled, and a `command/register-binding` binding fired by STIPC `feed_key` with the keyboard enabled, disabled and re-enabled. All 38 raw replies and the binding events were byte-identical between the two builds.
- Held-input release, 18 steps, with a raw-protocol client (one `xdg_toplevel` with `wl_pointer`, `wl_keyboard` and `wl_touch`): a STIPC button held and the pointer disabled gave `wl_pointer.button` released; a key held and the keyboard disabled gave `wl_keyboard.key` released; a touch point held and touch disabled gave `wl_touch.cancel`; input while disabled gave no events; re-enabling the keyboard resent the modifiers, and keys, buttons and touch were delivered again afterwards. The client received the same event sequence from both builds.

Not covered with real clients: more than one device of a kind, and device removal, since nested registers exactly three devices and never removes them; those are unit-tested only. Real libinput devices are for NB-6 on the test machine.

Shared keyboard (NB-3), 2026-11-25. STIPC `feed_key` then fired only IPC-registered bindings (fixed since, see the frontend fixes below), so configured bindings were driven through a second, isolated Shingle as the host: an outer `shingle-nested` (default `us` keymap) ran in a window on the host session with a private `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, Wayland socket, IPC socket, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `HOME`, and the `shingle-nested` under test ran as its Wayland client with its own private directories, a `de` keymap with `ctrl:nocaps`, and lavapipe (`VK_ICD_FILENAMES` set to `lvp_icd.json`) so that it could present through `wl_shm`. Keys were fed to the outer instance with STIPC `feed_key`, which delivers them to its focused client, the inner window, as real `wl_keyboard` events that winit reports to the inner instance. No input reached the host session. Command bindings touched marker files, workspaces were read with `window-rules/list-wsets`, and both instances were stopped with SIGTERM (exit 0).

| Check (inner keymap `de`, `ctrl:nocaps`) | NB-3 branch | `master` at `f35ff89` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `xkb:z` fires on physical `KEY_Y` | Pass | Fail: `xkb:y` fired instead, from the host's `us` layout |
| `xkb:y` does not fire on `KEY_Y` | Pass | Fail |
| `xkb:y` fires on `KEY_Z` | Pass | Pass, by the host layout |
| `super+y` fires on `KEY_Y` | Pass | Fail |
| `super+z` does not fire on `KEY_Y` | Pass | Pass |
| `super+xkb:odiaeresis` fires on `KEY_SEMICOLON` | Pass | Fail |
| `ctrl+k` fires with Caps Lock held (`ctrl:nocaps`) | Pass | Fail |
| Built-in `super+2` switches to workspace 2 | Pass | Fail |
| Built-in `super+1` switches back | Pass | Pass, trivially: it never left workspace 1 |

10 of 10 checks passed on the branch (the table plus an IPC reply check) and 4 of 10 on `master`. The outer instance sends no `wl_keyboard.modifiers` for STIPC keys, because STIPC does not run through XKB, so `master`, which took modifiers from the host, saw none at all; the branch derives them from the keys. The layout result does not depend on that: `master` matched `xkb:` bindings against the host's layout. The first attempts also found the FIFO and commit-timing protocol errors above, which disconnected the lavapipe client after its first swapchain recreation; the runs above used the fixed outer build for both columns. Not covered: holding modifiers across host focus changes (`crates/shingle-nested/src/host_keyboard.rs`), which needs host input; see step 9 below.

Frontend split (NB-4), 2026-11-25. Before moving any code, a scripted session pinned nested's behaviour. To be independent of the host session, the outer compositor was a small headless Shingle compositor with IPC, STIPC seat devices and a frame every 16 ms (no window on the host at all); the `shingle-nested` under test ran as its Wayland client on lavapipe with a private `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, Wayland socket, IPC socket, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `HOME`, with three raw-protocol `wl_shm` clients inside it. Keys, buttons, motion and touch were fed to the outer compositor over STIPC, which delivered them to the nested window as real `wl_keyboard`, `wl_pointer` and `wl_touch` events. The script has 38 steps: both switchers (also while Alt is held), Scale with keys, pointer hover and click, touch and the title filter, Expo with keys and a pointer click, sending a window to workspace 3 and following it, relative workspace switches, directional focus and move, a move drag and a resize drag with the pointer, grid snap and restore, click-to-focus, inversion on and off, a command binding and a held repeatable one, closing the focused window and the quit binding. After every step it recorded four IPC replies (`list-views` without PIDs, `get-focused-view`, `list-wsets`, `get-cursor-position`), a wlr-screencopy capture of nested's own output and one of the outer output, which shows the frame nested actually drew, Scale, Expo, the drop preview and inversion included. Because nested then decided after drawing a frame whether an animation needed another, a slow lavapipe frame could end an animation on an intermediate frame; the script therefore changes one view's alpha and sets it back before each capture, which makes nested draw the settled state again. That is fixed since: a frame draws and ends animations at one time, so the final state is always drawn (see the frontend fixes below).

| Build | IPC replies | Captures | Held binding runs | Quit binding |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `master` at `fcefeb9`, run twice | 152/152 identical | 74/74 identical | 46 and 46 | exit 0 |
| Key repeat moved (second commit) | 152/152 identical to `fcefeb9` | 74/74 identical | 46 | exit 0 |
| `App` moved into `Frontend` (third commit) | 152/152 identical | 74/74 identical | 46 | exit 0 |
| Final branch | 152/152 identical | 74/74 identical | 46 | exit 0 |

The held-binding count matches because the outer compositor repeats at Shingle's defaults too. With the nested instance configured for 10 repeats a second after 200 ms, holding the binding for 1.5 s ran it 45 times on `fcefeb9` (the host's 40 a second after 400 ms) and 15 times on the branch, which is the intended change. The nested smoke test on the same setup passed 14/14 on both `fcefeb9` and the branch: `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` printed and served from the private runtime directory, `stipc/get_display`, 77 sorted methods in `list-methods`, 48 globals in `wayland-info` including `xdg_wm_base`, `wl_seat`, `wl_shm` and `zwlr_layer_shell_v1`, one client mapped, focused and filling the output, and SIGTERM ending nested with status 0 after saving the session.

Not covered: real host keyboard focus changes, the left-handed and middle-emulation options (whose button translation changed, see the NB-4 entry in the design), the mouse wheel and relative pointer motion, which STIPC cannot produce; the wheel and zoom are covered by unit tests. Key repeat with physical keys is step 10 below.

Frontend fixes, 2026-11-25. STIPC keys sent to nested's own IPC socket, with the headless outer compositor of the NB-4 run as the only host (no window on the host session, no host input) and nested on lavapipe with a private `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, Wayland socket, IPC socket, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `HOME`. Nested's config had a `de` layout and two command bindings writing marker files; an IPC binding was registered with `command/register-binding`. Both builds were stopped with SIGTERM (exit 0).

| Check, keys fed with `stipc/feed_key` to nested | Branch | `master` at `9a2663c` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Default `super+2` switches to workspace 2 | Pass, (0, 0) to (1, 0) | Fail, stays at (0, 0) |
| YAML command `super+o` runs once | Pass, 1 run | Fail, 0 runs |
| YAML `super+xkb:z`, tapped on `KEY_Z` and then `KEY_Y` | Pass, 1 run | Fail, 0 runs |
| IPC binding on `KEY_F9` runs once | Pass, 1 run | Pass, 1 run |

The animation fix was checked with unit tests only (see [Automated](#automated)); no real-client capture was repeated for it.

Single binary, 2026-11-25: a Python fixture started the debug build of `target/debug/shingle` from the `shingle-single-binary` branch twice, each time with a private `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, IPC socket (`--ipc`), `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `HOME`, a config with `session: {restore: false}`, `--spawn sh -c ...` recording the child's `WAYFIRE_SOCKET` and `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, and lavapipe with implicit Vulkan layers disabled. The nested run had no backend option and only `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` set, to the host socket's absolute path (`DISPLAY` and `WAYLAND_SOCKET` unset), so it opened one window on the host session; no host input was sent. The headless run had `--headless` and no display variable at all. Both passed 17/17: the startup line named the backend and its reason (`using the nested backend (WAYLAND_DISPLAY is set)`, `using the headless backend (requested on the command line)`); `WAYLAND_DISPLAY=wayland-0` was printed and served from the private runtime directory and matched `stipc/get_display`; `list-methods` returned 77 sorted methods; the `--spawn` child saw the IPC path in `WAYFIRE_SOCKET` and the private socket in `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`; `wayland-info` listed 48 globals including `xdg_wm_base`, `wl_seat`, `wl_shm` and `zwlr_layer_shell_v1`; one raw-protocol `wl_shm` client mapped, was focused and filled the output (853×1440 nested, 1280×720 headless); `/proc/<pid>/fd` showed no DRM, input or TTY device open; and SIGTERM ended `shingle` with status 0 after it wrote `shingle/session.json` to the private state directory. Outside a session, `shingle` and `shingle --backend native` printed the native-not-built message and exited 1; `--backend drm`, `--bogus` and `SHINGLE_BACKEND=x` exited 2 with the error.

Toplevel handle and client environment (XW-1), 2026-11-25. All runs used the headless outer compositor of the NB-4 run as the only host (no window on the host session, no host input), the `shingle` binary under test nested in it on lavapipe or headless, and private `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, Wayland socket, IPC socket, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `HOME` for every instance. "Before" is a debug build of `5dbb9c5` (`master` at `daad6af` plus the pin test), "after" the branch head.

Environment check: Shingle was started with the host session's `DISPLAY=:1` and `WAYFIRE_SOCKET=/tmp/wayfire-wayland-1.socket` in its environment (and, headless, a stray `WAYLAND_SOCKET=99`), and at every spawn site a child wrote its environment to a file in the private directory: nested without `--ipc` (`--spawn`, autostart, a config command binding and the lock binding, with keys fed to the outer compositor), nested with `--ipc` (the same plus a restored session, `stipc/run` and an IPC `command/register-binding` command, with keys fed to nested's own IPC socket), headless with `--ipc` (`--spawn`, autostart, a restored session and `stipc/run`), and a compositor with STIPC seat devices and no frontend (the outer compositor's code, built from each revision), where the compositor runs IPC binding commands itself. Every instance exited with status 0 on SIGTERM.

| Children, 17 in all | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `DISPLAY` | `:1`, the host's, in all 17 | unset in all 17 |
| `WAYFIRE_SOCKET`, nested without `--ipc` (4) | the host's socket | unset |
| `WAYFIRE_SOCKET`, with `--ipc` (11) | the private IPC socket | the private IPC socket |
| `WAYFIRE_SOCKET`, embedder with IPC but no `--ipc` flag (2) | the host's socket | its own IPC socket |
| `WAYLAND_SOCKET` (headless and embedder, 6) | `99`, inherited | unset |
| `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` | the private `wayland-0` | the private `wayland-0` |
| `_JAVA_AWT_WM_NONREPARENTING` (removed from Shingle's own environment) | unset | `1` |

Nested pin script and smoke test, as in the NB-4 run, with the after build against the before build: the 38-step script gave 152/152 identical IPC replies and 74/74 identical outer and inner captures, the held repeatable binding ran 46 times in both and the quit binding exited 0 in both; two runs of the before build were identical to each other in the same way. The nested smoke test passed 14/14 on both builds: `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` printed and served from the private runtime directory, `stipc/get_display`, 77 sorted methods, 48 globals in `wayland-info` including `xdg_wm_base`, `wl_seat`, `wl_shm` and `zwlr_layer_shell_v1`, one client mapped, focused and filling the output, and SIGTERM ending nested with status 0 after saving the session. The session saved by the first environment run was restored by the second and third, relaunching its window.

Not covered: an X11 client, since there is no X server yet; XW-2 adds one and fills in `DISPLAY`.

XWayland server (XW-2), 2026-11-25: `tools/xwayland_test/run.py` ran the debug `target/debug/shingle` headless, five times, inside `unshare --user --map-root-user --mount --net --pid --fork --mount-proc` with a private directory bound over `/tmp` (so `/tmp/.X11-unix` was empty and the X locks private), empty tmpfs mounts over `/dev/dri` and `/dev/input`, and private `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, IPC socket, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `HOME`. X clients were the host's `xdpyinfo` and `xprop`; Xwayland was Debian's 24.1.6. 47/47 checks passed:

- Lazy (`mode: lazy`): `list-methods` returned 79 methods including both new ones; `stipc/get_display` and `stipc/get_xwayland_display` reported `:0`, and `/tmp/.X0-lock` and `/tmp/.X11-unix/X0` existed, before any X client, while `stipc/get_xwayland_pid` was -1 and Shingle had no Xwayland child; the `--spawn` child saw `DISPLAY=:0`. `xdpyinfo` then started Xwayland and succeeded, `xwayland-ready` arrived on a `window-rules/events/watch` subscription with `:0` and the PID, and the startup script ran once with `DISPLAY=:0`. `xprop -root` showed wlroots' 11 `_NET_SUPPORTED` atoms, `_NET_SUPPORTING_WM_CHECK` naming a window whose `_NET_WM_NAME` is `Shingle` and which points to itself, and `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` None. Xwayland had no DRM, input or TTY device open.
- Restart rule: SIGKILL 6.5 s after the lazy start left no server until the next `xdpyinfo`, which started a new one; SIGKILL 0.1 s after that start was not restarted, and the next `xdpyinfo` timed out. In eager mode (`mode: true`), SIGKILL 6.5 s after start restarted Xwayland at once, the new server served `:0`, and a SIGKILL right after that was not restarted.
- Eager mode: Xwayland ran with no X client, and a restored session's client, which recorded Shingle's children when it started, already saw the Xwayland PID. The server stayed after its last X client left.
- Idle: a lazily started Xwayland exited by itself 10.1 s after `xdpyinfo` left (status 0), and the next connection started a new one.
- SIGTERM ended Shingle with status 0 in every run, with Xwayland running or not; Xwayland was gone and `/tmp/.X0-lock` and `/tmp/.X11-unix/X0` were removed.
- `mode: false`: `stipc/get_display` reported `"xwayland": ""`, the PID -1, nothing was created in `/tmp` or `/tmp/.X11-unix`, and the `--spawn` child had no `DISPLAY`.

Xwayland's log in every run said `glamor: GBM Wayland interfaces not available` and `Failed to initialize glamor, falling back to sw`, so it uses its shared-memory path without `-shm`, as the design expected. Building `x11rb` 0.14 and `x11rb-protocol` 0.14 with the features used (Composite, XFixes) plus `shingle-xwayland` took 2.6 s in a debug build with two jobs; nested builds now compile both x11rb 0.13 (winit's) and 0.14.

The first harness run found two things the unit tests could not: a lazy Xwayland never exited when idle, because `-terminate` ignores only clients that set XFixes' disconnect mode, which wlroots' XWM does; and with that set, an eager Xwayland given a bare `-terminate` exited after its last X client. Both are fixed (see the design's XW-2 entry). An earlier version of the harness ran without a PID namespace; when an eager restart check got the PID -1 (its timing was wrong: it killed Xwayland 4.1 s after start, which the 5 s rule correctly did not restart) it signalled PID -1 and killed every process of the operator's session. The harness now runs in a PID namespace and signals only checked Xwayland PIDs.

Managed X windows (XW-3), 2026-11-25: the same harness, extended with a `windows` and a `probe` group, ran the debug `target/debug/shingle` headless (eager mode, 1280x720 output) in the same namespace, with the host's `xterm` 398, `xeyes`, `xlogo`, `xprop` and `xwininfo` and the in-tree probe client `target/debug/examples/xw_probe`. X clients ran as uid 1000 in a nested user namespace (see below). All seven groups passed, 87/87 checks, the last run on the final build; the XW-2 groups gave the same results as before.

- `xterm -e sleep 600`, `xeyes` and `xlogo`, started one after another, each appeared in `list-views` as mapped, with `app-id` `XTerm`, `XEyes` and `XLogo` (the `WM_CLASS` class), xterm's title, and a `pid` equal to the client's own PID whose `/proc/<pid>/comm` was the program. Their three tiles covered the 1280x720 workarea exactly without overlap, left to right in mapping order.
- `xprop -root _NET_CLIENT_LIST` listed the three windows in mapping order. `xwininfo -id` gave each window's absolute position and size equal to `view-info`'s geometry. xterm had `WM_STATE` Normal, and only the last-mapped, focused `xlogo` had `_NET_WM_STATE_FOCUSED`.
- `simple-tile/set-layout` with a new split tree, and `window-rules/configure-view` floating `xlogo` at 100,120 400x300, both reached the X geometry as `xwininfo` reported it.
- `close-view` closed `xterm` and `xeyes` through `WM_DELETE_WINDOW` (both list it, see below), and their views went away.
- Probe: a `view-pre-map` subscriber got the event for a probe window, which stayed unmapped in `list-views` until the subscriber's `unblock-map`. The probe's last `ConfigureNotify` matched its tile, and a `ConfigureRequest` for 5,5 100x100 while tiled was answered with a synthetic `ConfigureNotify` carrying the tile. `close-view` delivered `WM_DELETE_WINDOW`; a probe without it (`--no-delete`) was disconnected by `KillClient` and exited.
- Races: five probe windows without a background, destroyed as soon as the server reported them mapped, so before any buffer, left no view. A probe window unmapped as soon as each `MapNotify` arrived, ten times in a row (Xwayland creates the surface and sends the serial at that point), left no view, mapped again as exactly one view, and left none after its destroy. Whether a surface was in fact destroyed before its serial message in any of those cycles cannot be observed from outside; the exact orders are covered by the unit tests above.
- SIGTERM ended Shingle with status 0 after both groups.

The runs found three things: `xterm` run as the namespace's root fails to give its pty to group `tty`, which is not mapped, and exits, so the harness runs X clients as an ordinary user in a nested user namespace (`unshare --user --map-user=1000 --map-group=1000`, which execs the client, so its PID and comm stay the client's); `xeyes` on this host lists `WM_DELETE_WINDOW`, unlike what the design assumed, so `KillClient` was checked with the probe; and every close logged `BadWindow` for the `WM_STATE` change on the `UnmapNotify` that precedes a `DestroyNotify`, which the window manager now ignores for windows it has already forgotten. A rapid unmap-map loop that did not wait for each `MapNotify` left a mapped view, which was correct: the client's last unmap reached the server before the window manager's `MapWindow`, so the X window really was mapped.

Not covered by real runs: X keyboard focus and input (XW-4), override-redirect windows on screen (XW-4), a nested run with pixel checks, and a session restore of an X client. XW-4 covered all four (below).

X focus, unmanaged windows and client requests (XW-4), 2026-11-25: the harness, extended with `behaviour`, `session` and `nested` groups, ran the debug `target/debug/shingle` in the same namespace with the host's `xterm`, `xlogo`, `xprop`, `xwininfo` and `grim`, the probe client and a new Wayland probe client (`target/debug/examples/wl_probe`, crates/shingle/examples/wl_probe.rs). All input came from STIPC. All ten groups passed, 173/173 checks, the last run on the final build; the XW-2 and XW-3 groups gave the same results as before.

- Typing: with `xterm -e sh -c 'read line; ...'` focused after it mapped, `stipc/feed_key` typed `hello shingle` and Enter, and the line arrived in xterm's shell. `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` named xterm, and an `on created if app_id is "XTerm" then set alpha 0.5` rule gave it alpha 0.5. The X root window was 1280x720, the output's size.
- Focus between X and Wayland windows: a probe window got `FocusIn`, `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` and, alone, `_NET_WM_STATE_FOCUSED`, and `GetInputFocus` named it. When the Wayland probe mapped, it got `wl_keyboard.enter`, the X probe `FocusOut`, and `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` and the X focus became None. A click (`stipc/move_cursor` and `feed_button`) on the X probe moved the focus back (`FocusIn` there, `wl_keyboard.leave` on the Wayland probe), with `QueryPointer` reporting the pointer where Shingle had put it, and `focus-view` moved it to the Wayland probe again.
- Input models: a locally active probe was focused on map and got `WM_TAKE_FOCUS` and `FocusIn`; a globally active `NORMAL` probe got `WM_TAKE_FOCUS` but no focus on map; a no-input probe got neither. A click focused each in Shingle; the globally active one got `WM_TAKE_FOCUS` and no X focus, the no-input one an X focus of None. A probe that set the X focus to itself while another client's window was focused had it taken back.
- Dialogs: a probe with `WM_TRANSIENT_FOR` and no window type became its parent's child view, floated at its 300x200 centred on the parent and was focused, with `xwininfo` agreeing; a fixed-size dialog (equal minimum and maximum) floated at its size in the centre of the workarea.
- States: `_NET_WM_STATE` add and remove of fullscreen and of maximized (both directions) from the probe changed the view and the X geometry (a `ConfigureNotify` of 1280x720 at 0,0 for fullscreen) and came back in `_NET_WM_STATE`. `WM_CHANGE_STATE` Iconic minimized it, with `WM_STATE` Iconic and `_NET_WM_STATE_HIDDEN`, and `set-minimized` false restored it with `WM_STATE` Normal. `wm-actions/set-fullscreen`, `toggle_maximize` and `set-minimized` reached `_NET_WM_STATE` and the X geometry, and removing `HIDDEN` from the client restored the window. A probe started with `_NET_WM_STATE_FULLSCREEN` mapped fullscreen. `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` from the probe focused it, and `_NET_CLOSE_WINDOW` closed it through `WM_DELETE_WINDOW`.
- Stacking: focusing xterm and two probes in turn put each on top of `_NET_CLIENT_LIST_STACKING` and of the X stacking order `xwininfo -root -children` reported; the list held the same windows as `_NET_CLIENT_LIST`.
- Menus: Ctrl+left button in xterm mapped an override-redirect menu (218x446, around the pointer), above xterm in X and not a view. The X focus and `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` stayed on xterm while the menu, which has neither a window type nor `WM_HINTS`, had the Wayland keyboard, as Wayfire gives it (see the design's XW-4 entry). After the pointer moved off it and the button was released, the menu unmapped and the keyboard returned to xterm. An override-redirect probe of type `POPUP_MENU` mapped at the position it asked for, took no focus, and `QueryPointer` found the pointer over it; an untyped one took the keyboard while the X focus stayed, and gave it back when destroyed.
- Root cursor: over a probe window without a cursor of its own, XFixes reported a 24x24 image with hotspot 3,1, the size and hotspot of Adwaita's `default` cursor at size 24.
- Session: Shingle, running as uid 1000 like the X clients, saved a session with xterm in the left tile and xlogo in the right; the saved xterm's command was `xterm -e sleep 600`. The restored run started with both placeholders and relaunched both, and although xlogo mapped first, each claimed its placeholder's view ID and its own tile; the restored xterm was a new `xterm` process.
- Nested: nested Shingle ran on lavapipe as a client of a headless Shingle, both inside the namespace. In wlr-screencopy captures of the headless output, which show the frames nested drew, red and blue probes were at their view geometry; a green override-redirect probe was drawn whole over the red tile; Scale showed both as thumbnails, and a click on the red one (through the headless Shingle's pointer) ended Scale and focused it, with `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` following; Expo showed each in its own workspace's cell; Alt+Escape (the fast switcher) moved the focus between them, with `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` following; and xterm's menu was drawn over xterm (14,682 dark pixels in its rectangle, 72 before it opened).
- SIGTERM ended every Shingle with status 0.

The runs found four bugs the unit tests had not, all fixed: X's root window was 0x0 because xdg-output version 3 never sent `wl_output.done` after its details, so X clamped the pointer to (0, 0) and opened xterm's menu at the screen's corner; headless had no STIPC devices and reported no frames; an unmanaged window that committed its serial before its first buffer was never focused; and focusing an unmanaged window also moved the pointer focus, which ended xterm's pointer grab and closed its menu at once.

Not covered by real runs: `_NET_WM_MOVERESIZE` (headless has no frontend to run a move or resize; unit tests cover the mapping), the same-client `FocusIn` exception (unit tests only), the clipboard (XW-5, below) and HiDPI (XW-7).

Clipboard and primary selection (XW-5), 2026-11-25: the harness, extended with a `selection` group, ran the debug `target/debug/shingle` headless with `xwayland.mode: true` in the same namespace, with the host's `xclip` 0.13, `wl-copy` and `wl-paste` 2.2.1 and the Wayland probe client; `xclip` and wl-clipboard ran only there, against the private Shingle and its private `DISPLAY` and `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, so the operator's clipboard was never touched. All eleven groups passed, 199/199 checks, the last run on the final build; the 26 new checks:

- Both directions, both selections, both sizes: `xclip -selection clipboard -i` (and `primary`) to `wl-paste --no-newline` (`--primary`), and `wl-copy --foreground` (`--primary`) to `xclip -o`, each with 10 bytes and with 1 MiB, arrived byte for byte, as did 8 MiB from `xclip` to `wl-paste`. A build with temporary logging, removed afterwards, showed both kinds of `INCR` at work: `xclip` sent each 1 MiB as chunks of 1,048,575 and 1 bytes and the 8 MiB as eight chunks and 8 bytes, and Shingle sent each 1 MiB to `xclip` as 16 chunks of 64 KiB; 10 bytes went as one property.
- wlr-data-control: with `WAYLAND_DEBUG=1`, `wl-paste`'s trace showed the X selection arriving as a `zwlr_data_control_offer_v1` with `offer("text/plain;charset=utf-8")`, and no `wl_data_device` object, so no keyboard focus was involved.
- Slow reader: while `wl-paste | (sleep 3; cat)` held a 1 MiB transfer from `xclip`, an IPC call took 16 ms and an X primary selection reached `wl-paste --primary` with the reader still waiting; the reader then got all 1 MiB after 3.0 s.
