- Slow writer: the Wayland probe offered the clipboard through `wl_data_device` and answered each `send` 3 s late on a thread of its own. `xclip -o`'s request reached it as `text/plain;charset=utf-8`; meanwhile an IPC call took 1 ms and `wl-copy --primary` reached `xclip -o -selection primary`; `xclip -o` got the text after 3.0 s.
- `disable_primary_selection`: the clipboard was still bridged, an X primary selection that `xclip -o` could read never reached `wl-paste --primary`, and a Wayland one that `wl-paste --primary` could read never reached `xclip -o`.
- SIGTERM ended both Shingles with status 0.

The first runs found what the unit tests had not: `wl-copy --primary` refused to run, because Shingle's wlr-data-control devices were not told the (empty) selections when created, which the protocol requires and wl-clipboard relies on; this is fixed. The XW-2 to XW-4 groups gave the same results as before.

Not covered by real runs: a focused Wayland client reading an X selection through `wl_data_device` (`wl-paste` uses wlr-data-control, and so do the in-process tests; offers to focused clients come from `data_device`'s existing sync, which treats an X11 source like any other), copying from `xterm` (only `xclip` was used), and an X owner that never answers.

Host clipboard for nested, 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-nested-clipboard` branch. tools/gpu_test/run.py's new group `nested-clipboard` (tools/gpu_test/nested_clipboard_checks.py) ran nested Shingle on lavapipe as a client of `shingle --headless` in the GPU harness's namespace (user, mount, network and PID namespace, a private `/dev` with only the render node, NVIDIA's compute nodes and udmabuf, the host's `/run/user/1000` covered, no host display variables, every signal through `signal_checked`, every opened device checked); the headless Shingle played the host with its wlr-data-control version 2. Nothing ran on the operator's desktop or touched its clipboard. 36/36 checks passed:

- Nested logged `sharing [Clipboard, Primary] with the host through zwlr_data_control_manager_v1`.
- `wl-copy --foreground` on the headless Shingle's socket and `wl-paste --no-newline` on nested's, and the reverse, for the clipboard and the primary selection at 10 bytes and 1 MiB: all eight transfers arrived byte for byte, and after each the `wl-copy` was still running and its own compositor still pasted its data, so nothing was mirrored back (a `wl-copy --foreground` exits when its selection is taken).
- For each 1 MiB transfer a reader `wl-paste | (sleep 2; cat)` held its pipe for 2 s: meanwhile ten IPC calls to the two compositors took at most 17 ms, and the reader got the whole mebibyte after 2.0 to 2.1 s.
- Under `disable_primary_selection` nested logged `sharing [Clipboard]`, the clipboard crossed and the primary selection did not cross either way; with `behavior.host_clipboard: false` nested bound nothing and the clipboard did not cross either way. Every SIGTERM exited with status 0.
- The other harnesses still pass, the nested Shingles of the session and GPU harnesses now with the bridge on: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 239/239, tools/output_test/run.py 121/121, tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip` 27/27 (worst reply 25.3 ms, 30 changes in 3 writes; every layout and view identical after the SIGKILL and SIGTERM restarts), and every tools/gpu_test/run.py group (`offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj outputs nested-outputs nested-smoke nested-scaled wayfire-plugins wayfire-shell vswipe decoration switcher colours minimize-focus drag-icon resize nested-clipboard`) 283/283, no device opened outside the allowed set.

Not covered by real runs: ext-data-control, which Shingle does not offer as a host (the operator's Wayfire does; the bridge prefers it, and step 13 of [Needs operator: host input](#needs-operator-host-input) checks it), a host with wlr-data-control version 1 or none at all, the host's seat going away, and pasting with a real keyboard into focused applications.

Nested pointer bindings and resize configures, 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-nested-pointer-bindings` branch. The operator's nested Shingle on a scaled 4K host monitor reported `HEADLESS-1` as 3840x2160 at scale 1, and Alt+drag with the real mouse did nothing while the same drag through STIPC worked; kitty redrew slowly after a resize and not at all during one. The unit tests reproduce both (above); the harness results:

- tools/gpu_test/run.py's new group `nested-scaled` (tools/gpu_test/nested_output_checks.py): nested Shingle on lavapipe in a headless Shingle whose output has scale 1.5 and then 2, with the headless Shingle's own pointer bindings off so that Super and the buttons reach the nested window. At both scales `wlr-randr` reported nested's `HEADLESS-1` at the host's scale and `list-outputs` gave it the window's logical size, 1272x682; the headless Shingle's pointer at (322, 375) was at (318, 341) in nested, the window's origin being (4, 34); and a Super+left-button drag through the headless Shingle from the left of two tiled `wl_probe` clients onto the right half of the other swapped them, 12/12. The build before the fix fails the first check at both scales, as the operator saw: scale 1 and an output of 1908x1023 at 1.5 and 2544x1364 at 2, the window's physical size.
- tools/gpu_test/run.py's new group `resize` (tools/gpu_test/resize_checks.py), offscreen Shingle on the RTX 3060's render node with kitty (Mesa's EGL on `wl_shm`, since the namespace hides the card node) tiled beside a `wl_probe` and a floating `wl_probe`, dragging the boundary 300 pixels over 40 frames. Before the configure fix, with one motion per frame kitty got 41 configures and committed 30 buffers of 29 sizes, and with eight motions per frame (320 motions) it got 301 configures and committed 29 buffers of 28 sizes; it drew its final size 4 to 20 ms after the release, and the floating probe, which nothing changed, got 41 and 301 configures. After it: 40 configures and 27 buffers of 27 sizes, then 40 configures and 29 buffers of 28 sizes, final size 0 to 23 ms after the release, and no configure for the floating probe. So in this harness kitty kept drawing during the drags before the fix too: what the fix removes is the configure flood, a configure per motion to every toplevel, which grows with the pointer's rate and the number of windows. The operator's slow redraw is not reproduced here; it may come from the configure flood at a real mouse's rate with several kitty windows, from the scale bug, which had kitty draw 4K buffers at scale 1, or from something only the host shows. Step 12 of [Needs operator: host input](#needs-operator-host-input) checks it on the host.
- The other harnesses: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 239/239, tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip` 27/27 (worst reply 26.1 ms), tools/output_test/run.py 121/121 (the first run, before configured scales were kept, failed `nested: a reloaded scale is applied to the window size`, which the third commit fixes), and every tools/gpu_test/run.py group (`offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj outputs nested-outputs nested-smoke wayfire-plugins wayfire-shell vswipe decoration switcher colours minimize-focus drag-icon resize` 225/226, the miss being `syncobj`, whose probe was not built; then `syncobj nested nested-gpu nested-outputs nested-smoke nested-scaled` again with the final build, 64/65: one `syncobj: SIGTERM exits offscreen Shingle with status 0` timed out, and three more runs of `syncobj` passed 10/10 each), no device opened outside the allowed set.

Input gaps (WG-8 to WG-10), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-input-gaps` branch:

- tools/output_test/run.py 121/121, with WG-8's `wg8_text_input_checks` (tools/output_test/text_input_checks.py). Raw-protocol clients (tools/output_test/wayland_wire.py) of headless Shingle in the harness's namespace: the application mapped a toplevel and created a `zwp_text_input_v1` (global version 1); `activate` on its focused surface sent `enter` with that surface, and the input-method-v1 client got `activate` with a context. `set_surrounding_text` "Grüße" (3, 3), `set_content_type` latin and date and `commit_state` 11 reached the context as `surrounding_text`, `content_type` 256 and 9 (v1's date) and `commit_state` 11. The input method's `preedit_string` (11, "にほ") and `commit_string` (11, "日本") arrived at the application unchanged, and `deactivate` sent `leave` and ended the context. With an input-method-v2 client instead, activating again activated it with the surrounding text "abc", and its `delete_surrounding_text` (1, 0), `commit_string` "xyz" and `commit` arrived as v1's `delete_surrounding_text` (-1, 1) and `commit_string` (21, "xyz"). Shingle exited 0 on SIGTERM. Chromium with `--enable-wayland-ime` was not run.
- tools/gpu_test/run.py's new group `drag-icon` (tools/gpu_test/drag_icon_checks.py), offscreen Shingle on the RTX 3060's render node: a `wl_probe --drag TEXT --drag-icon` and a `wl_probe --drop` floated side by side; no magenta showed before the drag; a STIPC press on the source started a drag with a 32x32 magenta icon, and `grim` showed it centred on the pointer at (620, 250) over the background, at (620, 550) and at (950, 250) over the target, each time no longer at the previous stop; the target was entered, the release delivered the text, and the next capture had no magenta anywhere and the target's white where the icon had been. Before WG-10 the icon was never drawn.
- Per-device input options were not run on hardware: step 52 of [native-testing.md](native-testing.md#wg-9-per-device-input-options) is for the test machine.
- The other harnesses still pass: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 239/239, tools/session_test/run.py 13/13 (worst reply 15.9 ms) and `--checks round-trip` 17/17, and every tools/gpu_test/run.py group (`offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj outputs nested-outputs nested-smoke wayfire-plugins wayfire-shell vswipe decoration switcher colours minimize-focus drag-icon`) 224/224, no device opened outside the allowed set.
- Found on the way: input-method v1 got text-input v3's content purposes from `pin` on one too high (as in Wayfire); and adding the section fields to `input/list-devices` as null changed Wayfire's reply on every backend, which nested's pinned replies caught, so they are left out where no section applies.

Drag and drop with X (XW-6), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-xwayland-dnd` branch: the harness, extended with a `dnd` group, ran headless Shingle with `xwayland.mode: true` in the same namespace, with the X probe as an Xdnd source (`--xdnd-source`, with `--drag-icon`) or target (`--xdnd-target`) and the Wayland probe as a `wl_data_device` source (`--drag`) or target (`--drop`), each pair tiled side by side, and drove the drags with STIPC's `move_cursor` and `feed_button` (a press, three motions of up to 8 pixels, 12 motions to the other window's centre, 50 ms apart, then the release). All fourteen groups passed, 239/239 checks; the 27 new ones:

- X to Wayland: the X probe took `XdndSelection`, the Wayland probe was entered with `text/plain;charset=utf-8` (from the X source's `UTF8_STRING`), the X probe heard through the proxy that the drag was accepted with `XdndActionCopy`, a screencopy capture showed the probe's 32×32 magenta drag icon drawn over the Wayland window next to the pointer (993 magenta pixels in the 40×40 square below and right of it), the release made the X probe send `XdndDrop` to the proxy, the Wayland probe read "dragged from X" and finished, the X probe got `XdndFinished` performed with `XdndActionCopy`, and the icon was gone from the next capture.
- Wayland to X: the Wayland probe's drag entered the X probe with `XdndEnter` version 5 naming `UTF8_STRING`, `XdndPosition`s followed the pointer in root coordinates (the last at the window's centre, 960,360), the Wayland source got `target` with its type and `action` copy, the release made it `dnd_drop_performed`, the X probe converted `XdndSelection`, read "dragged from Wayland" and sent `XdndFinished`, and the source got `dnd_finished`.
- X to X: between two X probes the source found the target window itself under the pointer, not the proxy, and the text and `XdndFinished` went between them.
- Refused: over a Wayland probe without `--drop` nothing accepted the X drag, the X probe cancelled it on the release with `XdndLeave`, a second drag started normally and Shingle still answered.
- SIGTERM ended Shingle with status 0.

The other harnesses still pass: tools/output_test/run.py 112/112, tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip` 27/27 (worst reply 23.8 ms, 30 changes in 3 writes, nested's SIGTERM after 2.9 s; every layout and view identical after the SIGKILL and SIGTERM restarts) and `tools/gpu_test/run.py` with every group, `offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj outputs nested-outputs nested-smoke wayfire-plugins wayfire-shell vswipe decoration switcher colours minimize-focus`, 214 of 214 with its device check.

Not covered by real runs: real toolkits (no GTK or Qt X11 application is installed, and `xedit`, which the design named, has no Xdnd); a Wayland drag from a client other than the probe; `XdndProxy`, `XdndTypeList` and Xdnd 3 and 4 targets (unit tests only); an X source over a Wayland window when a button other than the first is held; the 2 s drop timeout; and touch drags, which are not bridged.

Fixes from the API documentation, 2026-11-25: a Python fixture (`doc_bugs_run.py`, outside the repository) ran the `shingle` binary nested in the headless outer compositor of the NB-4 run, so no window opened on the host session and no host input was sent. Each run had its own `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, Wayland and IPC sockets, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `HOME`, lavapipe, a 3×3 workspace grid and `workspace_switch_after: 300`. Raw-protocol `wl_shm` clients and IPC drove three scenarios; pointer and keys went through the outer compositor's STIPC as real `wl_pointer` and `wl_keyboard` events. The debug build of `d53d4bb` passed 23/23 checks, twice; `master` at `a5b9bf0` passed 8/21:

| Scenario | Branch | `master` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Null-buffer unmap: of two tiled clients, one commits a null buffer, then a new initial commit and a buffer | `view-unmapped` for it, only the other listed, at the full workarea width and focused; its initial commit answered with a configure; `view-mapped` with a new ID and both tiled again | No event, both still listed at half width, the hidden one still focused; its remap only worked because it had never unmapped |
| Top-edge drag: super+left drag of a tiled view from workspace (1, 1) to the middle of the top edge, held 1 s, dropped; then to the top-left corner | No switch while held, dropped view fills the workarea; the corner switches to (0, 0) after 300 ms and the view goes along | Switched up to (1, 0) while held; the corner from there switches nothing, as no row is above |
| Corrupt session: garbage in `shingle/session.json` with `session.restore` on | Starts and serves IPC, warns once naming `session.json` and `session.json.corrupt-SECONDS`, the set-aside file is byte-identical, a client maps, SIGTERM exits 0 and saves a fresh session | Exits 1 with `expected ident at line 1 column 30` and leaves the file in place |

`/proc/<pid>/fd` of both compositors showed no DRM, input or TTY device open. Writing the remapping client found that an unmapped toplevel got no configure for its new initial commit, and the first branch run showed the JSON error twice in the warning; both are fixed in the branch. The other fixes are covered by unit and protocol tests only.

DMA-BUF Vulkan setup (DB-1), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-gpu-dmabuf` branch. GPU runs used tools/gpu_test/run.py: a user, mount, network and PID namespace whose `/dev` held only `null`, `zero`, `full`, `random`, `urandom`, a private `pts` and `shm`, `dri/renderD128`, `nvidia0`, `nvidiactl`, `nvidia-uvm`, `nvidia-uvm-tools` and `udmabuf` (no card node, no `nvidia-modeset`, no `/dev/input`, no TTY), with `/run/user/1000` covered by a tmpfs, private home and XDG directories, and `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, `WAYLAND_SOCKET`, `DISPLAY`, `WAYFIRE_SOCKET` and `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS` removed. Every run's device check passed: the processes opened only `renderD128`, `nvidia0`, `nvidiactl`, `udmabuf` and `null`.

- `dmabuf_caps` in the namespace listed the NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3060 (driver 590.48.01) with render node 226:128 and `VK_KHR_external_semaphore_fd`. ARGB8888, XRGB8888, ABGR8888 and XBGR8888 each have the same lists, all with sRGB: sampling `0x0300000000606015`, `...6014`, `...6013`, `...6012`, `...6011`, `...6010` and `LINEAR`; rendering the same six block-linear modifiers but not `LINEAR`; every one up to 32768x32768. lavapipe (Mesa 25.0.7, with `VK_ICD_FILENAMES`) has no render node and `LINEAR` for sampling and rendering, up to 16384x16384, for all four formats.
- tests/dmabuf_import.rs in the namespace on NVIDIA: a linear XRGB8888 image exported by a second Vulkan device and written by the CPU, and an XRGB8888 image in `0x0300000000606015` that the second device drew into through `import_dmabuf(.., Render)`, were imported and drawn twice each with every pixel within 4 of the pattern and alpha 255: pass, with the acquire and release barriers and, in the ignored experiments, without them on both devices. The udmabuf tests skip on NVIDIA: the ignored `udmabuf_import_on_any_driver` imports without error but draws zeros or unrelated data (it failed in every run, with different wrong pixels), also after `DMA_BUF_IOCTL_SYNC`.
- The same tests on lavapipe in the namespace: all 7 pass, including the udmabuf imports (XRGB8888 with a zero alpha byte drawn opaque, ABGR8888 in byte order) and the experiments without barriers. So lavapipe imports linear buffers.
- The offscreen pixel tests and `shingle-render`'s unit tests pass in the namespace on NVIDIA.
- Nested still runs with unchanged screencopy: tools/xwayland_test/run.py's `nested` group (nested Shingle on lavapipe, which now opens its device through the DMA-BUF path, inside headless Shingle, both in its namespace) passed 14/14 with the branch and with `master` at `1be56cb`, and nested logged `DMA-BUF import available: no render node, 4 format and modifier pairs`. With `SHINGLE_XWAYLAND_TEST_CAPTURES` the nine settled captures of the outer output were saved for each build: the six taken at checked states (the override-redirect menu, its removal, the Scale thumbnails, the Expo wall and xterm's menu before and after) were byte-identical between branch and `master`. The other three are taken while an animation may still run and differed between two runs of `master` as well.

Not covered: client buffers, which need DB-4's protocol; NB-8's scanout buffers on a card; compressed modifiers, which this GPU does not list, so the barriers' effect on them is unknown.

Offscreen backend, frame capture and GPU harness (DB-2), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-offscreen-backend` branch. `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks offscreen nested baseline` ran in the namespace described for DB-1 (the same private `/dev`, no `nvidia-modeset`), passed 17/17, and its device check passed: the processes opened only `renderD128`, `nvidia0`, `nvidiactl`, `udmabuf`, `null` and `grim`'s files in the private `/dev/shm`.

- Offscreen Shingle logged `rendering offscreen on NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3060` and `DMA-BUF import available: render node 226:128, 28 format and modifier pairs`. A `wl_probe --pattern` `wl_shm` client mapped at 60,30 1170x650 (outer gaps 30, 50, 40, 60); `grim`'s 1280x720 capture showed red, green, blue and white at the centres of its four quadrants and the background (89, 89, 89) in the gap. After `invert/toggle` the capture showed cyan, magenta, yellow and black there, which the CPU composite could not show. SIGTERM ended Shingle with status 0. Before the signal fix it died with -15.
- Nested Shingle on lavapipe (`DMA-BUF import available: no render node, 4 format and modifier pairs`) mapped its window at 60,30 1170x650 in offscreen Shingle; its own `wl_probe --pattern` client mapped at 25,20 1135x615 inside it, and captures of the outer output showed the four quadrants at 85,50 plus their centres. Both exited with status 0 on SIGTERM.
- Baseline before DMA-BUF, against offscreen Shingle, which offers no linux-dmabuf: `vkcube --wsi wayland` printed `Selected GPU 1: llvmpipe (LLVM 19.1.7, 256 bits), type: Cpu`, drew and exited 0 after 300 frames, so it fell back to software rendering on `wl_shm`; with `--gpu_number 0` it printed `Selected GPU 0: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3060, type: DiscreteGpu` and `Could not find both graphics and present queues` and exited 1 without a window, because NVIDIA's WSI cannot present there. `es2gears_wayland -info` reported `EGL_VENDOR = Mesa Project` and ran at about 56 FPS, so NVIDIA's EGL was not used and Mesa's software EGL drew. Nested Shingle fell back to lavapipe (`no render node, 4 format and modifier pairs`) and mapped its window; with `VK_ICD_FILENAMES` set to only NVIDIA's ICD it exited with `failed to initialize wgpu: no compatible GPU adapter was found`. DB-4 repeats these against linux-dmabuf.
- tools/xwayland_test/run.py's `nested` group passed 14/14 with the branch; its override-redirect check captures nested's own output, which now comes from nested's drawn frame.

Buffer lifetime (DB-3), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-buffer-lifetime` branch, in the namespace described for DB-1 and DB-2. The new `wl_probe --fifo` mode maps its toplevel and then sends three commits in one flush: buffer `a` with a `wp_fifo_v1` barrier, then `b` and `c`, each with `wait_barrier`, so that `c` replaces `b` while both wait. `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks buffers` (offscreen Shingle on NVIDIA):

- Before the fix (the branch's first commit, `1ba68b0`, whose compositor is `master`'s): the probe got `release` for `a` and `c` only, and the check failed 4 of 5. `b` was never released, as [design/dmabuf.md](design/dmabuf.md#what-exists-today) suspected.
- With the fix: `release` for `a`, `b` and `c`, and the capture showed `c`'s quadrants at the view geometry; 5/5. `--checks buffers offscreen nested` passed 16/16, so DB-2's shm checks still pass, and the device check passed (only `renderD128`, `nvidia0`, `nvidiactl`, `udmabuf`, `null` and `grim`'s files in the private `/dev/shm`).
- tools/xwayland_test/run.py passed 199/199 twice; a first run, while other builds kept the load near 6, passed 196/199, and its failing checks were not kept. tools/output_test/run.py passed 24/24.

Not covered: buffers that stay locked after their commit (DMA-BUFs, DB-4) and real blockers (DB-4, DB-5); the queue is exercised only through the unit tests' fake blockers.

Client DMA-BUFs (DB-4), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-linux-dmabuf` branch, in the GPU namespace of DB-1 and DB-2 with one addition: DB-4's groups expose `/dev/nvidia-modeset` ([design/dmabuf.md](design/dmabuf.md#db-4-as-built)). Every Shingle ran with `SHINGLE_TRACE_BUFFERS=1`, and each client was judged by `grim` captures and by the trace of its run. `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks offscreen nested baseline buffers dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston` passed 64 of 65 before the fix below (the failure was the cube freeze) and 65 of 65 after it, and the device check passed: the processes opened only `renderD128`, `nvidia0`, `nvidiactl`, `nvidia-modeset`, `udmabuf`, `null`, a private pty and files in the private `/dev/shm`.

- `wayland-info` against offscreen Shingle listed `zwp_linux_dmabuf_v1` version 5 with main device `0xE280` (226:128) and a tranche of 28 pairs: ARGB, XRGB, ABGR and XBGR8888, each with `LINEAR` and NVIDIA's six block-linear modifiers `0x0300000000606010` to `...6015`, the caps DB-1 recorded; Shingle logged `linux-dmabuf version 5 on render node 226:128, 28 format and modifier pairs`.
- `vkcube --wsi wayland --c 600` on NVIDIA ran to its end with status 0; two captures a moment apart differed in thousands of samples of its view, and the trace showed 18 buffers (its swapchains of 3 at 500x500 and while the tile was configured, all `XR24` with modifier `0x0300000000606014`) each imported once, the shown ones used from the cache at every commit, no `shm upload` and about 600 releases. With `VK_ICD_FILENAMES` set to lavapipe it stayed on `wl_shm` (`shm upload` only, no import).
- Chromium (`--ozone-platform=wayland`, a fresh `--user-data-dir` in the private HOME, `--no-sandbox` because the namespace maps the user to root) showed the local green page and presented 3 imported DMA-BUFs. `weston-simple-dmabuf-egl` drew from 4 imported buffers; `mpv --vo=gpu-next --gpu-api=vulkan` played `av://lavfi:testsrc` from 4 imported buffers and exited 0 at the end.
- `glxgears` through Xwayland (lazy mode, Xwayland run with `-verbose 3` through `SHINGLE_XWAYLAND`) drew; `glxinfo -B` reported `NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3060/PCIe/SSE2`, Xwayland logged `GLX: Initialized glamor GL provider for screen 0` with `/dev/dri/renderD128` open, and offscreen Shingle imported its 6 buffers.
- Nested Shingle with only NVIDIA's ICD, against a compositor offering linux-dmabuf, started (against one without it, the `baseline` group still records `no compatible GPU adapter was found`, as in DB-2), offered linux-dmabuf on 226:128 itself, and presented its window through offscreen Shingle's import (9 buffers imported, used from the cache; the one `shm upload` is winit's cursor). Its `wl_probe --pattern` client's quadrants showed in captures of the outer output, and `vkcube` inside it drew from 18 buffers nested Shingle imported.
- With `SHINGLE_DMABUF=0` there was no global, the log said `SHINGLE_DMABUF=0 forces wl_shm`, and DB-2's baseline repeated: `vkcube` picked llvmpipe by itself and `es2gears_wayland` ran on `EGL_VENDOR = Mesa Project`.
- Recorded, not verified on DMA-BUF: NVIDIA's EGL cannot create a Wayland `EGLDisplay` without `/dev/dri/card0` (`EGL_BAD_ACCESS` in `eglQueryDeviceStringEXT` right after `stat("/dev/dri/card0")` fails, seen with `strace`), so `es2gears_wayland`, `eglgears_wayland`, `kitty` and Firefox drew through Mesa's llvmpipe on `wl_shm` (they mapped and drew, and the trace showed `shm upload` only), `mpv --gpu-api=opengl` exited with `Failed to find a usable DRM primary node`, and `weston-simple-dmabuf-feedback` asserted that the main device has a primary node. These need the card node: step 23 of [native-testing.md](native-testing.md).
- No real client waited for an implicit fence: NVIDIA's buffers polled readable at every commit (`blocked implicit` never appeared). The wait is covered only by the unit tests, with a pipe as the fence.
- **Found and fixed: NVIDIA's Vulkan clients froze through merged FIFO and commit-timing updates.** In roughly one run in six (4 of about 25), `vkcube` kept committing and getting every buffer released while its picture stopped changing. Instrumented runs showed offscreen Shingle drawing 60 frames a second and FIFO applying its deferred commit every frame, but commit timing deferring each one: once a burst of commits had been merged behind a FIFO barrier (each merge released the replaced buffer, as DB-3 made it), the client was no longer throttled by its buffers, its timestamps ran ahead of the clock, and every merge into the waiting commit-timing update kept the later deadline. FIFO and commit timing now queue updates in order with their own gates and deadlines, as wlroots' implementations do ([design/dmabuf.md](design/dmabuf.md#db-3-as-built)). After the change, `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks dmabuf` passed 14/14 in 10 runs out of 10, each with the cube changing between captures (thousands of differing samples) and `vkcube --c 600` ending with status 0 after about 598 releases; after it, too, `--checks buffers` passed 5/5 (the FIFO probe got `release` for `a`, `b` and `c`), the full `run.py --checks offscreen nested baseline buffers dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston` passed 65/65 with its device check, tools/xwayland_test/run.py 199/199 and tools/output_test/run.py 24/24.
- Not run: the native backend with DMA-BUF clients (step 23 of [native-testing.md](native-testing.md)) and a GPU loss with DMA-BUF clients, which only the native backend recovers from.

Explicit sync (DB-5), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-drm-syncobj` branch, in the GPU namespace of DB-4 (the `syncobj` group without `nvidia-modeset`, since it runs no Vulkan client). `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks offscreen nested baseline buffers dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj` passed 111 of 111, and its device check passed: the processes opened only `renderD128`, `nvidia0`, `nvidiactl`, `nvidia-modeset`, `udmabuf`, `null`, a private pty and files in the private `/dev/shm`. `--checks dmabuf` then passed 16 of 16 in 5 runs out of 5, each with its device check.

- `wayland-info` against offscreen Shingle listed `wp_linux_drm_syncobj_manager_v1` version 1 next to `zwp_linux_dmabuf_v1` version 5, and Shingle logged `linux-drm-syncobj version 1 on render node 226:128`.
- `vkcube --wsi wayland --c 600` committed every buffer with acquire and release points. In seven runs between 267 and 315 of its commits waited on their acquire point (`blocked syncobj`), with medians between 0.5 and 1.6 ms, and the rest had signalled by the time they were committed; about 600 release points were signalled. The cube changed between captures and the run ended with status 0 every time, and each swapchain image was still imported once.
- Also with acquire and release points: Xwayland 24.1.6's glamor presenting `glxgears` (22 commits, none waited), nested Shingle's wgpu window in offscreen Shingle (75 of 76 ready, 1 waited 0.12 ms), `vkcube` inside nested Shingle (18 ready), Chromium (3 ready) and `mpv --gpu-api=vulkan` (22 of 162 waited, median 1.1 ms). `weston-simple-dmabuf-egl` did not bind the protocol and stayed on implicit sync. Every DB-4 check passed again with the global offered.
- The stall probe (`--checks syncobj`, `syncobj_probe --seconds 10`): one client with two toplevels; `syncobj-stalled` committed two udmabuf DMA-BUFs behind acquire points 1 and 2 of a timeline nobody signalled, while `syncobj-live` redrew red and blue on every frame callback. Captures showed `syncobj-stalled` keeping its last shm pattern and `syncobj-live` in both colours; the probe got 86 frame callbacks for the live toplevel and none for the stalled one, no `release` and no signalled release point while stalled. Destroying `syncobj-stalled` released both DMA-BUFs and signalled both release points within a second, the trace showed 2 `syncobj release` lines and no `blocked syncobj` (the stalled commits never applied), and offscreen Shingle kept running and exited 0 on SIGTERM. A first run failed its capture checks because it captured during the map animations and the 4 s live phase was too short at the debug build's frame rate with two large shm uploads per frame; the check now waits for the settled capture and the live phase lasts 10 s.
- With `SHINGLE_SYNCOBJ=0` (`--checks nosyncobj`: `dmabuf`, `xwayland` and `nested-gpu` again) the log said `SHINGLE_SYNCOBJ=0 forces implicit sync`, `wayland-info` listed no syncobj global, the trace had no syncobj line, and every check passed on implicit sync (31 of 31).
- tools/xwayland_test/run.py passed 199/199 and tools/output_test/run.py 24/24.
- Not verified: NVIDIA's EGL clients (`es2gears_wayland`, `eglgears_wayland`, `kitty`, Firefox, `mpv --gpu-api=opengl`) with syncobj, which the design's acceptance list names for `es2gears_wayland`: in the namespace they still fall back to Mesa on `wl_shm`, as DB-4 found, so they never bind the protocol; and the native backend. Both are step 24 of [native-testing.md](native-testing.md).

Shared-memory uploads (DB-6), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-shm-upload` branch, in the GPU namespace of DB-4. `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj` passed 119 of 119, and its device check passed.

- Captures: `--checks offscreen nested buffers shm --captures DIR`, with captures now taken once two in a row are identical, saved five captures with the branch and five with `master`'s binary at `6405ed2`: the probe's quadrants, the same inverted, nested Shingle on lavapipe with its client, the FIFO probe's last buffer and the `shm` probe's square. All five were byte-identical between the two builds.
- Damage: `wl_probe --damage` committed a 1000×1000 XRGB8888 buffer from a pool sealed against shrinking, fully damaged, and the trace showed `shm upload 4000000 bytes`; after its release the probe drew a red 10×10 square and committed the same buffer with only that damage, and the trace showed `shm upload 400 bytes`; the probe got `release` after each commit, and the capture showed the square red on white. With `master`'s binary the second commit uploaded 4000000 bytes again.
- CPU time: `wl_probe --animate 600` drew 600 full-output 1280×720 XRGB8888 frames from a sealed pool, each a new grey, fully damaged, one per frame callback, against offscreen Shingle with no gaps; the harness read offscreen Shingle's user and system time from `/proc/PID/stat` from the view's mapping to the probe's last frame. Debug builds: `master`, two runs, 7.70 and 7.82 s of CPU (12.8 and 13.0 ms a frame, 15.4 and 15.2 s of wall time); the branch, four runs, 3.06 to 3.27 s (5.1 to 5.5 ms a frame, 13.1 to 13.6 s). Release builds of `shingle` with the same debug probe, three runs each, alternating: `master` 3.39 to 3.68 s (5.7 to 6.1 ms a frame, 14.2 to 14.5 s), the branch 0.80 to 0.83 s (1.3 to 1.4 ms a frame, 10.2 s). The machine's load average was below about 2. Nothing else was measured, such as GPU time or the client's CPU.
- The other groups saw shm clients upload as before: Mesa's llvmpipe clients (`es2gears_wayland`, `eglgears_wayland`, `kitty`, Firefox) and `vkcube` on lavapipe mapped and drew on `wl_shm`, with `shm upload` in the trace, and every DMA-BUF and syncobj check passed.
- Found and fixed: `syncobj_probe` took the `WouldBlock` that wayland-backend returns for a partial read as fatal, so the `syncobj` group failed 6 of 10 with this branch and with `master`'s binary; with the probe fixed it passed with both ([design/dmabuf.md](design/dmabuf.md#db-6-as-built)).
- tools/xwayland_test/run.py passed 199/199 and tools/output_test/run.py 24/24.
- Not verified: the native backend, including the GPU-loss path, after which surfaces whose shm buffers were already uploaded draw nothing until they commit again; and clients whose pools are sealed by their toolkit, beyond the probe.

Native backend (NB-6, NB-7, NB-9): not run with real clients or devices yet. Its steps are in [native-testing.md](native-testing.md), for the test machine.

Bring-up driver, 2026-11-27, the `shingle-bringup-driver` branch: tools/native_test/bringup.py and start_tty1.py have not run against the laptop, which had not arrived, nor against any other machine; only the fake SSH machine of their unit tests ran them. On this host, `bringup.py check localhost` refused with `localhost is this host (loopback)` and status 3 after only `ssh -G localhost`, which connects nowhere, and `bringup.py step 45` printed the step without a host. No shingle, sudo, uinput or chvt ran here.

Output management without a card (NB-9), 2026-11-25: tools/output_test/run.py (debug build of the `shingle-native-outputs` branch) ran headless Shingle, and nested Shingle on lavapipe as a client of a headless one, with `wlr-randr` 0.4.1 inside a user, mount, network and PID namespace with a private `/tmp`, empty `/dev/dri` and `/dev/input`, private runtime, config, state and home directories and the host's `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, `WAYLAND_SOCKET`, `DISPLAY` and `WAYFIRE_SOCKET` removed; every signal went through `signal_checked`. 24/24 checks passed:

- `wlr-randr` listed `HEADLESS-1` as `Shingle Headless output (HEADLESS-1)` with one mode, 1280x720 at 60 Hz, preferred and current, and Shingle held no GPU, input or TTY descriptor.
- `--custom-mode 1600x900@50Hz --dryrun` succeeded and changed nothing; without `--dryrun` the output became 1600x900 and `wlr-randr` showed that mode at 50 Hz as current. `--scale 2 --pos 100,20 --transform 90` gave the logical output 450x800 at 100,20 with the physical 1600x900 kept. `--off` failed with `failed to apply configuration` and changed nothing.
- `output: {mode: 1920x1080@75}` started a 1920x1080 output at 75 Hz; an `outputs` entry for `HEADLESS-1` with `800x600@50` and `x: 10` replaced it. A Wayfire file with `[output:DP-1]` (2560x1440@144, scale 2) before `[output:HEADLESS-1]` (1024x768@60) gave 1024x768 at 60 Hz at scale 1: the sections no longer overwrite each other.
- Nested reported its configured 75 Hz at the size the outer Shingle gave its window, and a reloaded `scale: 2` halved that size; SIGTERM ended every instance with status 0.

The first run found that headless Shingle never reloads its config (so the reload check moved to nested), that a floating window in Shingle is configured to the workarea rather than its own size (so the nested window's opening size could not be observed from outside; it is unit-tested), and, from writing the checks, that a scale change through wlr-output-management kept the logical size instead of the physical one; that is fixed. Native hotplug, EDIDs and mode changes on a card need the test machine.

The presentation probe (crates/shingle-native/examples/present_probe.rs) was checked as a client on 2026-11-25 in a nested Shingle, run from the NB-7 branch inside `bwrap --dev /dev --unshare-pid` with lavapipe and private runtime, config and state directories: it mapped its window, committed 30 frames, got 30 frame callbacks and 30 `presented` feedbacks (sequence 0 and no flags, as nested reports frames drawn without a display clock) and exited, and SIGTERM ended Shingle with status 0. present_check.py read its output and, with no flip log, reported every frame as not matching a flip, as it should. This checks the tools only; NB-7's presentation itself needs the test machine.

SIGTERM exits on a busy disk, 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-sigterm-exit` branch, in tools/xwayland_test/run.py's namespace. The harness failed `idle: SIGTERM exits with status 0` once on `master` and `eager: SIGTERM exits with status 0` with `eager: SIGTERM leaves no lock or socket` once in three DB-6 runs. Eight runs of the `eager` and `idle` groups and three full runs on `master` at `e64cdbc` passed on a quiet disk. Before stopping Shingle the harness read `/proc/PID/task/*/status`: one thread, with SIGINT and SIGTERM blocked (`SigBlk 0000000000004002`), so the signal went to the signalfd. With a second process rewriting a 2 GB file in `target/` on the same disk, three runs of `--only eager --only idle` failed `eager` 3 times and `idle` once, each with status `timeout`: the harness had sent SIGKILL after 10 s, and the eager lock and socket were left behind. Sampling `/proc/PID/wchan` and `/proc/PID/syscall` every 0.5 s after SIGTERM showed Shingle in `fsync` (system call 74, `folio_wait_bit_common`) for the whole time; it was saving the session, and with the timeout out of the way that exit took 10.6 s.

- With the fix, under the same disk load, `eager` and `idle` passed twice, exiting with status 0 after 12.9 to 17.5 s, blocked in `fsync` at 5 s; with the kill deadline lowered to 8 s for one run the lock and socket were gone either way (that exit took 7.9 s, in `xlog_wait_on_iclog`).
- tools/xwayland_test/run.py, unmodified, passed 199/199 five times in a row; in the first run `idle`'s exit took 6.7 s in `fsync` without any extra load, where the old 10 s limit was close. tools/output_test/run.py passed 24/24, and `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj` passed 119 of 119 with its device check.
- Nested, offscreen and native had the same order: they save through `Frontend::shutdown` while the compositor still holds the display. Their fix is covered by the frontend unit tests only; offscreen and nested exits ran in the harnesses above, native was checked by reading.
- Not changed then: the frontend still saved, with the same sync, after every layout change on the compositor's thread, so a busy disk could stall nested, offscreen and native while they ran. Fixed since; see the next paragraph.

Session writes during slow disks, 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-async-session-save` branch. tools/session_test/run.py ran headless Shingle (640x480) and nested Shingle on lavapipe as its client in a user, mount, network and PID namespace with a private `/tmp`, empty `/dev/dri` and `/dev/input` and no host display variables; nested's `XDG_STATE_HOME` was a FUSE mount (tools/session_test/slowfs.py) whose fsync slept 1.5 s. Two `wl_probe` clients mapped in nested; while the first session write was stuck in fsync the harness sent 30 `simple-tile/set-layout` requests, one every 100 ms over 3 s, alternating between tiling both views at a new split and floating the second, ending at 23 to 77 percent.

- With the branch, four runs passed 12/12: the worst IPC reply took 7.6 to 26.7 ms (median 2.5 to 16.4 ms), the 30 changes cost 3 writes, each stuck 1.5 s in fsync, SIGTERM ended nested with status 0 after 2.9 to 3.2 s (one write finishing, then the final one; 1 s after the signal only `session-writer` was waiting, in `request_wait_answer`), and `session.json` held the last layout at 0.23 and 0.77 with no temporary file left.
- The same harness against a build of `master` at `c202043` failed: every reply waited for a write (worst 1546 ms, median 1523 ms), and the 30 changes cost 28 writes.
- In the first run of the harness under development, nested once missed a 10 s SIGTERM deadline after its two probes had been killed first; that run's `set-layout` requests were failing because of the bug below. Four later runs with that order (probes closed or SIGKILLed first, the failing requests, the old 10 s stop) exited in 1.6 to 2.9 s, and the cause was not found.
- Found, not fixed: `simple-tile/set-layout` re-weighting views that are already tiled emits no event the frontend saves on (only `TilingChanged` when a view's tiled state flips), so such a change reaches disk only with the next save or at exit; and setting a layout whose split direction differs from the root's twice in a row fails with `malformed snapshot: non-root splits must have at least two children`, because the views' old nested split is left empty. The harness avoids both.
- tools/xwayland_test/run.py passed 199/199 (the `idle` exit took 4.7 s), tools/output_test/run.py 24/24 and `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj` 119 of 119 with its device check.
- Both bugs above are fixed since (MO-1, next paragraph).

Workspace sets and session version 2 (MO-1), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-wsets-core` branch. `tools/session_test/run.py --checks round-trip --master-binary ...` (tools/session_test/round_trip.py) ran headless Shingle (1280x720) and nested Shingle on lavapipe as its client in the session harness's namespace (user, mount, network and PID namespace, private `/tmp`, empty `/dev/dri` and `/dev/input`, no host display variables, every signal through `signal_checked`). Five `wl_probe` clients mapped in nested; `simple-tile/set-layout` arranged alpha (weight 3), bravo over charlie (1 to 2, weight 2) and delta (1) on the first workspace, `configure-view` floated delta at 300x200+40+30, and echo, floated by the layout, was sent to the third workspace and given 400x300+100+80. 26/26 checks passed:

- Round trip: the session was saved in the background as version 2 with one set, shown on `HEADLESS-1`. After a SIGKILL and a restart, and again after a SIGTERM (status 0) and a restart, the five clients were relaunched from their saved command lines and claimed their windows under the same view ids, and `get-layout` for all three workspaces and the `list-views` id, app id, title, geometry, tiled edges, fullscreen, minimized, sticky, stacking and parent were identical to before: three tiles at 768, 512x240 and 512x480 and the two floating views at their rectangles.
- Version 1: a `shingle` built from `master` at `99916cc` (from `git archive`, in a directory and target of its own) built the same layout and, on SIGTERM, saved a version 1 session. The branch's build restored it with every client relaunched under its view id and every layout and view as `master` had shown them, and on SIGTERM saved it back as version 2 with the same workspace trees.
- Found and fixed: `configure-view` giving a window that already floats a new rectangle sent no event the frontend saves on, so after a SIGKILL echo came back at the rectangle the layout had floated it at; on `master` too, where the same round trip fails both view checks. It now sends `Event::LayoutChanged`, as `set-layout` does when only weights or nesting change (the first bug above), and the nested split left empty by a second cross-direction layout is removed (the second).
- tools/session_test/run.py's slow-disk check, which now also reads version 2 sessions, passed 12/12 (worst reply 19.5 ms, 30 changes in 3 writes, SIGTERM after 3.0 s); tools/xwayland_test/run.py passed 199/199, tools/output_test/run.py 24/24 and `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj` 119 of 119 with its device check.

The colour and focus fixes, 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-colour-and-focus-fixes` branch:

- `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks colours` 4/4 (tools/gpu_test/colour_checks.py), with its device check. Offscreen Shingle on the NVIDIA render node read a wayfire.ini with `background_color = \#336699FF` and `[decoration] active_color = 0.4 0.2 0.6 1.0`: the settled `grim` capture showed (51, 102, 153) in all four corners and, around a floated `wl_probe --decoration server`, a frame of (102, 51, 153). The host's Wayfire 0.9.0, run headless in the same namespace on the render node with wlroots' GLES2 renderer (NVIDIA's EGL) and `plugins = ipc`, showed (51, 102, 153) in its own `grim` capture too. Before the fix Shingle drew that background as (124, 170, 203).
- `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks minimize-focus` 5/5 (tools/gpu_test/focus_checks.py). In offscreen Shingle and in headless Wayfire 0.9.0 alike, three `wl_probe` clients mapped as alpha, bravo and charlie; `wm-actions/set-minimized` on charlie focused bravo while `last-focus-timestamp` kept the order charlie, bravo, alpha; STIPC Alt+Tab once then left bravo focused and charlie minimized. Both gave the same observations.
- The other harnesses still pass: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 212/212, tools/output_test/run.py 112/112 (its default background is now checked as 26), tools/session_test/run.py 13/13 (worst reply 14.4 ms, 30 changes in 3 writes, SIGTERM after 3.0 s) and `--checks round-trip` 17/17, and `tools/gpu_test/run.py` with every group, `offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj outputs nested-outputs nested-smoke wayfire-plugins wayfire-shell vswipe decoration switcher colours minimize-focus`, 214/214 with its device check.
- Not run: the colours on a real monitor, through the native backend's scanout.

The Alt+Tab switcher's view (WG-7), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-switcher-view` branch:

- `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks switcher` 10/10 (tools/gpu_test/switcher_checks.py), with its device check. Offscreen Shingle on the NVIDIA render node read a wayfire.ini with `plugins = switcher simple-tile` and three `wl_probe --pattern` clients tiled side by side, mapped as first, second and third. STIPC `KEY_LEFTALT` held and `KEY_TAB` tapped sent `plugin-activation-state-changed` for `switcher` with `state: true`; the settled `grim` capture showed second's pattern in the middle of the 1280×720 output at 192×324 (its 422×720 tile fitted into 45 %), third to the left and first to the right a third of the output away at 66 % of that, dimmer, turned so that the edge towards the middle was the shorter one, and black corners, and the focus stayed on third. A second tap brought first to the middle; releasing alt focused first, `plugin-activation-state-changed` with `state: false` followed once the thumbnails were back, and the capture showed the three probes in their tiles again. A capture of the arrangement was looked at by eye: it shows Wayfire's switcher.
- The other harnesses still pass: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 212/212, tools/output_test/run.py 112/112, tools/session_test/run.py 13/13 (worst reply 25.3 ms, 30 changes in 3 writes, SIGTERM after 3.0 s) and `--checks round-trip` 17/17, and `tools/gpu_test/run.py` with every group, `offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj outputs nested-outputs nested-smoke wayfire-plugins wayfire-shell vswipe decoration switcher`, 205/205 with its device check.
- Not run: the switcher with a real keyboard in nested on the host session (see [Needs operator: host input](#needs-operator-host-input)).

Server-side decorations (WG-6), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-decoration` branch:

- `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks decoration` 11/11 (tools/gpu_test/decoration_checks.py), with its device check. Offscreen Shingle on the NVIDIA render node read a wayfire.ini with `plugins = decoration simple-tile move place`, `tile_by_default = none` and `[decoration]` `active_color = 1.0 0.0 1.0 1.0`, `inactive_color = 0.0 1.0 1.0 1.0`. A `wl_probe --pattern --decoration server` and a `wl_probe --pattern --decoration client` mapped and were told `server` and `client`; `configure-view` put them at 100,100 and 700,100, 400×300 each. The first's `geometry` was 100,100 400×300 and its `base-geometry` 104,134 392×262, the size it was configured to; the second's two were both 400×300. `grim` showed the first probe's quadrants inside a cyan frame with its title and three grey buttons, and the second's with the background right around it. A STIPC click on the first's title focused it and its frame turned magenta; a drag of its title bar, six pixels and then on by (94, 50), moved it by (94, 50), as the move follows the pointer from where it started; a click on maximize gave it the 1280×720 workarea and a second click its rectangle back; a click on minimize minimized it; and a click on close sent the probe `xdg_toplevel.close`, after which it exited with status 0. SIGTERM ended Shingle with status 0.
- `tools/xwayland_test/run.py --only decoration` 7/7, in the full run below: in headless Shingle with real Xwayland, `xterm`, which sets no `_MOTIF_WM_HINTS`, tiled at 0,0 640×720 with its X window, by `xwininfo`, at 4,34 632×682, its `base-geometry`; an `xw_probe --no-decorations` window filled its tile at 640,0 640×720. Floated at 200,150 500×400 by `configure-view`, xterm's X window was at 204,184 492×362 and the probe's at the full rectangle.
- The other harnesses still pass: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 212/212, tools/output_test/run.py 112/112, tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip` 27/27 (worst reply 14.2 ms, 30 changes in 1 write, SIGTERM after 14.7 s under a host load near 20) and `tools/gpu_test/run.py` with every group, `offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj outputs nested-outputs nested-smoke wayfire-plugins wayfire-shell vswipe decoration`, 195/195.
- What the first runs found, and what changed for it: X windows now have frames, so the XWayland harness's checks of their geometry fail as written. `xwininfo` and ConfigureNotify now match a view's `base-geometry`, a dialog's frame is centred on its parent's, and pointer positions meant for the xterm use its `base-geometry`. Nested Shingle's winit window asks for server-side decorations and now gets a frame from the Shingle hosting it, as from Wayfire; the GPU and output harnesses find nested's surface by `base-geometry`, and the XWayland harness's host ignores the request so that nested covers its output as its Expo check assumes. A first `nested` group run failed only its two SIGTERM checks, under a host load above 17, and passed 15/15 when run again. The WCM-style fixture's `[decoration]` section no longer warns, so the output harness expects one warning fewer. Scale and Expo placed the surface where the whole window should go, leaving the frame outside the slot; they now lay out the frame.
- Not run: frames with a real mouse in nested on the host session (see [Needs operator: host input](#needs-operator-host-input)).

Touchpad workspace swipes (WG-5), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-vswipe` branch:

- `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks vswipe` 10/10 (tools/gpu_test/vswipe_checks.py), with its device check. Offscreen Shingle on the NVIDIA render node read a wayfire.ini with `plugins = vswipe vswitch simple-tile`, a 3x1 grid and `[vswipe]` `gap = 100`, `background = 1.0 0.0 1.0 1.0`; a `wl_probe --pattern` mapped on workspace 1 and another on workspace 2, and the STIPC pointer was put in the middle of the output. Swipes came through `stipc/feed_gesture` in 12.8-pixel updates (0.05 of a workspace at the default speed factor). Five updates and two slow ones sent no `wset-workspace-changed` within 1 s. Twelve updates held without an end: a `grim` capture showed the first probe's green and white right half left of a magenta gap in the middle third of the output and the second probe's red and blue left half right of it, and no event came while the fingers were down. Lifting them sent one `wset-workspace-changed` from `{"x": 0, "y": 0}` to `{"x": 1, "y": 0}`; a long swipe back, after vswipe's 180 ms slide, sent one to `{"x": 0, "y": 0}`, after which `get-focused-output` reported workspace 0 and a capture showed the probe unshifted. A long three-finger swipe switched nothing. Offscreen Shingle exited 0 on SIGTERM.
- Found by the first run, a harness mistake rather than a bug: a swipe sent right after another's end began during vswipe's slide and went to the clients, as in Wayfire, where the plugin is still active then; the check now waits for the slide to end.
- The other harnesses still pass: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 205/205, tools/output_test/run.py 112/112 (with `list-methods` at 87 and the WCM-style fixture's warnings without line 376), tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip` 27/27 (worst reply 15.2 ms, 30 changes in 3 writes, SIGTERM after 3.0 s) and `tools/gpu_test/run.py` with every group, `offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj outputs nested-outputs nested-smoke wayfire-plugins wayfire-shell vswipe`, 184 of 184 with its device check.
- Not run: swipes on a real touchpad, which nested cannot receive (see [Needs operator: host input](#needs-operator-host-input)).

wayfire-shell (WG-4), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-wayfire-shell` branch:

- tools/output_test/run.py 112/112, with WG-4's `wg4_headless_checks` and `wg4_offscreen_checks` (tools/output_test/wayfire_shell_checks.py). A Python client that speaks the Wayland wire protocol itself (tools/output_test/wayland_wire.py), inside the harness's namespace, bound `zwf_shell_manager_v2` of headless Shingle with `--outputs 2` and a `zwf_output_v2` for each output. `wayland-info` listed the global at version 3, and `list-methods` returned 86 methods with `wayfire-shell/toggle_menu`. A top-edge hotspot of `HEADLESS-2` with threshold 10 and timeout 500 ms: after `stipc/move_cursor` to 3 pixels below that edge nothing came for 0.3 s, `enter` came after 0.517 s with `proximity_changed` 3, moving 300 pixels down sent `leave`, and HEADLESS-1's top edge did not enter it. After a click on `HEADLESS-2` made it active, a `wl_probe --fullscreen` mapped there and asked for fullscreen; HEADLESS-2's `zwf_output_v2` got `enter_fullscreen` and HEADLESS-1's nothing, and when the probe was stopped HEADLESS-2's got `leave_fullscreen`. STIPC `feed_key` of `KEY_LEFTMETA` pressed and released sent one `toggle_menu` to the active output (HEADLESS-2) and none to the other; with `KEY_A` tapped in between none came; `wayfire-shell/toggle_menu` with HEADLESS-1's `output_id` replied `ok` and sent HEADLESS-1 `toggle_menu`. Against offscreen Shingle on lavapipe in the same namespace, the client mapped a floating 200x150 toplevel at (540, 285), STIPC pressed `BTN_LEFT` on it, the client sent `zwf_surface_v2.interactive_move`, STIPC moved the pointer 150 right and 90 down in three steps and released the button, and the view was at (690, 375); a STIPC super tap there sent `toggle_menu` through the frontend. Each Shingle exited 0 on SIGTERM. The real wf-panel and wf-shell were not run.
- Found and fixed by that run: the move's button release never reached the compositor, so the client never got it and the seat held `BTN_LEFT` for good, which also stopped every later modifier-alone binding. The same held for a move or resize a client asks for with `xdg_toplevel.move` and for releases Scale and Expo took.
- Found while writing the unit tests and fixed: a focused fullscreen window hid the top layer of every output and was drawn in every output's scene; each output now promotes the fullscreen window focused there.
- The other harnesses still pass: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 205/205, tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip` 27/27 (worst reply 20.1 ms, 30 changes in 3 writes, SIGTERM after 3.0 s; every layout and view identical after the SIGKILL and SIGTERM restarts; without `--master-binary`) and `tools/gpu_test/run.py` with every group, `offscreen nested baseline buffers shm outputs nested-outputs nested-smoke wayfire-plugins wayfire-shell syncobj` 95 of 95 and `dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston nosyncobj` 79 of 79, each with its device check.

Client environment (WG-3), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-client-environment` branch:

- tools/output_test/run.py 96/96, with the new `client_environment_checks`: headless Shingle with `--ipc` in the harness's namespace, started without `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP`, `XCURSOR_*` or `WAYFIRE_CONFIG_FILE` except where stated, ran `env` through `stipc/run`. Under a YAML config with `input.cursor_theme: Adwaita` and `cursor_size: 32`, and Shingle given a host-style `WAYFIRE_CONFIG_FILE`, the child recorded `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=wayfire`, `XCURSOR_THEME=Adwaita`, `XCURSOR_SIZE=32`, no `WAYFIRE_CONFIG_FILE`, Shingle's `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` and IPC socket as `WAYFIRE_SOCKET`, no `DISPLAY` or `WAYLAND_SOCKET` and `_JAVA_AWT_WM_NONREPARENTING=1`; after `wayfire/set-config-options` set `input/cursor_size` to 40, a second child recorded `XCURSOR_SIZE=40`. Under a `wayfire.ini` with `cursor_size = 28` and Shingle started with `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=GNOME:test` and `XCURSOR_THEME=inherited-theme`, the child recorded those two unchanged, `XCURSOR_SIZE=28` and the file's absolute path as `WAYFIRE_CONFIG_FILE`. Both Shingles exited 0 on SIGTERM. Headless Shingle does not reload its file; the reload case is covered by the frontend unit test.
- data/shingle.desktop was not installed anywhere: `desktop-file-validate` reports only `DesktopNames` as an unknown key (Wayfire's file has the same key), and its start from a display manager is only planned in a unit test, since the native backend runs only on the test machine.
- The other harnesses still pass: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 205/205, tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip` 27/27 (worst reply 17.1 ms; that run's slow write sat in the FUSE fsync for its whole duration, so 30 changes made 1 write and nested's SIGTERM exit took 14.1 s; a second `--checks slow` run gave 3 writes, worst reply 26.3 ms and a SIGTERM exit after 3.1 s, as before; every layout and view identical after the SIGKILL and SIGTERM restarts) and `tools/gpu_test/run.py` with every group, `offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj outputs nested-outputs nested-smoke wayfire-plugins wayfire-shell`, 174 of 174 with its device check.

Wayfire defaults and plugin list (WG-2), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-wayfire-defaults` branch:

- `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks wayfire-plugins wayfire-shell` 14 of 14 with its device check (tools/gpu_test/wayfire_checks.py), offscreen Shingle on the render node reading a synthetic `wayfire.ini` each time. With `plugins = vswitch`, two `wl_probe` clients mapped, STIPC `feed_key` of `super+p` reached the focused probe as KEY_P pressed and released, and no `plugin-activation-state-changed` event followed within 3 s; both probes floated and `simple-tile/get-layout` held no view. With `plugins = vswitch scale simple-tile` the same keys produced the event for `scale` with state true, the probe got no key, and both probes tiled. Stand-in `wf-panel` and `wf-background` scripts, written into the namespace's private `/tmp` and put on Shingle's `PATH` there only, each recorded one start with `autostart_wf_shell = true`; with an autostart entry running `wf-panel` each again started once, the panel only by the entry; with `autostart_wf_shell = false` neither started. The real wf-shell, which this host does not have installed, was never run. Every Shingle exited 0 on SIGTERM.
- tools/output_test/run.py 91/91: `wcm_ini_checks` now expects 14 warnings for the WCM-style fixture, since Scale is not in its plugin list and its `pinch in 4` is no longer read; `list-config-options` listed all 332 options of the table, and `get-config-option` of `scale/toggle` returned the fixture's `<super> KEY_P | pinch in 4` with Wayfire's default `<super> KEY_P` although Scale is not loaded.

Windows per output in nested (MO-6), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-nested-outputs` branch. `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks nested-outputs nested-smoke` (tools/gpu_test/nested_output_checks.py) ran `shingle --headless` and nested Shingle on lavapipe as its client in the GPU harness's namespace (user, mount, network and PID namespace, a private `/dev` with only the render node, NVIDIA's compute nodes and udmabuf, the host's `/run/user/1000` covered, no host display variables, every signal through `signal_checked`, and every opened device checked), with `wl_probe` clients, `grim`, `wayland-info` and Python IPC clients, input only from the headless Shingle's STIPC. Nothing ran on the operator's desktop. 30/30 checks passed:

- `--backend nested --outputs 2`: the headless Shingle mapped two toplevels, "Shingle" and "Shingle - HEADLESS-2", tiled 640x720 each, and nested listed `HEADLESS-1` at 0,0 and `HEADLESS-2` at 640,0, each 640x720 like its window. A `wl_probe --pattern` moved to each output with `configure-view` `output_id` showed its four quadrants at its own window's place in `grim` captures of the headless Shingle, so each window presented its own output.
- `stipc/move_cursor` on the headless Shingle to 100,200 in the `HEADLESS-2` window put nested's cursor at 740,200, on `HEADLESS-2`, and to 940,100 in the `HEADLESS-1` window at 300,100; `stipc/feed_button BTN_LEFT` in each made that output active (`get-focused-output`), and a watcher got `output-gain-focus` for `HEADLESS-2`.
- `stipc/create_wayland_output` replied `ok` with `HEADLESS-3` (id 3), and the headless Shingle mapped a third window, whose size became `HEADLESS-3`'s while the others shrank to theirs. Closing the `HEADLESS-2` window with the headless Shingle's `window-rules/close-view` removed the output (a watcher got `output-removed`) and moved its probe to `HEADLESS-1`; the next `stipc/create_wayland_output` opened `HEADLESS-2` again (id 4), and the probe went back to it under `return`. `stipc/destroy_wayland_output` with `HEADLESS-3` closed that window. Destroying `DP-9` gave `Could not find output: "DP-9"`, the headless Shingle answered `stipc/create_wayland_output` with `Wayfire is not running in nested wayland mode!`, and `list-methods` returned 85 methods. Closing both windows through the headless Shingle, the last one last, ended nested with status 0.
- The nested smoke test with one window, in the namespace instead of on the host session: one window titled "Shingle" filled the 1280x720 output, `stipc/get_display` named the socket nested printed, `list-methods` returned 85 sorted methods, `wayland-info` listed 48 globals with one `wl_output`, `wl_compositor`, `wl_shm`, `wl_seat`, `xdg_wm_base` and `zwlr_layer_shell_v1`, and one `wl_probe` mapped, was focused and was configured to its tile, the whole output. Client protocols per output (MO-3), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-output-protocols` branch. `tools/output_test/run.py` ran headless Shingle with `--headless --outputs 2`, `HEADLESS-2` at scale 2, in the output harness's namespace (user, mount, network and PID namespace, private `/tmp`, empty `/dev/dri` and `/dev/input`, no host display variables, every signal through `signal_checked`), with `grim`, `wlr-randr`, `wayland-info`, `wl_probe` and `shingle-lock` on lavapipe. 65/65 checks passed, the 46 earlier ones unchanged and 19 new:

- `grim -o HEADLESS-1` captured 1280x720 and `grim -o HEADLESS-2` 2560x1440, its physical pixels at scale 2. A `wl_probe --layer HEADLESS-2` panel was configured 1280x30 and showed white in the first 60 rows of `HEADLESS-2`'s capture and nowhere on `HEADLESS-1`. `grim -g "1270,0 20x40"` across the boundary gave 40x80 pixels (grim takes the highest scale), the background on the `HEADLESS-1` side and the panel above the background on the `HEADLESS-2` side.
- `wlr-randr --output HEADLESS-2 --off` turned it off: its head stayed listed as disabled, `wayland-info` no longer showed its xdg-output, and the panel on it was sent `closed`, after which the probe exited with status 0. `--on` brought it back at 1280,0 with scale 2, and `--pos 0,720` moved it there.
- `wl_probe --report-outputs` entered `HEADLESS-1` with a preferred scale of 120 (1.0). With `HEADLESS-1` turned off, its window joined `HEADLESS-2` under `return`: the probe left `HEADLESS-1`, entered `HEADLESS-2` and was told buffer scale 2 and preferred scale 240; with `HEADLESS-1` on again it left `HEADLESS-2`, came back to `HEADLESS-1` and was told 1 and 120.
- A panel on `HEADLESS-3`, created over IPC, was configured 640 wide and closed when `wayfire/destroy-headless-output` destroyed the output.
- `shingle-lock --color ff0000` reported the session locked with opaque lock surfaces on 2 outputs, and both outputs' captures were red; typing a password through `stipc/feed_key` unlocked it (against a permit-all `shingle` PAM service bind-mounted over `/etc/pam.d` inside the namespace only), `shingle-lock` exited with status 0 and the captures were no longer red.
- `tools/xwayland_test/run.py --only outputs` with two headless outputs (`HEADLESS-2` 1024x768): `xrandr --listmonitors` listed 1280x720+0+0 and 1024x768+1280+0 and `xdpyinfo` a 2304x768 root; after `wlr-randr --output HEADLESS-2 --pos 0,720`, RandR showed 1024x768+0+720 and the automatic `HEADLESS-1` at 1280x720+1024+720, as wlroots places it, and the root grew to 2304x1488. 7/7.
- The other harnesses still pass: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 204/204 (199 before and the 5 `outputs` checks), tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip` 26/26 (worst reply 16.2 ms, 30 changes in 3 writes, SIGTERM after 2.9 s; every layout and view identical after the SIGKILL and SIGTERM restarts) and `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj` 119 of 119 with its device check.

- Found and fixed while building it: a probe on a panel closed by turning its output off was then told the first output's scale whenever that changed, and turning the first output off and on made the second one the first output and moved every automatic position.

Several outputs (MO-2), 2026-11-26, debug builds of the `shingle-output-registry` branch. `tools/output_test/run.py` ran headless Shingle with `--headless --outputs 2` (and `--outputs 3` for one run) in the output harness's namespace (user, mount, network and PID namespace, private `/tmp`, empty `/dev/dri` and `/dev/input`, no host display variables, every signal through `signal_checked`), with `wlr-randr`, `wayland-info`, `wl_probe` and Python IPC clients. 46/46 checks passed, the 24 earlier ones unchanged and 22 new:

- Automatic layout: `list-methods` returned 81 methods. `wlr-randr` listed `HEADLESS-1` at 0,0 and `HEADLESS-2` at 1280,0, `wayland-info` showed their xdg-outputs as 1280x720+0+0 and 1280x720+1280+0, and IPC gave them ids 1 and 2. `wlr-randr --output HEADLESS-2 --pos 0,720` fixed `HEADLESS-2` there, and the automatic `HEADLESS-1` moved to 1280,720, the right edge and `y` of the fixed output, as wlroots places it.
- `wayfire/create-headless-output` with 640x480 replied `HEADLESS-3` with id 3 at 2560,720; a second IPC connection watching events got `output-added` for it, and `wayland-info` and `wlr-randr` listed it. `wayfire/destroy-headless-output` removed it, with `output-removed` (id 3) to the watcher, and both tools no longer listed it; destroying `HEADLESS-1` was refused with Wayfire's text.
- Fixed layout: with `HEADLESS-1` at 0,0 and `HEADLESS-2` (1024x768) at -1024,200 in the YAML `outputs` entries, `wlr-randr` and `wayland-info` showed exactly those positions, and an output created over IPC was placed automatically at 1280,0, after the fixed ones.
- Session: a `wl_probe` mapped on the active output `HEADLESS-1` of a headless Shingle with three outputs, an IPC watcher got its `view-mapped` (with `output-id` 1), and Shingle saved its session on SIGTERM (status 0). With `HEADLESS-1` and `HEADLESS-3` swapped in the saved sets, a Shingle with two outputs relaunched the probe, whose window waited on `HEADLESS-1` (id 1) under `return`; `wayfire/create-headless-output` then created `HEADLESS-3`, which took the parked set, and `list-views` showed the window on `HEADLESS-3` (id 3); `destroy-headless-output` moved it back to `HEADLESS-1`.
- The other harnesses still pass: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 199/199 (its first run failed only its own check that `list-methods` returns 79 methods, which now says 81), tools/session_test/run.py `--checks slow round-trip` 26/26 (worst reply 12.8 ms, 30 changes in 3 writes, SIGTERM after 3.0 s; every layout and view identical after SIGKILL and SIGTERM restarts) and `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks offscreen nested baseline buffers shm dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston syncobj nosyncobj` 119 of 119 with its device check. The session round trip ran without `--master-binary`: `master` now writes version 2 itself, which the round trip covers.
- Not repeated: the nested IPC fixtures run on the host session (Wayfire compatibility, input devices, the nested smoke test), since nothing in this workspace may connect to the operator's desktop. Their payloads for one output are covered by the Wayland pin transcript, which is unchanged, and by the IPC unit tests; the new forms of `output-layout-changed` and `wset-workspace-changed` are recorded in [ipc.md](ipc.md#events) and checked by `layout_and_workspace_events_carry_wayfires_payloads`. `wset_to_json`'s `name` is now the set's index, where it was the output's name.
- Found and fixed: headless Shingle never drained its events, so IPC watchers of a headless Shingle never got `view-mapped` or any other event built from them (only those queued directly, such as `view-pre-map` and `xwayland-ready`), and the queue grew without bound; headless now drains every turn, which the `view-mapped` check above shows.

#### Screen sharing with real clients

2026-11-27, debug build, all inside tools/gpu_test/run.py's namespace against `shingle --backend offscreen` on the host's NVIDIA GPU, a 1280x720 output and a `wl_probe --pattern --cursor` window (tools/gpu_test/screencast_checks.py, [design/screen-sharing.md](design/screen-sharing.md)). The portal stack is private to the namespace: a D-Bus session bus of its own, /run/dbus covered by a tmpfs so the host's system bus is unreachable, PipeWire 1.4.2, WirePlumber 0.5.8 with its device monitors off, xdg-desktop-portal 1.20.3 and xdg-desktop-portal-wlr 0.7.1 (`chooser_type=none`, `output_name=HEADLESS-1`, `max_fps=30`); GStreamer's `pipewiresrc` is Debian's gstreamer1.0-pipewire 1.4.2, extracted under target/ by tools/gpu_test/fetch_screencast_deps.py since this host does not have it installed.

| Group | Method | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `screencast` | tools/gpu_test/portal_client.py: `CreateSession`, `SelectSources` (one monitor, cursor mode hidden or embedded), `Start` and `OpenPipeWireRemote` on org.freedesktop.portal.ScreenCast, the stream read with `pipewiresrc` | Pass, 16/16. Portal version 5 offers monitors and cursor modes 3; the stream is 1280x720 and shows the probe's four quadrants at its view geometry and the background in the gaps; the hidden cursor mode leaves the yellow cursor out and the embedded one shows it; 0 frames in 2 s while nothing changes and 54 in 2 s while a second probe animates, under the `max_fps=30` cap; a stream asking for one of the modifiers Shingle draws into negotiated DMA-BUFs (xdg-desktop-portal-wlr's `buffer_type: 1`) and got 11 DMA-BUF frames, which NVIDIA's tiled layout keeps from being mapped on the CPU; no errors in xdg-desktop-portal-wlr's log. |
| `image-copy-capture` | A raw-protocol client of ext-image-copy-capture (tools/gpu_test/capture_wire.py), and `grim` 1.4 | Pass, 13/13. An output session announces 2 shm formats, the render node, 6 modifiers each of XRGB8888 and ARGB8888 and 1280x720; its first frame is ready at once with `transform`, `damage` and `presentation_time` and shows the quadrants; the next waits until the pointer moves; the cursor is in a frame only with `paint_cursors`; a cursor session reports `enter`, the hotspot's position and hotspot (8, 8) and copies the 16x16 yellow image; a toplevel session's buffer is the window's 1170x650 and shows its quadrants to the edges, without background or cursor, and stops when the window closes; a frame into a GBM DMA-BUF (modifier `0x0300000000606014`) is ready, and that buffer, attached to a second window, shows the captured quadrants in `grim`'s capture; `grim` leaves the cursor out and `grim -c` draws it. |
| `ext-data-control` | The raw client's ext-data-control device and sources, with `wl-copy` and `wl-paste` 2.2.1 (wlr-data-control) | Pass, 8/8. A new device hears at once that both selections are empty; `wl-copy`'s text is offered with its 5 types and reads back; `wl-paste` and `wl-paste --primary` read the raw client's sources, and setting the clipboard ends `wl-copy`. |

Found and fixed by these runs: xdg-desktop-portal-wlr 0.7.1 failed every Start with "unable to receive a valid format from wlr_screencopy", since a version 3 frame without `linux_dmabuf` fails there, so the portal had never been able to share Shingle's screen; screencopy answered `copy_with_damage` at once, so a stream was redrawn as fast as it asked, and drew the cursor whatever `overlay_cursor` said; a stream of an output started with the other cursor mode while one ran waited for its first frame, since both are one client to Shingle; an ext-foreign-toplevel-list bound after a window mapped was never told about it. Not covered: xdg-desktop-portal-wlr 0.8, grim 1.5 and OBS, which are not installed here (the raw client stands in for their ext-image-copy-capture), the metadata cursor mode, which xdg-desktop-portal-wlr 0.7.1 does not offer, and the pixels of the portal's DMA-BUF stream itself, which only a GPU consumer could read.

The other harnesses on the same build, 2026-11-27: tools/xwayland_test/run.py 239/239, tools/output_test/run.py 121/121, tools/session_test/run.py 13/13 and `--checks round-trip` 17/17, every group of tools/gpu_test/run.py 331/332 and, after its expectation was changed to the host's ext-data-control (nested now prefers it from a headless host that offers it), `nested-clipboard` 36/36, and a 12-minute nested soak (tools/soak_test/run.py `--mode nested --minutes 12`, 46 cycles, nothing flagged; RSS settled at 315 MiB after the warm-up, 74 fds, median frame latency 19.2 ms).
