#### Frame pacing of the offscreen outputs

2026-11-27, debug builds, `tools/gpu_test/run.py --checks outputs` at niceness 19 with the 1-minute load between 1 and 6. The check "HEADLESS-1 presents 60 frames a second over 5 s, within 2 %" (tools/gpu_test/output_checks.py) failed now and then at low load with 286 to 293 frames.

- Measured: the `presented` events of both `wl_probe --animate --presentation` clients were kept per run, and a temporary field in the probe recorded how long after its presentation time each one arrived. On master 1ad85ad the events reached the 60 Hz probe a median 9.7 ms and up to 23 ms after the frame was presented, half of them more than 5 ms late. Every frame the backend drew still reached the probe (no gaps in `seq`), but their spacing ran from 4 to 51 ms in a pattern repeating every 100 ms, and spells of a missed refresh interval cost the frames. HEADLESS-1 counted 289 in the first run and 294 to 300 in 20 more (two at the 294 limit, mean 298).
- Cause: `Compositor::poll` flushed clients only at the end of a turn, after the event loop's wait. The offscreen backend draws after the frontend's turn and then polls, so the frame callbacks and feedback that `frame_presented` queued waited out the poll's timeout (the backend's 16 ms tick) or the next unrelated wake. The probe draws on frame callbacks and the backend draws when a commit asks, so the probe's commit came late; the pacer, which keeps a grid and draws a late frame at once, was then drawn late frame after frame until a grid slot passed empty. The pacer's arithmetic and the check's window were not at fault: the window counts 300 ± 1 of evenly spaced frames, and the probe redrew on every callback.
- Second cause, smaller: when both outputs are due in one turn the backend draws them one after the other, waiting for the GPU each time, so HEADLESS-1's events also waited for HEADLESS-2's draw: a median 3.2 ms for the frames that coincided with a 30 Hz frame, against 0.44 ms for the others.
- Fix: `poll` flushes what was sent since the last turn before it waits, and `frame_presented` flushes its events at once. Afterwards the events arrive a median 0.5 ms and at most 3 ms after presentation, frames are 10 to 23 ms apart, and HEADLESS-1 counted 300 and HEADLESS-2 149 or 150 in 20 consecutive runs, all passing.
- Native and nested already flushed in time, since they report presentations before the frontend's turn; headless (`--headless`) also queued its frame callbacks until the end of its next wait, which cost latency but no frames, and now sends them at once too.

The other harnesses still pass after the fix (2026-11-27, debug `shingle`, one after another at niceness 19): tools/gpu_test/run.py 259 of 259 for `offscreen nested baseline buffers shm syncobj outputs nested-outputs nested-smoke nested-scaled wayfire-plugins wayfire-shell vswipe decoration switcher colours minimize-focus drag-icon resize nested-clipboard screencast image-copy-capture ext-data-control launcher`, 91 of 91 for `dmabuf browsers xwayland nested-gpu nodmabuf weston nosyncobj resize-nested` and 240 of 240 for `apps`; tools/xwayland_test/run.py 239 of 239; tools/output_test/run.py 118 of 118, as many as master 1ad85ad runs there now. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all`, the long-line check and `RUSTDOCFLAGS='-D warnings' cargo doc --workspace --no-deps` are clean.

#### Needs operator: host input

These checks need physical keyboard, pointer and touch input on the host, which an agent must not inject. Run them from a terminal on the host session, in the repository, after `cargo build --bin shingle`; `wev` is in Debian's `wev` package. Each step lists what to look for in `wev`'s output, which appears in the terminal that started Shingle.

1. Start an isolated instance with one `wev` window. The config moves the bindings used below to `ctrl+alt`, because Wayfire's defaults grab `super`+drag and `alt+escape` before they reach the nested window; pick other keys if the host still grabs these.

   ```sh
   export T=$(mktemp -d); mkdir -p $T/run $T/cfg $T/state
   chmod 700 $T/run
   cat > $T/test.yaml <<'EOF'
   pointer: {move_button: ctrl+alt+btn_left}
   keybindings:
     fast_switcher_next: ctrl+alt+j
     expo_toggle: ctrl+alt+e
     quit: ctrl+alt+shift+q
   EOF
   env XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=$T/run XDG_CONFIG_HOME=$T/cfg \
     XDG_STATE_HOME=$T/state \
     WAYLAND_DISPLAY=$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/$WAYLAND_DISPLAY \
     target/debug/shingle --config $T/test.yaml \
     --ipc $T/run/ipc.sock --spawn wev
   ```

2. In a second host terminal, define an IPC helper, open a second `wev` window and register a test binding on `F8`:

   ```sh
   export T=<the same directory>
   ipc() {
     python3 - "$T/run/ipc.sock" "$1" "$2" <<'PY'
   import json, socket, struct, sys
   s = socket.socket(socket.AF_UNIX)
   s.connect(sys.argv[1])
   body = json.dumps({'method': sys.argv[2],
                      'data': json.loads(sys.argv[3])}).encode()
   s.sendall(struct.pack('=I', len(body)) + body)
   n = struct.unpack('=I', s.recv(4))[0]
   print(s.recv(n).decode())
   PY
   }
   ipc stipc/run '{"cmd": "wev"}'
   ipc input/list-devices '{}'
   ipc command/register-binding \
     "{\"binding\": \"KEY_F8\", \"command\": \"touch $T/f8\"}"
   ```

3. Keyboard (ID 1). Run `sleep 4; ipc input/configure-device '{"id": 1, "enabled": false}'`, click into a `wev` window and hold `a` until the command returns. Expected: a `wl_keyboard` key event with state released at that moment, no repeats after it, and no further key or modifier events while typing. Press `F8` in the nested window: `$T/f8` must not appear. Re-enable with `'{"id": 1, "enabled": true}'` while holding `shift`: `wev` shows a modifiers event with shift depressed, and keys arrive again; `F8` now creates `$T/f8`.
4. Pointer (ID 2). Run `sleep 4; ipc input/configure-device '{"id": 2, "enabled": false}'` and hold the left button over a `wev` window until it returns. Expected: a `wl_pointer` button release at that moment; afterwards no motion, button or axis events, and `ipc window-rules/get-cursor-position '{}'` stays the same while the mouse moves. Re-enable: motion and clicks arrive again.
5. Drag clean-up. With the pointer enabled, run `sleep 4; ipc input/configure-device '{"id": 2, "enabled": false}'`, then start a `ctrl+alt`+left-button drag of one `wev` window onto the other and keep moving. Expected: when the pointer is disabled the drag ends with the windows as they were, without a swap, drop preview or snap; `ipc simple-tile/get-layout '{"wset-index": 1, "workspace": {"x": 0, "y": 0}}'` shows the original order. Re-enable the pointer afterwards.
6. Switcher. Run `sleep 4; ipc input/configure-device '{"id": 1, "enabled": false}'`, then in the nested window hold `ctrl+alt` and press `j` to open the fast switcher, and keep `ctrl+alt` held. Expected: when the keyboard is disabled the switcher closes as if `ctrl+alt` were released, with the selected window focused, and does not reopen or stay stuck. Re-enable the keyboard.
7. Touch (ID 3), only with a touchscreen. Run `sleep 4; ipc input/configure-device '{"id": 3, "enabled": false}'` and keep a finger on a `wev` window. Expected: `wl_touch` cancel, not up, at that moment, and no touch events afterwards. Re-enable: touches arrive again.
8. Clean quit and restore. Note `ipc simple-tile/get-layout '{"wset-index": 1, "workspace": {"x": 0, "y": 0}}'`, then press `ctrl+alt+shift+q` in the nested window. Expected: `shingle` exits and `$T/state/shingle/session.json` exists. Start it again with step 1's command but without `--spawn wev`: both `wev` windows come back in the same tile order and sizes and the same `get-layout` output.
9. Keyboard focus (NB-3). Hold `ctrl+alt` on the host keyboard, click into a `wev` window inside the nested window, and press `e` while still holding them. Expected: Expo opens, because modifiers held on focus gain are taken over. Close it with `escape`. Then hold `ctrl+alt` in the nested window, click a host window outside it, release them there, come back into the nested window and press `e`: `wev` receives the `e` key with no modifiers held and Expo does not open.
10. Key repeat (NB-4). Press `ctrl+alt+e` to open Expo and hold `right` (or `l`). Expected: the selection moves once, then after 400 ms about 40 times a second (Shingle's defaults), whatever the host's own repeat settings are. Close Expo with `escape`.
11. Scaled host (the 2026-11-26 scale fix). Move the nested window to a monitor whose Wayfire scale is above 1, or start it there. Run `ipc window-rules/list-outputs` and expect `HEADLESS-1` with that scale and a geometry of the window's size in the host's logical pixels, not its physical pixels. Move the host pointer over the nested window: Shingle's own cursor, where a client sets none, must sit under the host cursor everywhere, also near the far corner. With two `wev` windows tiled, start a `ctrl+alt`+left-button drag on the left one and drop it on the right half of the other: the two must swap. Move the window to a monitor of another scale and repeat `list-outputs`: the scale and size follow.
12. Resize redraw (the 2026-11-26 configure fix). Start another instance as in step 1, with `resize_button: ctrl+alt+btn_right` added to its `pointer` line and `--spawn kitty` instead of `--spawn wev`, and run `wev &` in that kitty so that the two tile. Drag the boundary between them with `ctrl+alt` and the right button, slowly and then fast. Expected: kitty redraws at new sizes while the drag runs, and at its final size at once after the release.
13. Host clipboard (the 2026-11-26 bridge). On the host desktop run `printf host-text | wl-copy`, then in the nested window's `foot` (start one with `--spawn foot`) run `wl-paste`: expected `host-text`, and the instance's log shows `sharing [Clipboard, Primary] with the host through ext_data_control_manager_v1` (Wayfire 0.10 offers it; the harness only ran wlr-data-control). Copy text in a nested application (or `printf nested-text | wl-copy` in that `foot`) and paste on the host with `wl-paste` and with Ctrl+V into a host application: expected `nested-text`. Select text in each direction and middle-click paste for the primary selection. Copy a large file (`wl-copy < /usr/bin/bash` on the host, `wl-paste > $T/bash; cmp $T/bash /usr/bin/bash` inside) and check that neither desktop stutters meanwhile.
14. Stop the instance with `pkill -TERM -f "ipc $T/run/ipc.sock"` (it now exits cleanly on SIGTERM) and remove `$T`.

Record each result here, replacing this list.

Touchpad workspace swipes (WG-5) cannot be checked from here: nested Shingle gets no touchpad swipes from the host, because winit reports none on Wayland. They are step 43 of [native-testing.md](native-testing.md#step-43-four-finger-swipes-move-the-workspace-wall), on the test machine's touchpad; until then they are checked through `stipc/feed_gesture` only.

The Alt+Tab switcher's view (WG-7) was driven only through STIPC keys. With a real keyboard, start the instance of step 1 with `--spawn foot` three times over (or open three foot windows), hold Alt and tap Tab a few times, with and without Shift, then release Alt. Expected: what the GPU harness's group `switcher` checks, with the thumbnails sliding between the slots over half a second and back after the release, and the window in the middle focused.

Server-side frames (WG-6) were driven only through STIPC. With a real mouse, start the instance of step 1 with `behavior: {preferred_decoration_mode: server}` added to `$T/test.yaml` and `--spawn foot` (foot asks for server-side decorations when offered), float the window with `ipc window-rules/configure-view '{"id": 1, "geometry": {"x": 100, "y": 100, "width": 500, "height": 400}}'`, then hover each button, click minimize, maximize (twice) and close, drag the title bar, double-click it, and drag each border and corner. Expected: what the GPU harness's group `decoration` checks, buttons taking their colour under the pointer, and, where Shingle draws the cursor, the resize cursor over a border.

#### Needs operator: two nested windows (MO-6)

The harness checks nested Shingle's windows only as a client of a headless Shingle, with input fed through STIPC. These steps check them on the host session with real input; run them from a terminal on the host session, in the repository, after `cargo build --bin shingle`.

1. Start an isolated instance with two windows and a `wev` client:

   ```sh
   export T=$(mktemp -d); mkdir -p $T/run $T/cfg $T/state $T/home
   chmod 700 $T/run
   printf 'xwayland: {mode: false}\nsession: {restore: false}\n' \
     > $T/test.yaml
   env XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=$T/run XDG_CONFIG_HOME=$T/cfg \
     XDG_STATE_HOME=$T/state HOME=$T/home \
     WAYLAND_DISPLAY=$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/$WAYLAND_DISPLAY \
     target/debug/shingle --backend nested --outputs 2 \
     --config $T/test.yaml --ipc $T/run/ipc.sock --spawn wev
   ```

   Expected: two host windows, titled "Shingle" and "Shingle - HEADLESS-2", with `wev` in the first.
2. With the `ipc` helper of the host-input steps above (step 2, same `$T`), run `ipc stipc/run '{"cmd": "wev"}'` and then `ipc window-rules/configure-view '{"id": 2, "output_id": 2}'`. Expected: the second `wev` is shown in the second window.
3. Move the host pointer through both windows and click in each. Expected: each `wev` reports pointer motion only while the pointer is over its window; `ipc window-rules/get-cursor-position '{}'` reports an `x` of at least `HEADLESS-2`'s `x` (`ipc window-rules/list-outputs '{}'`) while the pointer is in the second window, and after a click there `ipc window-rules/get-focused-output '{}'` names `HEADLESS-2`.
4. Type into each `wev` after clicking it, and switch between the two windows with the host's focus keys while holding a key. Expected: keys reach the `wev` that was clicked, and no key stays held in the window that lost focus.
5. Resize the second host window. Expected: `ipc window-rules/list-outputs '{}'` gives `HEADLESS-2` the window's new size and its `wev` is configured to it.
6. Run `ipc stipc/create_wayland_output '{}'`, close the second window with its close button, and run `ipc stipc/create_wayland_output '{}'` again. Expected: a third window opens for `HEADLESS-3`; when the second window closes its `wev` moves to another window; the next call opens a window for `HEADLESS-2`, and the `wev` returns to it.
7. Close every window. Expected: closing the last one ends `shingle` with status 0. Remove `$T`.

When recording new verification, keep real-client results separate from compiled or unit-tested ones.
